Arithmetic of critical p p -adic L L -functions Thanks: D.B. was partially supported by the Agence National de Recherche (grant ANR-18-CE40-0029) in the framework of the ANR-FNR project “Galois representations, automorphic forms and their L L -functions”. Thanks: K.B.’s research conducted in this publication was funded by the Irish Research Council under grant number IRCLA/2023/849.
Denis Benois, Kâzım Büyükboduk

Abstract
. Our objective in the present work is to develop a fairly complete arithmetic theory of critical p p -adic L L -functions on the eigencurve. To this end, we carry out the following tasks: • We give an “étale” construction of Bellaïche’s p p -adic L L -functions at a θ \theta -critical point on the cuspidal Coleman–Mazur–Buzzard eigencurve. • We introduce the algebraic counterparts of these objects (which arise as appropriately defined Selmer complexes) and develop Iwasawa theory in this context, including a definition of an Iwasawa theoretic ℒ \mathscr{L} -invariant ℒ Iw cr \mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\mathrm{Iw}} . • We formulate the (punctual) critical main conjectures, and study its relationship with its slope-zero counterpart. Along the way, we also develop descent theory (paralleling Perrin-Riou’s work). • We introduce what we call thick (Iwasawa theoretic) fundamental line and the thick Selmer complex to counter Bellaïche’s secondary p p -adic L L -functions. This allows us to formulate an infinitesimal thickening of the Iwasawa main conjecture, and we observe that it implies both slope-zero and punctual critical main conjectures, but it seems stronger than both. • We establish an 𝒪 𝒳 \mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}} -adic leading term formula for the two-variable p p -adic L L -function over the affinoid neighborhood 𝒳 = Spm ⁡ ( 𝒪 𝒳 ) \mathcal{X}={\rm Spm}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}) in the eigencurve about a θ \theta -critical point. Using this formula we prove, when the Hecke L L -function of f f vanishes to order one at the central critical point, that the derivative of the secondary p p -adic L L -function can be computed in terms of the second order derivative of an 𝒪 𝒳 \mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}} -adic regulator (rather than a regulator itself).



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
11F11, 11F67 (primary); 11R23 (secondary)

Preface

Our objective in the present work is to develop a fairly complete arithmetic theory of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions on the eigencurve.

As the first task, we give an “étale” construction of Bellaïche’s
p
-adic
L
-functions at a
\theta
-critical point on the cuspidal Coleman–Mazur–Buzzard eigencurve (corresponding to a critical
p
-stabilization of cuspidal newform
f
). The interpolation properties of our improved
p
-adic
L
-function are deduced from a novel “eigenspace-transition via differentiation” principle.

As the second task, we introduce the algebraic counterparts of these objects (which arise as appropriately defined Selmer complexes) and develop Iwasawa theory in this context. These allow us to interpret the degeneracy properties of the critical
p
-adic
L
-functions as an extreme example of the exceptional zero phenomenon, and lead us naturally to the definition of an Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\mathrm{Iw}}
, whose construction and properties should be of independent interest. We prove that the cyclotomic improvement of the critical
p
-adic
L
-function coincides with the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function, up to multiplication by
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\mathrm{Iw}}
. In particular, the critical
p
-adic
L
-function is nonzero if and only if the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant is. It seems to us that there are no results paralleling these in the Betti counterpart of the theory.

Armed with these constructions, we then formulate the punctual critical main conjecture and show that it is equivalent to its slope-zero counterpart, subject to the non-vanishing of our Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant. We establish an Iwasawa theoretic descent formalism (paralleling Perrin-Riou’s theory in the non-critical scenarios), which in turn allow us to establish leading term formulae (within and off the critical range) for the module of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions, involving classical invariants.

To complete the picture, one needs a similar theory for Bellaïche’s secondary
p
-adic
L
-function, since its interpolation properties suggest that it also carries arithmetic information. Our key insight in the treatment of Bellaïche’s secondary
p
-adic
L
-function is that its arithmetic properties should be encoded in what we call
thick
(Iwasawa theoretic) fundamental line and the
thick
Selmer complex, which come attached to the infinitesimal deformation of Deligne’s representation along the eigencurve. Based on this insight, we formulate an infinitesimal thickening of the Iwasawa main conjecture, and we observe that it implies both slope-zero and punctual critical main conjectures, but it seems stronger than both. We also develop a descent formalism for the
thick
Iwasawa theoretic Selmer complexes away from the central critical point. The descent at the central critical point when the Hecke
L
-function of
f
vanishes escapes our methods, for the underlying thick Selmer complex is not semi-simple in these scenarios.

The discussion concerning the semi-simplicity of the thick Selmer complex raises the question of whether one should expect to interpret the derivative of the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function at the central critical point in terms of
p
-adic height pairing when the relevant
L
-value vanishes. To that end, we establish an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic leading term formula for the two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function over the affinoid neighborhood
\mathcal{X}={\rm Spm}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
in the eigencurve about a
\theta
-critical point. Using this formula we prove, when the Hecke
L
-function of
f
vanishes to order one at the central critical point, that the derivative of the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function can be computed in terms of the second order derivative of an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic regulator (rather than a regulator itself). The
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic leading term formula also shows that the derivative of the (punctual) critical
p
-adic
L
-function at the central critical point can be computed in terms of heights on the thick Selmer complexes. It would be interesting to understand the meaning of this formula in terms of the more standard leading term formula involving heights associated to the slope-zero root of the Hecke polynomial of
f
.

Introduction

0.1.
Basic notation

0.1.1.

We fix forever an embedding

\iota_{\infty}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathbb{C}.
For any prime number
p,
we fix an isomorphism

\iota_{\infty,p}:\mathbb{C}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}_{p}
and put

\iota_{p}:=\iota_{\infty,p}\circ\iota_{\infty}\,:\,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathbb{C}_{p}.
Using the embeddings
\iota_{\infty}
and
\iota_{p}
, we consider
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
as a subset of
\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbb{C}_{p}.

0.1.2.

For any prime
p
, we write
v_{p}\,:\,\mathbb{C}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}\cup\{+\infty\}
for the
p
-adic valuation on

\mathbb{C}_{p}
normalized by
v_{p}(p)=1.

If
E
is an extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we denote by
\mathcal{O}_{E}
its ring of integers
and by
\mathscr{H}_{E}
the algebra
of formal power series
with coefficients in
E
which converge on the
p
-adic open unit disc.
We will often omit
E
from the notation and write, for example,
\mathscr{H}
in place of
\mathscr{H}_{E}
.

0.1.3.

Let
\Gamma
denote the Galois group of
\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}.
Then
\Gamma\simeq\Gamma_{1}\times\Delta,
where
\Gamma_{1}=\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p}))
and
\Delta=\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})/\mathbb{Q})
. For any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[\Gamma]]
-module
M
, we denote as usual by
M^{\Gamma}
its
\Gamma
-invariants, and by
M_{\Gamma}:=M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[\Gamma]]}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
its
\Gamma
-coinvariants, where
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is endowed with the trivial
\Gamma
-action.

Fix a topological generator
\gamma_{1}\in\Gamma_{1}
and define

\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1}):=\{h(\gamma_{1}-1)\mid h\in\mathscr{H}\}
and

\mathscr{H}(\Gamma):=\mathbb{Q}_{p}[\Delta]\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1}).

The algebra
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
is equipped with the canonical
involution
\iota
such that
\iota(\tau)=\tau^{-1}

for all
\tau\in\Gamma
and the twist maps

\mathrm{Tw}_{j}\,:\,\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\rightarrow\mathscr{H}(\Gamma),\qquad\mathrm{Tw}_{j}(f(\gamma-1))=f(\chi^{-j}(\gamma)\gamma-1).

Note that
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
is the large Iwasawa algebra introduced
in
[
PR94
]
. It contains the usual Iwasawa algebra

\Lambda:=\mathcal{O}_{E}[\Delta]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]].

For any affinoid algebra
\mathcal{X}
over
E,
set
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)=\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\widehat{\otimes}_{E}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}.
Let
X(\Gamma)
denote the group of finite multiplicative characters of
\Gamma
with values in
E^{*}.

0.1.4.

For any commutative ring
R,
we denote by
\mathcal{P}(R)
the category of finitely generated projective
R
-modules of rank one. These modules are exactly those that are invertible for the tensor product, and therefore
\mathcal{P}(R)
has the structure of a Picard category. A complex
C^{\bullet}=(C^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
R
-modules is said to be
perfect
if there exists a bounded complex
P^{\bullet}=(P^{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
of finitely generated projective
R
-modules which is quasi-isomorphic to
C^{\bullet}
. We denote by
\mathcal{D}^{p}(R)

the derived category of perfect complexes of
R
-modules. Recall that the determinant functor of Knudsen–Mumford

{\det}_{R}\,:\,\mathcal{D}^{p}(R)\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}(R)

is defined as follows. For any projective
R
-module
P
of finite rank
r,
we set
{\det}_{R}(P)=\wedge^{r}P
. The module
{\det}_{R}(P)
is invertible and we denote by

{\det}^{-1}_{R}(P)
its
R
-dual. For any perfect complex
C^{\bullet}
, we fix
a quasi-isomorphic bounded complex
P^{\bullet}
and put
{\det}_{R}(C^{\bullet})=\underset{i\in\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}{\det}_{R}^{(-1)^{i}}(P^{i}).
This functor is unique up to isomorphism
of functors. We refer the reader to
[
KM76
]
for further details.

0.2.
p
-adic
L
-functions of modular forms

0.2.1.

Let
f=\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}a_{n}q^{n}\in S_{k}(N,\varepsilon_{f})
be a normalized newform (for all Hecke operators
\{T_{\ell}\,:\,\ell\nmid N\}
and
\{U_{\ell},\langle\ell\rangle\,:\,\ell\mid N\}
) of weight
k\geq 2
and level
N.

Fix a prime number
p\geqslant 3

such that
(p,N)=1
, and denote by
\alpha
and
\beta
the roots of the
polynomial
X^{2}+a_{p}X+\varepsilon_{f}(p)p^{k-1}.

Let

f_{\alpha},f_{\beta}\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{p},\varepsilon_{f}),\qquad\Gamma_{p}=\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)

denote the stabilizations (or refinements) of
f
attached to these roots.
We will always assume that
\alpha\neq\beta

1
1

1

This property is conjectured to hold always, and it is proved only for modular forms of weight two. It holds automatically in the critical-slope scenario, which is the main subject of the present paper.
.

0.2.2.

We first review the case
v_{p}\left(\alpha\right)<k-1
. The constructions that we recall below depend on the choice of a rational basis
\xi:=\{\xi^{+},\xi^{-}\}
of the Betti realization of the motive
\mathcal{M}(f)
associated to
f
(cf. §
2.3.1
).
Using the theory of modular symbols, Amice–Vélu in
[
AV75
]
and Višik in
[
Viš76
]
have given a construction of
p
-adic distributions

2
2

2

The subscripts “S” in the notation for distributions and
p
-adic
L
-functions stand for “Stevens”.

\mu^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\xi}
of growth order
\leqslant v_{p}(\alpha)
, which are characterized by the property that they interpolate the critical values of Hecke
L
-functions
L(f,\rho,s)
attached to the twists of
f
.
The Mellin transforms of
\mu^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\xi}
give rise to an element

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)

(where we recall that
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)=\mathscr{H}_{E}(\Gamma)
, with
E
being a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
that contains the Hecke field of
f_{\alpha}
), which we call the
p
-adic
L
-function attached to the

p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}.
The interpolation property of
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)

reads

(0.1)

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})=(j-1)!\cdot e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)\cdot\frac{L_{\infty}(f,\rho^{-1},j)}{(2\pi i)^{j+1-k}\cdot\Omega_{f}^{\pm}},\qquad 1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1,

where
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
and

e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{p^{j-1}}{\alpha}\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{\beta}{p^{j}}\right)&\textrm{if $\rho=\mathds{1}$},\\
\displaystyle\frac{p^{nj}}{\alpha^{n}\tau(\rho^{-1})}&\textrm{if $\rho$ has conductor $p^{n}.$}\end{cases}

Here
\Omega_{f}^{\pm}
denote the periods attached to

\xi^{\pm}
, and
\tau(\rho^{-1})
is the Gauss sum.

0.2.3.

Assume now that
v_{p}(\alpha)=k-1
, in which case we say that
f_{\alpha}
has critical slope. This case can be divided
in two subcases:

\bullet
Non-
\theta
-critical case. The
p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}
is not in the image of the
p
-adic
\theta
-operator

\theta^{k-1}:=\left(\frac{d}{dq}\right)^{k-1}.

\bullet

\theta
-critical case. The
p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}
belongs to the image of
\theta^{k-1}.

The non-
\theta
-critical case was established by Pollack–Stevens in
[
PS13
]
. It is worthwhile to note that the
p
-adic
L
-functions of Pollack–Stevens are characterized via the properties of the
f_{\alpha}
-isotypic Hecke-eigenspace of the space of modular symbols and
satisfy the classical interpolation property (
0.1
).

The
\theta
-critical case, which is the main subject of this paper, is rather different and will be reviewed in §
0.3
.

0.2.4.

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\rm cusp}
denote the cuspidal Coleman–Mazur–Buzzard eigencurve and let
w:\mathcal{C}^{\rm cusp}\to\mathcal{WS}
denote the weight map.
We denote by
x_{0}
the point on the eigencurve that corresponds to the

p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}
of
f
. As a result of Coleman’s control theorem
[
Col96
, Theorem 6.1]
, one can deduce that the eigencurve is étale at
x_{0}
over the weight space if

f_{\alpha}
does not have critical slope.
Moreover, the refinement
[
PS13
, Theorem 8.1]
of Coleman’s control theorem shows that the eigencurve is étale over the weight space at all classical non-
\theta
-critical points; see
[
Bel12
, Lemma 2.8]
.

When
f_{\alpha}
is non-
\theta
-critical, a two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)
was constructed by Stevens (unpublished, see also
[
Pan03
]
). Here
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\rm cusp}
is a sufficiently small affinoid neighborhood of the point
x_{0}
,
and the notation
\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}
corresponds to the choice of some overconvergent modular symbol on
\mathcal{X}.
The specialization of

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
at any classical point in
\mathcal{X}
coincides, up to a nonzero constant, with the
p
-adic
L
-function of Amice–Vélu and Višik
of the modular form
f_{x}
attached to
x
, and this property characterizes
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
.

0.2.5.

The algebraic counterparts of
p
-adic
L
-functions are the modules of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions constructed
by Perrin-Riou in
[
PR95
]
. Namely, let
V_{f}
denote the
two-dimensional Galois representation with coefficients in
E
attached to

f
(cf.
[
Del69
]
). Consider the
k
th cyclotomic twist
V_{f}(k)
of
V_{f}.
The restriction of

V_{f}(k)
on the decomposition group at
p
is crystalline, and the eigenvalues
of the Frobenius operator
\varphi
acting on the crystalline Dieudonné module
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}(k))
are
\{\alpha/p^{k},\beta/p^{k}\}
. Let

D^{(\alpha)}[k]
and
D^{(\beta)}[k]
denote the corresponding
\varphi
-eigenspaces. Using these data, one can define an Iwasawa theoretic Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{f}(k),\alpha\right)
together with a canonical trivialization

i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(k)}^{(\alpha)}\,:\,{\det}^{-1}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{f}(k),\alpha\right)\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).

Let
T_{f}(k)
be a Galois stable lattice in
V_{f}(k)
and
N_{\alpha}[k]
an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice in
D^{(\alpha)}[k]
. This choice fixes a free
\Lambda
-module of rank one inside
{\det}^{-1}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{f}(k),\alpha\right)
, called the fundamental line, and the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k])
is defined as the image of this
\Lambda
-module in
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
under the canonical trivialization
i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(k)}^{(\alpha)}.

Let
f^{*}=f\otimes\varepsilon_{f}^{-1}
denote the dual form. Recall from §
0.1.3
that
\iota
is the canonical involution on
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
. We put
\alpha^{*}:=p^{k-1}/\beta
(which is one of the roots of the Hecke polynomial of the dual form
f^{*}
at
p
), and let
\xi^{*}
denote the basis of the Betti realization of the motive associated to
f^{*}
that is dual to
\xi
(cf. §
2.4.1
). Perrin-Riou’s formulation of the Main Conjecture asserts that

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k]\right)=\left(L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right)

as
\Lambda
-modules if the lattices
T_{f}(k)
and
N_{\alpha}[k]
are compatible with the period isomorphisms. We refer the reader to
[
PR95
]
and
[
Ben14
]
for precise statements. Thanks to the fundamental works of Kato, Rubin, and Skinner–Urban,
this conjecture is proved in a wide variety of cases, cf.
[
Kat04
,
SU14
,
Wan15
]
.

0.2.6.

In
[
PR95
]
, Perrin-Riou built a very general machine that associates a
p
-adic
L
-function to any norm-compatible system of global cohomology classes. In
[
Kat04
]
, Kato constructed a canonical element
\mathbf{z}(f,\xi)
in the first Iwasawa cohomology of
V_{f}
and showed that the corresponding
p
-adic
L
-function
3
3

3

The subscript “K” in the notation of
p
-adic
L
-functions is the initial of “Kato”.

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
coincides with
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*}).

0.2.7.

Classical formulations of the Main Conjecture (especially if
f
is ordinary at
p
) are stated in terms of the characteristic ideals of corresponding Selmer groups. These are equivalent to the formulation in the previous paragraph. However, since the formalism of Selmer complexes
[
Nek06
]
plays a crucial role in our study of the
\theta
-critical scenario, we adopt it from the beginning. The main reason is that, in our treatment, we will be naturally led to consider
p
-adic representations over various (simple) artinian rings
A
, such as
\mathcal{O}_{E}[X]/(X^{2})
. The Galois cohomology modules over such rings are not perfect objects in the derived category of
A
-modules, which is remedied by working with the total derived functors
\mathbf{R\Gamma}.

0.3.
The
\theta
-critical case

0.3.1.

We turn our attention to the
\theta
-critical case.
It is very different by nature from the classical case, and we describe
below some new phenomena which appear in this setting:

0.3.2.
The eigencurve at a
\theta
-critical point

If
f_{\alpha}
is
\theta
-critical, then
\mathcal{C}^{\rm cusp}
is still smooth
at
x_{0}
, but it
is no longer étale over
\mathcal{WS}

[
Bel12
, Theorem 2.16 and Proposition 4.6]
. Let
e>1
denote the ramification
index of the weight map at
x_{0}.
We fix a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
that contains the image of the Hecke field of
f_{\alpha}.

Then there exist affinoid neighborhoods

\mathcal{W}={\rm Spm}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}})
and
\mathcal{X}={\rm Spm}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
of
k\in\mathcal{WS}(E)
and
x_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{\rm cusp}(E)
of the form

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}=E\left<Y/p^{2r}\right>,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}[X]/(X^{e}-Y).

Here, we use the standard notation

E\langle Z\rangle:=\left\{\sum_{n\geq 0}c_{n}Z^{n}:c_{n}\in E,\lim_{n\to\infty}c_{n}=0\right\}

to denote the Tate-algebra over
E
with parameter
Z
. We will adjust our choice of
\mathcal{W}
on shrinking it as necessary (which amounts to increasing
r
) for our arguments.

0.3.3.
Overconvergent modular symbols

The generalized
f_{\alpha}
-eigenspace
\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}
the space of overconvergent modular symbols
has dimension
e
over
E.
More precisely, the action of the Atkin–Lehner
operator
U_{p}
equips
\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}
with the structure of an
E[X]
-module, and

(0.2)

\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}\simeq E[X]/(X^{e}).

0.3.4.
Local
p
-adic representation

The restriction of the representation
V_{f}
on the decomposition group at
p
splits into
direct sum of two one-dimensional representations, namely

(0.3)

V_{f}=V_{f}^{(\alpha)}\oplus V_{f}^{(\beta)},

where
V_{f}^{(\alpha)}
and
V_{f}^{(\beta)}
are crystalline of Hodge–Tate
weights
1-k
and
0
respectively, cf.
[
BE10
]
.

0.3.5.
Triangulation

Shrinking
\mathcal{X}
if necessary, we can consider
the canonical (up to an isomorphism)
p
-adic Galois representation
V_{\mathcal{X}}
of rank two over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
interpolating Deligne’s
p
-adic representations
at classical points. Let
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathcal{X}})
denote the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module attached to
V_{\mathcal{X}}
. This module is equipped with
a canonical submodule
\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
of rank one (triangulation of
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathcal{X}})
) afforded by the refinements of modular forms at classical points.
As a result of the decomposition (
0.3
), the specialization
\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}
of
\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
at
x_{0}

is a non-saturated submodule of
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{f})
. More precisely,

(0.4)

\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}=t^{k-1}\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{f}^{(\alpha)}),

(cf.
[
KPX14
]
, §6.4), where
t
is Fontaine’s
``2\pi i"
.

0.3.6.

Greenberg conjectured that the restriction of the Galois representation
V_{f}
to the decomposition group at
p
splits only if

f
has CM, cf.
[
Gha04
]
. It is therefore expected that the
\theta
-criticality occurs only in the CM setting.

In the unpublished note
[
Bel
]
4
4

4

We are grateful to J. Bergdall and R. Pollack for bringing Bellaïche’s note to our attention.
, Bellaïche explains that a conjecture of Jannsen
[
Jan89
]
combined with Greenberg’s conjecture and the decomposition (
0.3
) would imply
e=2
.

0.3.7.
Two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function

If
x_{0}
is
\theta
-critical, Bellaïche
[
Bel12
, §4.4]
constructed
a two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
,
which interpolates
p
-adic
L
functions of Amice–Vélu and Višik
at classical points
x\in\mathcal{X}\setminus\{x_{0}\}.
The behavior of
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
at
x_{0}
is more complicated (as compared to its counterpart in the non-
\theta
-critical scenarios) and reflects the structure (
0.2
) of the module of overconvergent modular symbols. Let
X
denote the local parameter about
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
, and consider

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[i]}(f,\xi):=\dfrac{\partial^{i}}{\partial X^{i}}L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})\Big|_{X=0}\,\qquad\qquad i=0,\ldots,e-1\,.

The function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
is simply the specialization
of
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
at
x_{0}.

For
1\leqslant i\leqslant e-1
, the functions
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[i]}(f,\xi)
are Bellaïche’s secondary
p
-adic
L
-functions. In
[
Bel12
, §4.4]
, Bellaïche showed that if
i<e-1
, then

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[i]}(f,\xi;\chi^{j})=0

in the critical range
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and that
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[e-1]}(f,\xi)
verifies the expected interpolation property (
0.1
). In particular,
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[e-1]}(f,\xi)
is nonzero.
We also remark that
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
decomposes into the product

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell_{i}^{\iota}\right)\cdot L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{\textrm{imp}}(f,\xi),\qquad\qquad\ell_{i}^{\iota}=i+\frac{\log(\gamma_{1})}{\log\chi(\gamma_{1})},

where
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{\textrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
is bounded. We do not know whether or not
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
is nonzero; see, however, Remark
0.4.3
for our results in this direction.

We refer the reader to §
2.3.4
and §
2.3.5
for a summary of these results.

0.3.8.

Bellaïche’s constructions proceed by interpolating Betti cohomology. Our first objective in the present article is to recover his results via interpolation of étale cohomology. Throughout this paper, we assume that
e=2
, as this clarifies the exposition greatly (and it is always expected to hold). We shall give an alternative construction of Bellaïche’s
p
-adic
L
-functions in terms of Beilinson–Kato elements and the triangulation over the Coleman–Mazur–Buzzard eigencurve (see §
2
). One of the consequences of our construction is the leading term formulae we prove (in §
5
) for the critical
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Another objective of the present article is to introduce the algebraic counterparts of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions and develop the tools for an Iwasawa theoretic treatment of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions (see §
3
and §
4
).
Our key insight in this treatment is that the Iwasawa theory of the
p
-adic

L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
is somehow trivial, and that the Main Conjecture in the
\theta
-critical setting should relate the thick
p
-adic

L
-function

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})+XL_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})

to Iwasawa theoretic invariants of the infinitesimal deformation

V_{k}=V_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/(X^{2})
of
V_{f}
along the eigencurve.

We have organized our discussion to emphasize the underlying principles in an abstract setting. These suggest that our constructions in the present article can be immensely generalized. For example, we will build on our approach in the present article in future work to construct
p
-adic Rankin–Selberg
L
-functions in the
\theta
-critical case (where there are no prior constructions available). This is crucial if one desires to extend the results of
[
BPS
]
on
p
-adic Gross–Zagier formulae to treat neighborhoods of
\theta
-critical (not only critical slope, as in op. cit.) points.

Before we explain our results in detail and outline our strategy, let us compare our approach via Beilinson–Kato elements and the triangulation over the eigencurve to the approach via the theory of modular symbols.

Bellaïche and Stevens construct the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{X},\xi})
by evaluating a generator of the space of modular symbols (which they prove to be a locally free sheaf of rank one over the eigencurve) at the divisor
\{\infty\}-\{0\}
. One may think of the divisor
\{\infty\}-\{0\}
as an analogue of the
p
-local Beilinson–Kato element as follows. By
[
Eme05
, Corollary 4.5]
, this divisor can be thought of as a functional on the completed cohomology of the classical modular curves. Combined with the local-global compatibility proved in
[
Eme11
]
and Colmez
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence (see
[
Col10
]
), one may then construct an element in the
p
-local Iwasawa cohomology. One expects
5
5

5

Shortly after an initial version of this article was circulated as
[
BB
]
, Colmez and Wang released a preprint
[
CW
]
emphasizing this viewpoint.
this class to be closely related to the
p
-local Beilinson–Kato element.

Pairing the
p
-local image of the Beilinson–Kato element with the exponential of a suitable differential which arises from the triangulation of the eigencurve about
x_{0}
, we recover

L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\mathcal{X},\xi})
.
In Bellaïche’s construction, the non-étaleness of the eigencurve at
x_{0}
is reflected by the non-semisimplicity (as a Hecke module) of the space of modular symbols; cf. (
0.2
).
In our construction, it exhibits itself as the failure (
0.4
) of the global triangulation over the eigencurve to restrict to a saturated triangulation at
x_{0}
.

0.4.
Overview of constructions and results

Set-up

. The weight map

There are natural free

0.4.2.
p
-adic
L
-functions

The starting point of our étale construction of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions is the interpolation of the Beilinson–Kato elements over
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\rm cusp}
to the (partially normalized)
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic Beilinson–Kato class
\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})
. See §
2.4.3
for a summary of the basic properties of this class. The construction of
\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
is one of the main results of the prequel
[
BB22
, Theorem A]
to the present article, where it is denoted by
{\mathbb{BK}}^{[\mathcal{X}]}_{N}(j,\xi)
.

Remark A
.

We briefly comment on the construction of the “big” Beilinson–Kato class
\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
in
[
BB22
]
and its comparison with related literature.

The construction of this class in the context of slope-zero families is the subject of Ochiai’s work
[
Och05
]
. Hansen
[
Han16
]
and Wang
[
Wan21
]
, using different methods, give a construction of such a class under the condition that
\mathcal{X}
is étale over
\mathcal{W}
. The approach in
[
BB22
]
(which, crucially for the present work, applies also when
\mathcal{X}
is smooth but
not
étale over
\mathcal{W}
) is a synthesis of the techniques of
[
Bel12
,
LZ16
,
Han16
]
, and relies on the overconvergent étale cohomology of Andreatta–Iovita–Stevens
[
AIS15
]
and Kings’ theory of
\Lambda
-adic sheaves developed in
[
Kin15
]
. This approach requires checking (which we do, building on the results of Ash–Stevens and Pollack–Stevens) that the construction of the local pieces of the cuspidal eigencurve using different types of distribution spaces over the weight space, or compactly versus non-compactly supported cohomology produce the same end result. The interested reader may refer to
[
BB22
, §5.3, §5.1.5, §6.3]
for details on this technical aspect. In a different direction, Colmez and Wang interpolate Beilinson–Kato elements over the universal deformation space
[
CW
]
.

Fix a generator
\eta\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
such that its specialization
at
x_{0}
coincides with the canonical generator of
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\alpha)})
(cf. §
2.4.3
). Our
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)
is obtained on pairing

\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
with the image of the element

\bbeta=1\otimes(X\eta)+X\otimes\eta\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

under the large (Perrin-Riou) exponential map
\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}}
associated
to
\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
(cf. Definition
2.10
and
2.22
).
We refer the reader to §
1.1.4
for a very general (abstract) discussion of large exponential maps, and to §
2.2.2
and §
2.4.2
for an overview of the Kedlaya–Pottharst–Xiao triangulation.

The interpolation properties of
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
(and its improvements) are the content of Theorem
B
below. In analogy
with Bellaïche’s construction, we let

L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi):=L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi;x_{0})

denote the specialization of
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
to
x_{0}
, and put

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi):=\displaystyle\left.\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial X}L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\right)\right|_{X=0}.

We let
L^{\pm}_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)^{\pm}
denote the
\pm 1
-eigencomponent of
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
for the action of complex conjugation in
\Gamma
. We similarly define
L^{[0],\pm}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
and
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi)
. For any continuous character

\phi\,:\,\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*}
, we put
L(f,\xi;\phi):=\phi(L(f,\xi))
where
L=L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha},L^{[0],\pm}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha},L^{[1],\pm}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}
, etc.

Theorem B

(Theorem
2.23
(i) and Equation (
2.46
))
.

Assume that
e=2
.

At any classical point
x\in\mathcal{X}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
,
the specialization
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi;x)
of
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
to
x
agrees with the Manin–Vishik
p
-adic
L
-function

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha(x)}(f_{x}^{\circ},\xi_{x})
in the following sense: there exist
some nonzero constants
A_{x}^{\pm}\in E
, which depend on the choice of
\eta
, such that

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi;x)=A_{x}^{\pm}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(x)\,L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha(x)}^{\pm}(f_{x}^{\circ},\xi_{x}),

where
\mathcal{E}_{N}(x)
is the product of Euler-like factors at bad primes defined
in (
2.42
).

There exists a bounded
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)\in\Lambda_{E}
such that

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell^{\iota}_{i}\right)L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)\,.

In particular,
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})=0
for all integers
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
characters
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
of finite order.

Note that the factor
\mathcal{E}_{N}(x)
appears already in the interpolation of Beilinson–Kato elements; cf. the main results of
[
BB22
]
.

We call
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
that appear in the statement of Theorem
B
the cyclotomic improvement of the critical
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
.

We may in fact show that our two-variable function
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
agrees with Bellaïche’s
p
-adic
L
-function.
The next theorem relies crucially on the validity of condition (
GP)
), which we formulate in Section
2.1.1
. Roughly speaking, it amounts to the requirement that the induced triangulation of the infinitesimal deformation is in general position with respect
to the Hodge–Tate filtration.

Theorem C

(Theorem
2.23
(ii) and Theorem
2.24
)
.

Assume that
e=2
and condition (
GP)
) holds true.
Then

The secondary
p
-adic
L
-functions
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi)
verify the following interpolation property:

For every character
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
of finite order and integer
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
we have

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})=C_{\mathrm{K}}\cdot{(j-1)!}\,e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(f{;\rho\chi^{j}})\,\frac{L_{\infty}(f,\rho^{-1},j)}{(2\pi i)^{j+1-k}\Omega_{f}^{\pm}}\,,\qquad\qquad\textrm{\,if\, $\rho(-1)=\mp(-1)^{j}\,,$}

where
e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)
is given as in (
2.34
),
C_{\mathrm{K}}
as in (
2.44
), and
{\mathcal{E}}_{N}(f;\rho\chi^{j})
is the value of
{\mathcal{E}}_{N}(f)
at
\rho\chi^{j}
; cf. Equation (
2.41
).

There exist functions
u^{\pm}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\times}

such that

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)=u^{\pm}\,{\mathcal{E}}_{N}\,L_{\mathrm{S}}^{\pm}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})\,.

Part i) of Theorem
C
is deduced from very general (abstract) results we obtain in §
2.2
; see in particular Proposition
2.12
and Theorem

. The crux of the proof of the latter is the “eigenspace-transition via differentiation” principle we establish in Proposition
2.6
, which we believe is of independent interest. Part ii) follows from Part i) and Theorem
B
via a continuity argument.

The proof of Theorem
C
shows that, even without assuming (
GP)
), we have (shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary)

(0.6)

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)=u^{\pm}\,{\mathcal{E}}_{N}\,L_{\mathrm{S}}^{\pm}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})

for some
u^{\pm}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
. If the condition (
GP)
) does not hold, the proof of Theorem
C
also shows that
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})=0
for all

and
j
as above. In fact, we have the following implications:

(0.7)

\textrm{\eqref{item_C4} fails}\quad\iff\qquad\parbox[t]{113.81102pt}{$L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi,\rho\chi^{j})=0$\\
on the central critical strip}\quad\iff\qquad u^{\pm}(x_{0})=0\quad\implies{}\quad L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi)=0.

This is the motivation for our:

Conjecture D

(see Conjecture
2.28
)
.

Condition (
GP)
)
holds true for the infinitesimal deformation provided by the eigencurve
at a
\theta
-critical point.

It follows from (
0.7
) that

(0.8)

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi)\neq 0\quad\implies\quad\mathrm{Conjecture~\ref{conj: GP intro}}.

We refer the reader to Remark

(iv) where we explain a proof of Conjecture
D
in many cases of interest. See also Remark

and §

for an elaboration on the hypothesis (

).

Let
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
denote the
p
-adic
L
-function associated to Kato’s zeta element via Perrin-Riou’s construction. It follows immediately from definitions that it coincides with
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)

up to the factor
\mathcal{E}_{N}(f).
Therefore,
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)

and
L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
are a pair of
p
-adic
L
-functions canonically attached
to
f
and
\xi
, and it follows from (
0.6
) that

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi)=\lambda^{\pm}(f)L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi)

for some canonically determined constants
u^{\pm}(x_{0})=:\lambda^{\pm}(f)\in E
. According to (
0.7
), the constants
\lambda^{\pm}(f)
are nonzero if and only if the property (
GP)
) holds true.

Question E
.

Can one compute the fudge factors
\lambda^{\pm}(f)
explicitly?

This question inquires about an explicit comparison of two natural constructions of
p
-adic
L
-functions, extending that in the non-
\theta
-critical scenario (cf. Proposition
2.20
below, where both constructions yield the same
p
-adic
L
-function on the nose). The constants
\lambda^{\pm}(f)
appear in the formulations of our Main Conjectures for
\theta
-critical forms (Conjectures
I
and
K
below). The fact that the non-vanishing of
\lambda^{\pm}(f)
is equivalent to a seemingly non-trivial property (
GP)
) suggests that this is a subtle problem.

We refer the reader to §
0.4.3
below where we observe that the non-vanishing of
\lambda^{\pm}(f)
can be explained, in the scenario when
f
has CM, by the non-vanishing of the non-critical values of Katz’
p
-adic
L
-function.

0.4.3.
Improved
p
-adic
L
-function and punctual Main Conjectures

Critical
\mathscr{L}
-invariants

In Chapter
3
, we address the arithmetic properties of our critical
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
as well as its cyclotomic improvement
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
, much in the spirit of Perrin-Riou’s theory. It follows from Kato’s fundamental results
[
Kat04
, Theorems 12.4 and 12.5]
that for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, the first Iwasawa cohomology
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
is a free
\Lambda_{E}
-module of rank one and the localization map

(0.9)

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))\oplus H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))

is injective (since the image of the Beilinson–Kato element under this map is non-zero
by Kato’s explicit reciprocity law). Here,
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))
and
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))
are both free
\Lambda_{E}
-modules of rank one, and come equipped with canonical bases. This allows us to define the slope
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\in\mathrm{Frac}(\Lambda_{E})

of the image of the map (
0.9
) with respect to these canonical bases, which we call the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant (see §
3.2.3
). Note that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))
for different
j
are related to one another via the twisting map in the Iwasawa algebra. Namely, we have

\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))=\mathrm{Tw}_{j}\left(\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})\right)\,.

The roots of the Hecke polynomial of the dual modular form
f^{*}
are

\alpha^{*}=p^{k-1}/\beta
and
\beta^{*}=p^{k-1}/\alpha.

In particular, the
\beta^{*}
-stabilization of
f^{*}
is
p
-ordinary, and we can consider the corresponding (ordinary)

p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
(which coincides with

L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
).
The next proposition follows immediately from the definitions:

Proposition F

(Proposition
2.27
)
.

Assume that
e=2
. Then,

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=(-1)^{k-1}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*})\,\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(k))\,L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\,.

It seems that there is no direct way to prove the analogue of the comparison in Proposition
F
for the
p
-adic
L
-functions that are constructed by interpolating Betti cohomology (namely, without appealing to Theorem
C
). This serves as an independent justification to pursue a Perrin-Riou style construction of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions.

Conjecture G
.

The Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
is a nonzero function.

We stress once again that it is a priori unclear that the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
, or Bellaïche’s
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
, is nonzero (see also
[
LLZ13
, Remark 3.5]
for a relevant discussion).
It follows from Proposition
F
that
the non-vanishing of
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})

is equivalent to the non-vanishing of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
. We then infer from
(
0.8
) that
Conjecture

implies Conjecture
D
.

We refer the reader to §
0.4.3
(which is a summary of our results in §
3.2.3
) for a detailed analysis of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
. In light of the discussion therein, we remark that proving its non-vanishing even at
\chi^{-j}
for even a single integer

, let alone its explicit computation, seems to be a difficult problem. For those integers
j
that are outside the critical range (e.g.
j\geq k
), Proposition
3.16
expresses its value at
\chi^{-j}
as the ratio of two
p
-adic regulators. When
f
has CM, the non-vanishing of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
at

can be related to the non-vanishing a value of Katz
p
-adic
L
-function outside its range of interpolation, cf. (

⁢
L
3

)
) below.

Throughout §
0.4.3
, we assume that
f
has CM by an imaginary quadratic field
K
where
p=\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{p}^{c}
necessarily splits.

In
[

]
, Bellaïche related
L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
to the restriction of the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function to a suitable “line” of characters. In
[
LLZ13
, Theorem 3.2]
, Lei–Loeffler–Zerbes proved an analogue of this result for
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
. As a result, we infer that when
f
has CM,
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
and
L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
coincide up to multiplication by a nonzero constant (cf.
[
LLZ13
]
, Theorem 3.4).

It follows from the discussion in the preceding paragraph that
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
and
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
are nonzero if and only if the restriction of a Katz
p
-adic
L
-function to a suitable range of characters (more precisely, to a range of characters whose Hodge–Tate weights at
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{c}
are
1-k+j
and
j
, respectively) is. Since the range of interpolation for the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function contains no such characters, the question as to whether
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
and
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
are nonzero remains open in general.

Our results show that
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=\lambda^{\pm}(f^{*})\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*})\,L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
, or equivalently
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=\lambda^{\pm}(f^{*})\,L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
, without assuming
f
is a CM form. If we assume in addition that
f
is a CM form, we explain in §
3.2.2
(see also (

⁢
L
3

)
) below for a summary) that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
is nonzero if and only if the said restriction of the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function is nonzero, exploiting Rubin’s proof of Iwasawa main conjectures over imaginary quadratic fields. Combined with the result of Bellaïche, this gives a new proof of the results of
[
LLZ13
]
, exhibiting a consistency between the two approaches.

Let us assume in this paragraph that
f=f_{A}
is the newform attached to an elliptic curve

(that necessarily has CM) with

In Corollary

Conjecture H

\mathscr{L}1

Punctual main conjecture for the improved
p
-adic

-function and Iwasawa descent

Recall that the
\beta^{*}
-stabilization of

is
p
-ordinary, and the classical (
p

(

,
β
)

relates the

-adic

-function

to the module
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k),N_{\beta}[k])
of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions. On the other hand, if
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f})
is a nonzero function, Perrin-Riou’s construction provides us with a well-defined

p
-adic

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k]).
We can now formulate the punctual

-critical Main Conjecture

(

,

)

:

Conjecture I
.

Conjecture
G
holds and

MC

(
f
,
α
)

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k])^{\pm}=\left(\lambda^{\pm}(f^{*})\cdot L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{\mathrm{imp},\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right)
as
\Lambda
-modules.

We immediately obtain the following from Proposition
F
and the known cases
of the
p
-ordinary Main Conjecture:

Theorem J
.

Assume that
e=2
and
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
is a nonzero function.
Then the conjectures

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

are equivalent.
In particular,

MC

(
f
,
α
)

holds assuming that either the condition (
SZ1)
) or else the condition (
SZ2)
) below is valid.

Modules of
p
-adic
L
-functions are defined in terms of an Iwasawa theoretic fundamental line given as in §
3.3.3
, whose decent properties play a crucial role in our leading term formulae for the modules of
p
-adic
L
-functions in §
3.5
. Our main result in this vein is Theorem
3.45
, where we prove Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer-style formulae (both within and off the critical range) for the the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions
\mathbf{L}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])
, involving the classical invariants associated to
f_{\alpha}
, which include the Tate–Shafarevich groups,
p
-adic regulators and Tamagawa numbers; see §
3.2.4
for definitions of these objects.

Let us assume in this paragraph that
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})=0
. We close §
0.4.3
with an explanation of why it is conceivable in this scenario that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f})
has a simple pole at
\chi^{-\frac{k}{2}}
. In view of Proposition
F
, this is equivalent to saying that the order of vanishing of
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
at
\chi^{-\frac{k}{2}}
is one less than that of the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
. If

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

both hold true (cf. Theorem
J
), then this is equivalent to the assertion that the order of vanishing of the algebraic
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k])^{\pm}
at
\chi^{-\frac{k}{2}}
is one less than that of its slope-zero counterpart
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k),N_{\beta}[k])^{\pm}
. In Theorem
3.44
below, we explain (under certain hypotheses which are expected to always hold true) that this amounts to a comparison of the dimensions of the fine Selmer group
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
and the Bloch–Kato Selmer group
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
, and indeed that the dimension of the former is one less than that of the latter.

0.4.4.
Main Conjectures for the infinitesimal deformation

Theorem
J
shows that, once the non-vanishing of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
is known, the Main Conjectures

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

are equivalent. Let us set
\widetilde{E}=E[X]/(X^{2})
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)=\widetilde{E}\otimes_{E}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).

The interpolation formulae for
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
(compare with Theorem
C
(i)) suggest that not only the critical
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
but the
thick

p
-adic
L
-function

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})={L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})+X\cdot{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\in\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma),

carries arithmetic information. Since the construction of Bellaïche’s secondary
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi)

involves the infinitesimal deformation of
V_{f}
along the eigencurve, it is natural to consider this deformation as a natural source of arithmetic invariants.

This motivates us to define and study the properties of what we call the thick Selmer complex and (Iwasawa theoretic) fundamental line associated with the infinitesimal deformation
V_{k}
of
V_{f}
along the eigencurve. As before, we combine Perrin-Riou’s approach in
[
PR95
]
and the formalism of Selmer complexes. In the context of infinitesimal deformations studied in this book, it is crucial that we work in the derived categories of modules over appropriate Iwasawa algebras,
and the theory of Selmer complexes is indispensable even for the formulation of the Main Conjecture. The reason is that the infinitesimal deformations of classical Iwasawa algebras contain nilpotent elements and the theory of determinants over such rings is far more complicated than that over regular rings. We refer the reader to Section
4.1
for further details and explanations. We also note that the exposition of classical Perrin-Riou’s theory from the point of view of Selmer complexes can be found in
[
Ben14
]
.

Let
\varpi_{E}
denote a uniformizer of
E
and let us put
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}:=\mathcal{O}_{E}+\varpi^{-n}\mathcal{O}_{E}X\subset\widetilde{E}.
It is not difficult to see (cf. Lemma
4.1
) that for any
n\gg 0
, there exists a Galois-stable
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
-lattice
T^{(n)}_{k}
contained in the free
\widetilde{E}
-module
V_{k}
of rank
2
, which can be viewed as an infinitesimal thickening of
T_{f}
, and which is uniquely determined in a suitable sense. Set
\Lambda^{(n)}=\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)=\widetilde{E}\otimes_{E}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).
Fix a free
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
-submodule
N^{(n)}

of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})/X^{2}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
such that
N^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}^{(n)}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{E}=N_{\alpha}.

We consider the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}
\textstyle{\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]})\right)\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\,\,.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]})
denote the local condition at
\ell\in S.

For
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}
we take the usual unramified local conditions, which (see
[
Nek06
]
):

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}):=\left[(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))^{I_{\ell}}\otimes\Lambda^{\iota}\xrightarrow{1-f_{\ell}}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))^{I_{\ell}}\otimes\Lambda^{\iota}\right],

where
I_{\ell}
is the inertia subgroup at
\ell
and
f_{\ell}
is the geometric Frobenius. In particular, it doesn’t depend on
N_{k}^{(n)}.

For
\ell=p,
we take

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}):=\left(N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)[-1],

and the vertical map is induced by the derived version of the large exponential map.
Let
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k},\alpha)
denote the Selmer complex defined by the above diagram.
If Conjecture
G
holds (i.e. the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})
is non-zero), then the
cohomology of this complex is concentrated in degree
2
, and the
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k},\alpha)
has a free resolution of length
1
. This allows us to define a canonical trivialization

i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\,:\,{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\,.

As in the classical case (cf.
[
PR95
,
Ben14
]
),
the Iwasawa-theoretic fundamental line
\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}}[j]\right)
is defined as

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}

\displaystyle\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right):=

\displaystyle{\det}^{-1}_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\oplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right)\right)\right)\otimes{\det}_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\right)\right).

Note that it follows from the general results of Flach
[
Fla00
]
that the determinants in this definition are well-defined. The exact triangle (
4.4
) provides us with a natural injection

(0.12)

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right)\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\,.

Let us set
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(\infty)}:=\underset{n\geqslant 0}{\cup}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
and put

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right):=\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(\infty)},\qquad n\gg 0.

(One can show that this definition does not depend on
n
.) By linearity, the injection (
0.12
) extends to an injective map

(0.13)

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),{N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]}\right)\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\,,

which allows to identify
\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),{N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]}\right)
with a projective
\Lambda^{(\infty)}
-submodule
of the
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)
-module
{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right).

Armed with these constructions, we define the infinitesimal thickening

{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right)
of the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions as the image of

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right)

under (
0.13
) composed with the canonical map
i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}
.

We may now formulate the infinitesimal thickening

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

of the Main Conjecture:

Conjecture K
.

Conjecture
G
holds true, and

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{[\infty)}(k),N^{(\infty)}_{\alpha}[k]\right)^{\pm}=\left(\lambda^{\pm}(f^{*})\cdot\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right)
as
\Lambda^{(\infty),\pm}
-modules.

The infinitesimal thickening

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

of the Main Conjecture implies the punctual Main Conjecture

MC

(
f
,
α
)

(and therefore, also the
p
-ordinary Main Conjecture

MC

(

,
β
)

). Moreover, based on the Iwasawa descent formalism we develop in §
4.2.5
, we prove Theorem
L
below, which tells us that

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

is consistent with the combined conclusions of

MC

(
f
,
β
)

and Theorem
B
.

Theorem L

(Theorem
4.15
and Corollary
4.16
)
.

Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
be an integer such that either
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
, or else
j=\frac{k}{2}
and
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0.

Assume that
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(k-j))\neq 0
and

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

is valid. Then:

L^{[1],\pm}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*},\chi^{j})=u\cdot C_{\mathrm{K}}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*};\chi^{j})\cdot\frac{e_{p,\alpha^{*}}(f^{*}\mathds{1},j)}{e_{p,\beta^{*}}(f^{*}\mathds{1},j)}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\beta}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)^{\pm}\,.

Here,
u\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}
is a
p
-adic unit,
C_{\mathrm{K}}
is given as in (
2.44
),
and
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\beta}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)^{\pm}
is the leading term at the trivial character of the module of slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-functions.

We remark that the thick Selmer complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)
are not semi-simple. As a result, we can not make use of the standard descent techniques (cf.
[
BG03
]
, Lemma 8.1). Note that Theorem
L
avoids the scenario when

, since our descent formalism fails in that case.

Height formulae

In a complementary direction to our work we summarized in §
0.4.3
and §
0.4.4
(where our main tool was Iwasawa descent), we calculate the special values of our
p
-adic
L
-functions at the central critical point (and when
L(f,\frac{k}{2})=0
) in terms of
p
-adic regulators:

Theorem M

(Theorem
5.29
)
.

Suppose that
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f,s)=1
and Perrin-Riou’s conjecture (
PR)
) as well as the condition (
GP)
) hold true.

(
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic leading term formula) For sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
, we have the following identity in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
:

X\cdot{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s}L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\ \Big|_{{s=\frac{w(x)}{2}}}=-R_{\mathcal{X}}\,.

Here,
{\rm LOG}_{\eta}
is the big logarithm introduced in Definition
5.26
,
P
is the fixed generator of the big Selmer group
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
as in §
5.3.5
, and
R_{\mathcal{X}}
is the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic regulator given as in (
5.31
) .

(Leading term formula for the thick
p
-adic
L
-function)
We have the following identity in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/X^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \log_{\omega_{x_{0}}^{\prime}}(P_{x_{0}})\,\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}&(f,\xi)\Big|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}\\
&={(-1)^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\,C_{\mathrm{K}}}\,{\Gamma\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{\alpha}\right)\left(1-\alpha p^{-\frac{k}{2}}\right)^{-1}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}+\frac{X}{2}\frac{d^{2}R_{\mathcal{X}}}{dX^{2}}\big|_{X=0}\right).\end{aligned}

where
P_{x_{0}}\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})
is the specialization of
P
to the center
x_{0}
of the affinoid
\mathcal{X}
(which corresponds to the
\theta
-critical eigenform
f_{\alpha}
), and

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})\xrightarrow{\langle\,,\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}}E

is the specialization of the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-valued height pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}
to weight
k
.

In particular, Theorem
M
(ii) tells us that the derivative of an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic regulator (rather than a regulator itself) computes the derivative
\frac{\partial}{\partial s}{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi)\Big|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}
of the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function.

It is evident that our constructions in the context of the Buzzard–Coleman–Mazur eigencurve is a particular case of a very general phenomenon. It would be very interesting to study this in the context of general eigenvarieties. This includes the case of
(\mathrm{GL}2\times\mathrm{GL}2)_{/\mathbb{Q}}
, which we plan to investigate in the near future.

A natural question
12
12

12

We thank the anonymous referee for bringing this to our attention.
is what happens when the
\theta
-critical eigenform
f^{\alpha}
is replaced with a
p
-regular theta series
g
of a real quadratic field in which
p
splits. By the work of Bellaïche and Dimitrov
[
BD16
, Theorem 1.1]
, the the eigencurve is smooth but not étale at the corresponding point
x_{g}
. Our construction of the
p
-adic
L
-function in §
2
applies in this scenario. One important difference (between the case of weight-one form and the case of
\theta
-critical forms that we consider here) is that the Kedlaya–Pottharst–Xiao triangulation is saturated at
x_{g}
, cf.
[
KPX14
, Proposition 6.4.5]
. As a result, the
extreme exceptional zero phenomenon
(see Part (ii) of Theorem
B
) for the
p
-adic
L
-function associated to a
\theta
-critical eigenform does not seem to occur in this case. In the same vein, since new phenomena (as far as our constructions are concerned) emerge only when the triangulation fails to be saturated at the point of the eigencurve that we are interested in, our arithmetic considerations in Chapters
3
and onward do not seem to apply in the case of weight-one points.

0.4.5.
Layout

We close our introduction with a layout of our article.

Chapter
1
reviews general constructions that we rely on throughout the article. This involves a review of
p
-local Galois cohomology in §
1.1.2
, Perrin-Riou’s large exponential maps in §
1.1.4
and the theory of exponential maps over affinoid algebras in §
1.1.8
. In §
1.2
, we introduce Selmer complexes (à la Perrin-Riou, Pottharst, and the first named author)
in great generality and review their basic properties. We review the formalism of
p
-adic heights in §
1.3
and conclude this chapter proving in §
1.3.3
a Rubin-style formulae for
p
-adic height pairings.

We begin Chapter
2
establishing our “eigenspace-transition by differentiation” principle (Proposition
2.6
), which is of fundamental importance for our methods. In §
2.2
, we develop an abstract theory of Perrin-Riou
p
-adic
L
-functions in a scenario where the underlying triangulation is non-saturated. This is, of course, modelled on the behaviour of the relevant objects in the context of the Buzzard–Coleman–Mazur eigencurve, and inspired by the beautiful work of Bellaïche
[
Bel12
]
. The eigenspace-transition by differentiation principle is used in this portion to prove the interpolation properties of the secondary (abstract)
p
-adic
L
-function (cf. Theorem
2.16
).

In §
2.3
and onwards, we focus our attention on the eigencurve and apply our general machinery in this context. We carefully review in §
2.3
the earlier constructions of
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to modular forms (and on the eigencurve) via modular symbols. We give an “étale” construction of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions in §
2.4
(see §
2.4.4
for the case of
\theta
-critical forms, which is the main focus of the present paper). We also explain in §
2.4.4
and §
2.4.5
their relation with their counterparts constructed via modular symbols.

Chapter
3
is devoted to an Iwasawa theoretic treatment of a
\theta
-critical form
f_{\alpha}
. After defining various Selmer groups attached to Deligne’s Galois representation
V_{f}
in §
3.2
, we introduce critical
\mathcal{L}
-invariants in §
3.2.3
, study their basic properties, and prove in §
3.2.3
that they can be interpolated to an Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant (which appears in the statement of Proposition
F
). A big bulk of §
3.2.3
is dedicated to establish explicit criteria for the non-vanishing of the critical
\mathcal{L}
-invariant. In §
3.3
, we introduce our modules of (punctual) algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions (both for the critical
p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}
and the slope-zero
p
-stabilization
f_{\beta}
), in terms of Iwasawa theoretic fundamental lines and Selmer complexes we define therein.

We formulate the punctual main conjectures

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

in §
3.4
and study their relation with one another.
For completeness, in §
3.5.4
, we prove Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer formulae for the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions within and off the critical range for
f
(which are due to Perrin-Riou in the non-
\theta
-critical scenario). These, of course, can be translated to leading term formulae for the punctual critical
p
-adic
L
-function
L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
whenever the punctual critical main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
α
)

holds
13
13

13

We explained in Theorem
J
that

MC

(
f
,
α
)

holds assuming that
e=2
, the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f})
is nonzero, and either the condition (
SZ1)
) or else the condition (
SZ2)
) is valid.
.

In Chapter
4
, we carry out the key algebraic constructions to counter our thick
p
-adic
L
-functions. In order to establish an integral theory (which can in turn afford a non-trivial descent theory), we first prove in §
4.1
that the infinitesimal deformation
V_{k}
contains a Galois stable lattice. Attached to this lattice, we introduce our thick fundamental line in §
4.1.2
, whose determinant naturally embeds into the determinant of our Iwasawa theoretic thick Selmer complex (cf. (
4.8
)). We establish a general criteria to bound the projective dimensions of
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{E}(\Gamma)
-modules in §
4.1.3
, which we apply with our Iwasawa theoretic thick Selmer complex to trivialize its determinant (assuming that the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant is nonzero), an in turn also our thick fundamental line.

With these constructions at hand, we can define our infinitesimal thickening of the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions (cf. Definition
4.4
) and formulate, in §
4.2.1
, the infinitesimal thickening

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

of the critical main conjectures, where we also prove that

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

is stronger than the punctual main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
α
)

. We establish a general descent formalism over the ring
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{E}(\Gamma)
in §
4.2.3
, which we apply in §
4.2.4
with the thick Iwasawa theoretic Selmer complex to obtain an explicit interpretation of the Bockstein morphisms (which are relevant to the definitions of
p
-adic heights). Based on these, we establish an Iwasawa descent procedure in §
4.2.5
avoiding the central critical point if
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})=0
. In this scenario, we prove a factorization formula for the specializations of the thick fundamental line in Theorem
4.14
in terms of the critical and slope-zero fundamental line, and a leading term formula for the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function in terms of the slope-zero fundamental line (cf. Theorem
L
above).

We supplement our discussion in Chapter

in §
