39.94789pt\raise-51.28168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37502pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}_{\beta,j,n}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.61249pt\raise-59.1289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-49.74998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.61249pt\raise-59.1289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 80.61249pt\raise-59.1289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{1}(K_{n},\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j)),}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

where the diagonal map is induced by the large exponential map for
\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j).

Throughout the remainder of §
2.1
, we assume that
\alpha\neq p^{k-1}
and
\beta\neq 1.
In other words, we assume that
V^{(\alpha)}\neq E(1-k)
and

V^{(\beta)}\neq E(0).

Note that this property holds automatically if
V
is the
p
-adic realization of a pure motive of motivic weight
w\notin\{0,2(k-1)\}
that has good reduction at
p
.

Let
\rho
be a finite character of
\Gamma
of conductor
p^{n}.

We treat
\rho
as a primitive character of
G_{n}.

Define

(2.9)

\displaystyle a(\rho,j)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{p^{j-1}}{\beta}\right)\left(1-\frac{\beta}{p^{j}}\right)^{-1},&\textrm{if $\rho=1$},\\
\alpha^{n}&\textrm{if $\rho\neq 1$},\end{cases}

\displaystyle b(\rho,j)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{p^{j-1}}{\alpha}\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{p^{j}}\right)^{-1},&\textrm{if $\rho=1$},\\
\beta^{n}&\textrm{if $\rho\neq 1$}.\end{cases}

Consider the maps

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\alpha,j}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}))\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(\rho^{-1}\chi^{j})),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\alpha,j}(z)=a(\rho,j)\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}(z)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(V^{(\beta)}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(\rho^{-1}\chi^{j})),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j}(z)=b(\rho,j)\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j,n}(z),

where
\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j,n}
denote the
\rho
-isotypic components of the underlying exponential maps (cf. §
1.1.1
).

The following “eigenspace-transition by differentiation” principle is key to our main calculations.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
(\widetilde{V},\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
be a deformation of
(V,\mathbf{D})
for which the condition (
GP)
) is satisfied. Assume in addition that
\alpha\neq p^{k-1}
and
\beta\neq 1.

Then for any finite character
\rho
and any integer
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathfrak{D}(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\alpha,j}}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{j}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{D}(V^{(\beta)}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(\rho^{-1}\chi^{j})).}

Proof.

Let
z=f(\pi)\otimes d_{\delta\chi^{j}}\in\mathfrak{D}(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}))

where
d_{\delta\chi^{j}}
is a fixed generator of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j})).

By Lemma
1.5
,

(2.10)

\Xi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}(z))=\begin{cases}d_{\delta\chi^{j}}\otimes f^{(\rho)}(\zeta_{p^{n}}-1)&\text{if $n\geqslant 1$},\\
(1-p^{-1}\varphi^{-1})(1-\varphi)^{-1}(d_{\delta\chi^{j}})&\text{if $n=0.$}\end{cases}

and analogously

\Xi_{V^{(\beta)}(j),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}(\overline{\kappa}_{j}(z)))=\begin{cases}{\overline{\kappa}_{j}}(d_{\delta\chi^{j}})\otimes f^{(\rho)}(\zeta_{p^{n}}-1)&\text{if $n\geqslant 1$},\\
(1-p^{-1}\varphi^{-1})(1-\varphi)^{-1}({\overline{\kappa}_{j}}(d_{\delta\chi^{j}}))&\text{if $n=0.$}\end{cases}

First, we consider the case
n\geqslant 1.
Then formula (
2.10
)
gives:

\Xi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}(z))\,\,\equiv\,\,X\cdot\Xi_{V^{(\beta)}(j),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}({{\kappa_{j}}}(z)))\pmod{\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1-j}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)})\otimes K_{n}}\,.

For
0\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
, we have
\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1-j}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j))\subset\operatorname{Fil}^{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j)).
Since
\operatorname{Fil}^{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j))\otimes K_{n}
is in the kernel of the exponential map, it follows that

(2.11)

\exp_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),K_{n}}\left(\Xi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}(z))\right)=\exp_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),K_{n}}\left(X\cdot\Xi_{{}V^{(\beta)}(j),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}({{\kappa_{j}}}(z)))\right).

Since the exponential map is
G_{n}
-equivariant, we deduce using
the diagrams (
1.18
) and (
2.8
) that

(2.12)

\widetilde{\alpha}^{n}\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}(z){=}\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}(z))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{diagram: comparision of exponentials for different slopes}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Exp}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),n}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}(z))\\
=(j-1)!\exp_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),K_{n}}(\Xi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}(z))),\qquad n\geqslant 0,

where
\widetilde{\alpha}\in\widetilde{E}
is the eigenvalue of
\varphi
on

{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
. Likewise,

(2.13)

\beta^{n}\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j,n}({\kappa_{j}}(z))=\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j,n}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}({\kappa_{j}}(z)))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm\eqref{diagram: comparision of exponentials for different slopes}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Exp}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),n}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}({X{\kappa_{j}}}(z)))\\
=(j-1)!\exp_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),K_{n}}(X\cdot\Xi_{V^{(\beta)}(j),n}^{(\rho)}((\mathrm{id}\otimes\varphi)^{n}({{\kappa_{j}}}(z)))),\qquad n\geqslant 0.

These together with (
2.11
) yield

(2.14)

\widetilde{\alpha}^{n}\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}(z)=\beta^{n}\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j,n}({\kappa_{j}}(z)).

Since we have
\widetilde{\alpha}(0)=\alpha\neq 0
, it follows that

\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}(z)\in X\cdot H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(\rho^{-1}\chi^{j}))

as well. Since we have
\widetilde{\alpha}-\alpha\in XE[X]/(X^{2})
and
X^{2}
annihilates
\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}
, this in turn shows that

(2.15)

\widetilde{\alpha}^{n}\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}(z)=\alpha^{n}\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho)}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),n}(z).

Now the case
\rho\neq\mathds{1}
of the proposition follows on combining (
2.14
) and (
2.15
).

It remains to consider the case
\rho=\mathds{1}
. Formula (
2.10
) reads

\displaystyle\Xi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),0}^{(\mathds{1})}(z)=b(\mathds{1},j)\,d_{\delta\chi^{j}},

\displaystyle\Xi_{V^{(\beta)}(j),0}^{(\mathds{1})}(\overline{\kappa}_{j}(z))=a(\mathds{1},j)\,\overline{\kappa}_{j}(d_{\delta\chi^{j}}).

Therefore

a(\mathds{1},j)\,\Xi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),0}^{(\mathds{1})}(z)\equiv Xb(\mathds{1},j)\,\Xi_{V^{(\beta)}(j),0}^{(\mathds{1})}\left(\overline{\kappa}_{j}(d_{\delta\chi^{j}})\right)\pmod{\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1-j}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)})\otimes K_{n}},

and

a(\mathds{1},j)\,\exp_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),K_{n}}\left(\Xi_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),0}^{(\mathds{1})}(z)\right)=b(\mathds{1},j)\,\exp_{\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(j),K_{n}}\left(X\cdot\Xi_{V^{(\beta)}(j),0}^{(\mathds{1})}\left(\overline{\kappa}_{j}(d_{\delta\chi^{j}})\right)\right)

for
0\leqslant j\leqslant k-1.
From diagram (
1.18
) we obtain that

a(\mathds{1},j)\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}),0}(z)=b(\mathds{1},j)\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}_{\beta,j,0}(z).

The proposition is proved.

∎

Corollary 2.7
.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathfrak{D}(V^{(\alpha)}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\kappa}_{j}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{D}(V^{(\beta)}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\beta,j}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}(\rho^{-1}\chi^{j})),}

where the vertical map is induced by
\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\rho)}_{\alpha,j}.

Corollary 2.8
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
2.6
, we have

\mathrm{im}\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\mathds{1})}_{\alpha,j}\right)=H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X\cdot\widetilde{V}(\chi^{j}))=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},XV^{(\beta)}(\chi^{j})).

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
2.6
and the fact that

\mathrm{im}\left(\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}^{(\mathds{1})}_{\beta,j}\right)=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},XV^{(\beta)}(\chi^{j}))=H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X\cdot\widetilde{V}(\chi^{j})).

∎

2.2.
Abstract critical
p
-adic
L
-functions

2.2.1.
Algebraic preliminaries

Let
E
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Fix an integer
k\geqslant 2
. For any auxiliary fixed integer
r\geqslant 0
, we consider
the affinoid
\mathcal{W}=\mathrm{Spm}\left(E\left<Y/p^{2r}\right>\right)
as the closed disc about

k
and radius
1/p^{2r}
. In more precise terms, if we let
D(k,p^{m})=k+p^{m}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
denote the closed disc with center
k
and radius
1/p^{m}
, we may then identify each
y\in D(k,p^{2r})
with the maximal ideal

\mathfrak{m}_{y}=\left((1+Y)-(1+p)^{y-k}\right).

In particular, it allows to identify the integers
k+p^{2r}\mathbb{Z}
with a subset
of
\mathcal{W}(E).
Morally,
\mathcal{W}
should be viewed as the weight space.

We analogously consider the affinoid
\mathcal{X}=\mathrm{Spm}\left(E\left<X/p^{r}\right>\right)

as a closed disc of radius
p^{r}
centered at some point
x_{0}
. The map

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}},\qquad Y\mapsto X^{2}

induces the map
w\,:\,\mathcal{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{W}
, which we refer as the weight map. Note that
w(x_{0})=k
. The weight map is ramified at
x_{0}
with ramification index
2
. The set

\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E):=\{x\in\mathcal{X}(E)\mid w(x)\in\mathbb{Z},{w(x)\geqslant 2}\}

will be called the set of classical points in
\mathcal{X}
.

Suppose that
M_{\mathcal{X}}
is a finitely generated free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module of rank
d.
We may consider
M_{\mathcal{X}}
also as a
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-module, via the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
. For any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E)
and
y\in\mathcal{W}(E),
we set

M_{x}=M_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x},\qquad M_{y}=M_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}},w}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}/\mathfrak{m}_{y}.

Note that
M_{x}
and
M_{y}
are finitely dimensional
E
-vector spaces of dimensions
d

and
2d
, respectively.
We denote by

\pi_{x}\,:\,M_{w(x)}\rightarrow M_{x}
the natural projection.

Consider
X
as a function on
\mathcal{X}.
For each
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),
let
X(x)
denote the value
of
X
at
x.
Set

M[x]:=\ker\left(X-X(x)\,:\,M_{w(x)}\rightarrow M_{w(x)}\right).

Note that
M[x]
is an
E
-vector space of dimension
d.
The multiplication by
X+X(x)

gives an isomorphism

M_{x}\xrightarrow{\sim}M[x].

In particular, for
x=x_{0}
one has
M_{x_{0}}=M_{\mathcal{X}}/XM_{\mathcal{X}},

M_{k}=M_{\mathcal{X}}/X^{2}M_{\mathcal{X}}
and

M[x_{0}]=XM_{\mathcal{X}}/X^{2}M_{\mathcal{X}}.

Suppose that
M_{\mathcal{X}}
and
M_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
are both free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules of
finite rank equipped with an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-linear (sic!) pairing

(\,,\,):M_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}M_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}

satisfying the following condition:

Adj)

For every
m^{\prime}\in M_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
and
m\in M_{\mathcal{X}}
, one has

(2.16)

(Xm^{\prime},m)=(m^{\prime},Xm).

For any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),
this pairing induces a pairing

(\,,\,)_{w(x)}:M_{w(x)}^{\prime}\otimes M_{w(x)}\longrightarrow E.

On restricting the second argument to
M[x]
in this pairing, we obtain a unique pairing

(\,,\,)_{x}\,:\,M^{\prime}_{x}\otimes_{E}M[x]\longrightarrow E

such that the restriction of
(\,,\,)_{w(x)}
to the subspace

M_{w(x)}^{\prime}\otimes_{E}M[x]
factors as

(2.17)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 35.32285pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-35.32285pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{\prime}_{w(x)}\otimes_{E}M[x]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.13351pt\raise-15.62991pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{x}\otimes{\rm id}\,\,}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.32285pt\raise-13.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.78458pt\raise 6.91945pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.33057pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\,,\,)_{w(x)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.74509pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 85.53397pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 141.74509pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-19.39874pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 59.32285pt\raise-19.39874pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{\prime}_{x}\otimes_{E}M[x]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 117.08989pt\raise-14.25938pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\,,\,)_{x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.74509pt\raise-2.24913pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We refer the reader to
[
BB22
, §2.4]
for a proof and further details.

Let
M_{\mathcal{X}}
be a
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module and let us put
M^{\circ}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}M_{\mathcal{X}}
, endowing it with the structure of an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module through the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-action on the first factor. We have
a natural specialization map

(2.18)

\mathrm{sp}_{x}\,:\,M^{\circ}_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow M_{w(x)}\,

which is defined as the composition

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}M\xrightarrow{x\otimes{\rm id}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}M\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}/\mathfrak{m}_{w(x)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}M=M_{w(x)}\,.

In particular, we have
\left(M^{\circ}_{\mathcal{X}}\right)_{x}\simeq M_{w(x)}
.

We also recall the following result:

Lemma 2.9
.

Assume that
M_{\mathcal{X}}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module of rank one.
Let
m
denote a generator of
M_{\mathcal{X}}
and let

\Phi=1\otimes Xm+X\otimes m\in M_{\mathcal{X}}.

Then:

i)

We have
(X\otimes 1)\Phi=(1\otimes X)\Phi.

ii)

For any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),
the element
{\rm sp}_{x}(\Phi)
generates the
E
-vector
space
M[x].

iii)

We have
{\rm sp}_{x_{0}}(\Phi)=Xm_{x_{0}}.

Proof.

This is an abstract version of
[
Bel12
, Lemma 4.13 & Proposition 4.14]
. See also
[
BB22
, §2.3]
. ∎

2.2.2.
Triangulations

We maintain the notations of Chapter
1
. Let
V_{\mathcal{X}}
be a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module of rank
2
endowed with a continuous action of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
.
According to our conventions in §
2.2.1
, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E)
, we set

V_{w(x)}=V_{\mathcal{X}}/\mathfrak{m}_{w(x)}V_{\mathcal{X}}

and
V_{x}=V_{\mathcal{X}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x}V_{\mathcal{X}}.

Recall that
V_{k}=V_{\mathcal{X}}/X^{2}V_{\mathcal{X}},

V_{x_{0}}=V_{\mathcal{X}}/XV_{\mathcal{X}},
and
V[x_{0}]:=XV_{k}
. We shall assume that the Galois representation
V_{\mathcal{X}}
has the following properties:

C1)

For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E),
the restriction of
V_{x}
on the decomposition group at
p
is semistable of Hodge–Tate weights
(0,1-w(x)).

C2)

There exists
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
such that for all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)
the eigenspace
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x})^{\varphi=\alpha(x)}
is one dimensional.

C3)

For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)-\{x_{0}\}

{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x})^{\varphi=\alpha(x)}\cap\operatorname{Fil}^{w(x)-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x_{0}})=0\,.

and

{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x_{0}})^{\varphi=\alpha(x_{0})}=\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x_{0}})\,.

The prototypical example of
V_{\mathcal{X}}
will arise as the Galois representation afforded by the eigencurve in a neighborhood of a
\theta
-critical point.

Let
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{X}}(V)
denote the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
over
\mathscr{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
associated to the restriction of
V
on
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}.
For each
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),

the specialization map identifies
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{X}}(V)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}},x}E
with the

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{x})
associated to the Galois representation
V_{x}.

On shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary, it follows from
[
KPX14
]
that one can construct a unique
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule
\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\subseteq\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},\mathcal{X}}(V)
of rank one satisfying the following properties:

\varphi\Gamma_{1}
)

\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathbf{D}_{\delta}
with
\delta\,:\,\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}
such that
\delta(p)=\alpha
and
\left.\delta\right|_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{*}}=1.

\varphi\Gamma_{2}
)

{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{x})={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x})^{\varphi=\alpha(x)}
for each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\textrm{cl}}(E)
, where
\mathbf{D}_{x}:=\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}},x}E
.

\varphi\Gamma_{3}
)

For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\textrm{cl}}(E)-\{x_{0}\},
the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{D}_{x}
is saturated in
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{x}).

Let
\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\mathrm{sat}}
denote the saturation of the specialization
\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}
of the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
at
x_{0}
. Then, it follows from the discussion in the final section of
[
KPX14
]
and in view of our running hypothesis (
C3)
), we have
\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}=t^{k-1}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\mathrm{sat}}
and

{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\mathrm{sat}})=\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x_{0}})
.
Set
V:=V_{x_{0}},

\mathbf{D}:=\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}},

\widetilde{V}:=V_{k}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}:=\mathbf{D}_{k}.

Then
(\widetilde{V},\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
is a deformation of

(V,\mathbf{D})
in the sense of Section
2.1.1
.

2.2.3.
Abstract
p
-adic
L
-functions in families

Suppose that we are given another free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
of rank two which is equipped with a continuous
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
-action, together with a Galois equivariant
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-linear pairing

(2.19)

(\,,\,):V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\otimes V_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}

satisfying condition (
Adj)
), namely that for every
v^{\prime}\in V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
and

v\in V_{\mathcal{X}}
, we have
(Xv^{\prime},v)=(v^{\prime},Xv)
.
On the level of Iwasawa cohomology, it induces the
\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}
-bilinear pairing
(
1.6
):

(2.20)

\left<\,\,,\,\,\right>\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}(1))\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}})^{\iota}\longrightarrow\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}.

(Here we consider
V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
and
V_{\mathcal{X}}
as
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-modules). Tensoring the first factor
with
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)
and the second one with
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)
(sic!), we obtain a

\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)
-bilinear map

(2.21)

\left<\,\,,\,\,\right>_{\mathcal{X}}\,:\,\left(\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}(1))\right)\otimes\left(\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}})^{\iota}\right)\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)\,.

In this section we introduce the formalism of Perrin-Riou’s
p
-adic
L
-functions following
[
BB22
, §4.2]
.
We note that our treatment here concerns the technically more challenging
\theta
-critical case, whereas in op. cit. we have studied the non-
\theta
-critical scenario.
The following construction is inspired by Bellaïche’s construction of secondary

p
-adic
L
-functions using modular symbols
[
Bel12
]
.

Recall that we set
\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}):=\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\pi]]^{\psi=0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}).

Although
\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
has a natural structure of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module, we consider it as
an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-module by restriction of scalars and set

\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}).

Then
\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D})_{\mathcal{X}}
has a natural structure of a
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module given
by the multiplication on the second factor.
More explicitly, one can write:

\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}[[\pi]]^{\psi=0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\,.

The large exponential map
\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},h}
can be extended by linearity to an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-linear map

\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},h}^{\circ}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}).

Composing this map with the injection

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}\left(\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}})\right)=\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}}),

we obtain a map (which we denote by the same symbol)

(2.22)

\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},h}^{\circ}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}})\,.

For any
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module generator
\eta\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}),
set

(2.23)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\bbeta:=X\otimes\eta+1\otimes(X\eta)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bbeta}:=(1+\pi)\otimes\bbeta\in\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ}.

The reader is invited to compare this with the definition of the modular symbol
\Phi
in
[
Bel12
, §4.3.3]
.

Let
c
denote the unique element of
\Gamma
of order
2
, so that we have
c(\zeta_{p^{n}})=\zeta_{p^{n}}^{-1}
for every natural number
n
.

Definition 2.10
.

For each
h\geqslant 0
and cohomology class
\mathbf{z}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}(1))
, we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},\eta,1-h}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}(1))\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},\eta,1-h}(\mathbf{z}):=\left<\mathbf{z},c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},h}(\widetilde{\bbeta})^{\iota}\right>_{\mathcal{X}}.

We define the two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function associated to
\mathbf{z}
setting

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z})=\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},\bbeta,1}(\mathbf{z})\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma).

In this subsection, we shall study the specializations of
L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z})
.
For any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E)
, let us set

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x):=L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z})_{x}\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\,.

By
[
BB22
, Lemma 2.3]
, the specialization
\bbeta_{x}
of
\bbeta
is a generator of

{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[x].

The large exponential map given as the compositum

\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}[x],h}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}[x])\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbf{D}[x])\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V[x])

is compatible with (
2.22
) in the evident sense, so that we have

(2.24)

\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},h}(\widetilde{\bbeta})_{x}=\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}[x],h}(\widetilde{\bbeta}_{x})\,.

Recall that for any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),
the specialization of (
2.19
)
gives rise to a pairing

V^{\prime}_{x}\otimes_{E}V[x]\xrightarrow{(\,\,,\,\,)_{x}}E.
Therefore one has a pairing
on Iwasawa cohomology:

(2.25)

\displaystyle\left<\,\,,\,\,\right>_{x}\,:\,\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{x}^{\prime}(1))\otimes\left(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V[x])^{\iota}\right)\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).

Since the pairing
(\,\,,\,\,)_{x}
is induced by the pairing
(\,\,,\,\,)_{w(x)}\,:\,V_{w(x)}^{\prime}\otimes V_{w(x)}\rightarrow E,
the pairing (
2.25
) is induced by the pairing

(2.26)

\left<\,,\,\right>_{w(x)}\,:\,\left(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{\prime}_{w(x)}(1))\right)\otimes\left(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{w(x)})\right)^{\iota}\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).

These facts will be used repeatedly.

We have a well defined map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbf{D}_{x},\eta_{x},1-h}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{x}^{\prime}(1))\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{E}(\Gamma)\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbf{D}_{x},\eta_{x},1-h}(z):=\left<z,c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}[x],h}(\widetilde{\bbeta}_{x})^{\iota}\right>_{x}.

For any
\mathbf{z}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}(1))
, set

L_{p,\eta_{x}}(\mathbf{z}_{x}):=\mathfrak{L}_{\mathbf{D}_{x},\eta_{x},1}(\mathbf{z}_{x}),

where
\mathbf{z}_{x}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{x}^{\prime}(1))
is the specialization of
\mathbf{z}.

The proposition below follows immediately from the above discussion and the functoriality
of cup-products:

Proposition 2.11
.

For any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),
one has

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x)=L_{p,\eta_{x}}(\mathbf{z}_{x}).

2.2.4.
Specialization at
x_{0}

For any continuous character
\rho\,:\,\Gamma\rightarrow E^{*}
, let

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x,\rho):=\rho\circ L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x).

denote the value of
L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x)
at
\rho.

We also define analytic
p
-adic
L
-functions

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x,\rho,s):=L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x,\rho\left<\chi\right>^{s})\,.

In this subsection, we are interested in the specialization of
L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z})
at
x_{0}.

From definition,
it follows that

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z})=\left<\mathbf{z},c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D},0}(X\widetilde{\eta})^{\iota}\right>_{\mathcal{X}}+X\left<\mathbf{z},c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D},0}(\widetilde{\eta})^{\iota}\right>_{\mathcal{X}}.

Hence,

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})=\left<\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}[x_{0}],0}(X\widetilde{\eta}_{x_{0}})^{\iota}\right>_{x_{0}}\,.

Note that
\mathbf{D}[x_{0}]
is canonically isomorphic to the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
associated to
V^{(\alpha)}_{x_{0}},
and therefore

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})=\left<\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},X\left(c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)},0}(\widetilde{\eta}_{x_{0}})^{\iota}\right)\right>_{x_{0}}.

Proposition 2.12
.

The
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})

can be written in the form

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell_{i}^{\iota}\right)\cdot L_{p,\eta}^{\mathrm{imp}}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}}),

where
L_{p,\eta}^{\mathrm{imp}}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}})\in\Lambda_{E}
and
\ell_{i}^{\iota}=i+\log(\gamma_{1})/\log\chi(\gamma_{1}).

For any finite character
\rho\in X(\Gamma),
the analytic
p
-adic function

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho,s)
can be written in the form

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho,s)=\underset{i=1}{\overset{k-1}{\prod}}(s-i)\cdot L_{p,\eta}^{\mathrm{imp}}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho,s),

where the function
L_{p,\eta}^{\mathrm{imp}}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho,s)
is bounded
on the open unit disc.

Proof.

Since
V^{(\alpha)}
is a one-dimensional representation with Hodge–Tate weight
-(k-1)
,
the Perrin-Riou exponential map is defined over
\Lambda_{E}
; cf. (
1.22
):

\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)},1-k}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)})\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)}).

It follows from the fundamental relation (
1.19
) that

\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)},0}=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell_{i}\right)\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)},1-k}.

Let us set

L_{p,\eta}^{\mathrm{imp}}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}}):=\left<\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},X\left(c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)},1-k}(\widetilde{\eta}_{x_{0}})^{\iota}\right)\right>_{x_{0}}\,.

Then
L_{p,\eta}^{\mathrm{imp}}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}})\in\Lambda_{E}
,
and the first assertion follows from the bilinearity of
\left<\,,\,\right>_{x_{0}}.
The second assertion is its immediate consequence.
∎

Corollary 2.13
.

For any integer
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and any character
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
of finite order,

L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho\chi^{j})=0.

2.2.5.
Secondary
p
-adic
L
-function

In the spirit of Bellaïche’s approach and in view of Proposition
2.12
(ii), we introduce the following secondary
p
-adic
L
-function.

Definition 2.14
.

The function

L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0}):=\left.\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial X}L_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z})\right)\right|_{X=0}

is called the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function attached to
\mathbf{z}.

By definition,

(2.27)

L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})=\left.\left<\mathbf{z},c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D},0}(\widetilde{\eta})^{\iota}\right>\right|_{X=0}\,.

We explain how the constructions above are related to the scenario studied in Section
2.1
. Recall that we have set
V:=V_{x_{0}},

\mathbf{D}:=\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}},

\widetilde{V}:=V_{k},

\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}:=\mathbf{D}_{k}
, and considered

(\widetilde{V},\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
as a deformation of
(V,\mathbf{D}).
Let us also set
V^{\prime}:=V^{\prime}_{x_{0}}
. With these identifications in mind, the pairing (
2.26
) then reads

(2.28)

\left<\,,\,\right>_{k}\,:\,\left(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}^{\prime}(1))\right)\otimes\left(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V})\right)^{\iota}\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\,.

Then,

L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})=\left<\mathbf{z}_{k},c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}},0}(\eta_{k})^{\iota}\right>_{k}\,.

Our main result concerning the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function
L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})
relates its special values in the range
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
to the special values of the
p
-adic
L
-function associated to the slope-
0
eigenvalue
\beta
. Throughout §
2.2.5
, we assume that
(\widetilde{V},\widetilde{D})
satisfies the condition (
GP)
).

Definition 2.15
.

We define the slope zero one variable
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{p,\kappa_{0}(\eta)}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}})
as

(2.29)

L_{p,\kappa_{0}(\eta)}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}}):=\left<\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},c\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}_{\beta,0}\left(\kappa_{0}(\eta_{k})\otimes(1+\pi)\right)^{\iota}\right>_{x_{0}}

where the map
\kappa_{0}
is given as in Proposition
2.4
and
the map
\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}_{\beta,0}
is defined by (
2.7
).

As usual, for a character
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
of finite order and
j\in\mathbb{Z},
we put

L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho\chi^{j}):=\rho\chi^{j}\circ L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0})\,,\qquad L_{p,\kappa_{0}(\eta)}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},\rho\chi^{j}):=\rho\chi^{j}\circ L_{p,\kappa_{0}(\eta)}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}}).

The following assertion should be compared to the discussion in
[
Bel12
, §4.4]
.

Theorem 2.16
.

Let
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
be a finite character of
conductor
p^{n}.
Then for each integer
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
we have

L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho\chi^{j})=\left(\frac{b(\rho,j)}{a(\rho,j)}\right)^{n}L_{p,\kappa_{0}(\eta)}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},\rho\chi^{j}),

where
a(\rho,j)
and
b(\rho,j)
are given in (
2.9
).

Proof.

For any finite character
\rho
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have a canonical pairing

\left(\,,\,\right)_{\rho\chi^{j}}\,:\,H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}^{\prime}(\rho^{-1}\chi^{1-j}))\otimes H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\widetilde{V}(\rho\chi^{j}))\longrightarrow E\,.

Let us set
\eta[j]:=t^{-j}\eta\otimes\chi^{j}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}(\chi^{j}))
and
\widetilde{\eta}=\eta_{k}\otimes(1+\pi).
Formula (
1.8
) yields

L^{[1]}_{p,\eta}(\mathbf{z},x_{0},\rho\chi^{j})=(\rho\chi^{j})\circ\left<\mathbf{z}_{k},c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}},0}({\widetilde{\eta}_{k}})^{\iota}\right>_{k}=(-1)^{j}\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{-j}(\mathbf{z}_{k})^{(\rho)},c\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho^{-1})}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j})}\bigl({\widetilde{\eta}_{k}[j]}\bigr)^{\iota}\right)_{\rho\chi^{j}}\,,

and

\displaystyle L_{p,\kappa_{0}(\eta)}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},\rho\chi^{j})

\displaystyle=(\rho\chi^{j})\circ\left<\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}},c\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}_{\beta,0}\left({\kappa_{0}(\widetilde{\eta}_{k}})\right)^{\iota}\right>_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j}\left<\mathrm{Tw}_{-j}(\mathbf{z}_{x_{0}})^{(\rho)},c\circ\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho^{-1})}_{\beta,j,n}\left({\kappa_{j}\bigl(\widetilde{\eta}_{k}[j]}\bigr)\right)^{\iota}\right>_{\rho\chi^{j}}.

By Proposition
2.6
,

a(\rho,j)^{n}\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho^{-1})}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j})}({\widetilde{\eta}_{k}[j]})=b(\rho,j)^{n}\,\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}^{(\rho^{-1})}_{\beta,j,n}\left({\kappa_{j}(\widetilde{\eta}_{k}[j]})\right).

The proof of our proposition follows from these identities.
∎

2.3.
Modular symbols and
p
-adic
L
-functions

In the present subsection, we review the constructions of
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to eigenforms via the theory of modular symbols.

For any module
M
over an unspecified Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}
and any eigenform
f
, we denote by
M[f]
(resp. by
M_{(f)}
) the
f
-isotypic eigenspace (resp. the generalized
f
-eigenspace) in
M
.

2.3.1.
The motive attached to
f

Let
Y_{1}(N)
denote the modular curve associated to the congruence subgroup
\Gamma_{1}(N).

We denote by
\lambda\,:\,\mathcal{E}_{N}\rightarrow Y_{1}(N)
the universal elliptic curve over
Y_{1}(N)
and set
\omega:=\lambda_{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}/Y_{1}(N)}.

Let
f=\underset{n=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}a_{n}q^{n}
be a modular form of weight
k
, level
N
and nebentypus
\varepsilon_{f}.

The motive
\mathcal{M}(f)
associated to
f
has the following explicit description in terms
of realizations:

•

The Betti realization

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f)
of
\mathcal{M}(f)
is the
f
-isotypic eigenspace

H^{1}\left(Y_{1}(N)(\mathbb{C}),\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}\left(R^{1}\lambda_{*}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathbb{Q}}\right)\right)[f]\subset H^{1}\left(Y_{1}(N)(\mathbb{C}),\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}\left(R^{1}\lambda_{*}(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathbb{Q}}\right)\right).

•

The de Rham realization

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{dR}}(f)
of
\mathcal{M}(f)
is the
f
-isotypic eigenspace in

H^{0}\left(Y_{1}(N),\Omega^{1}_{Y_{1}(N)}\otimes\omega^{k-2}\right)\oplus\overline{H^{0}\left(Y_{1}(N),\Omega^{1}_{Y_{1}(N)}\otimes\omega^{k-2}\right)}.

•

For each prime number
p
, the
p
-adic realization

\mathcal{M}_{p}(f)
of
\mathcal{M}(f)
is the
f
-isotypic component

H^{1}_{\mathrm{{\acute{e}}t,c}}\left(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}\left(R^{1}\lambda_{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\right)[f]\subset H^{1}_{\mathrm{{\acute{e}}t,c}}\left(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathrm{Sym}^{k-2}\left(R^{1}\lambda_{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\right)\right).

The space of cuspidal forms
S_{k}(N)
injects into
H^{0}(Y_{1}(N),\Omega^{1}_{Y_{1}(N)}\otimes\omega^{k-2})
via the Eichler–Shimura map, and we consider
S_{k}(N)[f]
as a subspace
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{dR}}(f)
through this injection.

Let
f^{*}:=\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\overline{a}_{n}q^{n}
denote the dual modular form. There exists a canonical pairing

\mathcal{M}(f^{*})\otimes\mathcal{M}(f)\longrightarrow\mathbb{Q}(k-1)

which induces pairings between the realizations of
\mathcal{M}(f^{*})
and
\mathcal{M}(f).
The complex conjugation acts on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f)
, and we fix an eigenbasis

\xi:=\{\xi_{+},\xi_{-}\},\qquad\xi_{\pm}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f)^{\pm}

for its action.

Let
\xi^{*}:=\{\xi^{*}_{+},\xi^{*}_{-}\}
denote the dual basis of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f^{*}).

The comparison isomorphism

\mathrm{comp}_{\mathrm{dR},\infty}\,:\,M_{\mathrm{dR}}(f^{*})\otimes\mathbb{C}\simeq M_{\mathrm{B}}(f^{*})\otimes\mathbb{C}

gives rise to complex periods
\Omega^{\pm}_{f}
defined by

\mathrm{comp}_{\mathrm{dR},\infty}(f^{*})=\Omega_{f}^{+}\xi^{*}_{+}+\Omega_{f}^{-}\xi^{*}_{-}.

We remark that this normalization of
\Omega^{\pm}_{f}
agrees with
[
Kat04
]
.

2.3.2.
p
-adic
L
-functions

Recall that we have fixed a prime number
p\nmid N.
The Hecke polynomial

X^{2}-a_{p}X+\varepsilon_{f}(p)p^{k-1}
has two nonzero roots which we shall denote by
\alpha

and
\beta
. We will assume that
\alpha\neq\beta
(this conjecturally always holds). Without loss of generality, we may and will assume that the roots are ordered in such a way that

v_{p}(\alpha)\geqslant v_{p}(\beta).

Denote by

f_{\alpha}(q):=f(q)-\beta f(q^{p}),\qquad f_{\beta}(q):=f(q)-\alpha f(q^{p})

the stabilizations of
f
with respect to
\alpha
and
\beta.

Put
\Gamma_{p}:=\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)
. For a field
F
, we let
\mathscr{P}_{k-2}(F)
be the space of polynomials over
F
of degree
\leqslant k-2
equipped with the canonical left action of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. We denote by
\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(F)
the
F
-linear dual of
\mathscr{P}_{k-2}(F)
. Recall the space of complex-valued modular symbols

\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(\mathbb{C})):={\mathrm{Hom}}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathrm{Div}^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q})),\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(\mathbb{C}))\,.

The complex conjugation acts on this space, and we will denote by
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}^{\pm}(\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(\mathbb{C}))
the eigenspaces for this action.

Recall that the Eichler–Shimura integrals of
f_{\alpha}

2
2

2

The modular symbols below depend on the choice of the root
\alpha
, but we suppress that dependence from our notation.

give rise to a pair of complex modular symbols

\varphi_{\infty}^{\pm}\in\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(\mathbb{C}))[f_{\alpha}].

It follows from the theorem of Shimura and Manin that the modular symbols

\displaystyle\varphi_{\xi}^{+}:=\frac{(2\pi i)^{k-1}}{2\Omega_{f}^{(-1)^{k}}}\cdot{\varphi_{\infty}^{+}},

\displaystyle\varphi_{\xi}^{-}:=-\frac{(2\pi i)^{k-1}}{2\Omega_{f}^{(-1)^{k-1}}}\cdot{\varphi_{\infty}^{-}}

are rational and therefore, can be considered as elements of
\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(E))[f_{\alpha}]
for a sufficiently large extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Let
\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E)
denote the space of overconvergent distributions of weight
k-2

(cf.
[
PS13
,
Bel12
]
).
The evaluation of distributions on polynomials of degree
\leqslant k-2
induces
a Hecke equivariant projection map

(2.30)

\rho_{k-2}\,:\,\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))\longrightarrow\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(E))\,.

Let us first assume that
f_{\alpha}
does not have critical slope, i.e.
v_{p}(\alpha)<k-1
. By Stevens’ control theorem
[
PS13
, Theorem 5.4]
, the map (
2.30
) induces an isomorphism on the slope
<k-1
subspaces

\rho_{k-2}\,:\,\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))^{<k-1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(E))^{<k-1}.

Therefore, there exists a unique pair of overconvergent modular symbols
\Phi_{\xi}^{\pm}\in\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}_{k-2}^{\dagger}(E))^{<k-1}
such that

\rho_{k-2}(\Phi_{\xi}^{\pm})=\varphi_{\xi}^{\pm}.

We define the
p
-adic distributions

\mu^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\xi}:=\Phi_{\xi}^{\pm}((\infty)-(0)).

We recall that to each distribution
\mu
on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{*}
, one can associate its Amice transform in the ring
\mathscr{R}_{E}^{+}
of power series in
\pi
converging on the open unit disc:

\mathscr{A}_{\mu}(\pi):=\int(1+\pi)^{x}d\mu(x)\in(\mathscr{R}_{E}^{+})^{\psi=0},

where
\psi
denotes the operator (
1.2
). Since
(\mathscr{R}_{E}^{+})^{\psi=0}=\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\cdot(1+\pi),
there exists
a unique element
\mathrm{Mel}(\mu)\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
such that

\mathscr{A}_{\mu}(\pi)=\mathrm{Mel}(\mu)\cdot(1+\pi).

We call
\mathrm{Mel}(\mu)
the Mellin transform of
\mu.

The
p
-adic
L
-function associated to the distribution

(2.31)

\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}:=\mu^{+}_{\mathrm{S},\xi}+\mu^{-}_{\mathrm{S},\xi}

is defined as the Mellin transform of
x^{-1}\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}
:

(2.32)

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi):=\mathrm{Mel}(x^{-1}\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}).

A standard computation with modular symbols shows that
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)

coincides with the classical
p
-adic
L
-function of Manin–Vishik. In particular, it satisfies the following interpolation property: for any
finite character
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
, we have

(2.33)

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi,\rho\chi^{j})=(j-1)!\cdot e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)\cdot\frac{L(f,\rho^{-1},j)}{(2\pi i)^{j+1-k}\cdot\Omega_{f}^{\pm}},\qquad 1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1,

where

(2.34)

e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)=\begin{cases}\left(1-\frac{p^{j-1}}{\alpha}\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{\beta}{p^{j}}\right)&\textrm{if $\rho=\mathds{1}$},\\
\frac{p^{nj}}{\alpha^{n}\tau(\rho^{-1})}&\textrm{if $\rho$ has conductor $p^{n}.$}\end{cases}

Let us now assume that
v_{p}(\alpha)=k-1
and that
f_{\alpha}
is non-
\theta
-critical, namely that it is not in the image of the
\theta
-operator
\theta_{k}:=\left(\frac{d}{dq}\right)^{k-1}
. Pollack and Stevens
[
PS13
]
proved that in this case the generalized eigenspace
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}
is one-dimensional, and therefore the projection map

\rho_{k-2}\,:\,\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathscr{V}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}

is an isomorphism. This shows, as in the non-critical-slope case, that there exist unique lifts
\Phi_{\xi}^{\pm}
of the pair of modular symbols
\phi^{\pm}_{\infty}
. The distribution
\mu_{S,\xi}
and the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{{\mathrm{S}},\alpha}(f,\xi)
are then defined via the equations (
2.31
) and (
2.32
), respectively.

2.3.3.
Modular symbols in families and
p
-adic
L
-functions on the eigencurve

Let
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
denote the cuspidal eigencurve of level
N

constructed with the Hecke operators
U_{p}
,
T_{\ell}
and
\left<\ell\right>
for

(\ell,Np)=1.
Let
x_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
denote the point on

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
which corresponds to the stabilisation
f_{\alpha}^{*}
of
f^{*}.

We will denote by
\mathcal{X}
a sufficiently small affinoid disc centered at
x_{0}

and by
\mathcal{W}
an affinoid disc centered at
k
in the weight space. Shrinking these discs, one can always assume that the weight map
w\,:\,\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}
is surjective. Moreover it is étale if
x_{0}
is not
\theta
-critical (cf.
[
Bel12
, Proposition 2.11]
). For any
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E),
we denote by

f_{x}
the associated eigenform and define the function
\alpha(x)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
by
U_{p}(f_{x})=\alpha(x)f_{x}.
In particular,
f_{x_{0}}=f_{\alpha}
and
\alpha(x_{0})=\alpha.

Let
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))
denote the module of

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-valued overconvergent modular symbols
[
Bel12
]
(see also
[
BB22
, §5]
). The operator
U_{p}
acts on
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))

and we denote by
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))^{\leqslant\nu}
the submodule
on which
U_{p}
acts with slope at most
\nu\in\mathbb{R}
. The complex conjugation acts on

\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))^{\leqslant\nu}
and we have a canonical decomposition into the direct sum of the corresponding eigenspaces:

\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))^{\leqslant\nu}=\mathrm{Symb}^{+}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))^{\leqslant\nu}\oplus\mathrm{Symb}^{-}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))^{\leqslant\nu}.

Fix a real number
\nu>k_{0}-1
. Then for a sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
we have well defined
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules

\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathcal{X})^{\leqslant\nu}:=\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}^{\pm}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))^{\leqslant\nu}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{W},\nu}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}},

where
\mathbb{T}^{\pm}_{\mathcal{W},\nu}
denote the Hecke algebra acting on
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}^{\pm}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}(\mathcal{W}))^{\leqslant\nu}.

Then:

•

\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathcal{X})^{\leqslant\nu}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
of rank one (see
[
Bel12
, §4.2.1]
).

•

The natural projection

(2.35)

\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathcal{X})^{\leqslant\nu}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}

is surjective. See
[
Bel12
, Theorem 3.10]
for a proof of this claim. When
k=2
, one further needs to input from Theorem 3.30(iii) in op. cit., which tells us that the support of the cokernel of the map (
2.35
) is necessarily Eisenstein (therefore trivial, since
f_{\alpha}
is cuspidal).

Let us first assume that
x_{0}
is not
\theta
-critical. Choose any lifts

\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}^{\pm}\in\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathcal{X})^{\leqslant\nu}
of

\Phi_{\xi}^{\pm}\in\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k_{0}-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}

and set

\mu^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\xi,\mathcal{X}}:=\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}^{\pm}((\infty)-(0)),\qquad\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi,\mathcal{X}}:=\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi,\mathcal{X}}^{+}+\mu^{-}_{\mathrm{S},\xi,\mathcal{X}}.

Stevens’ two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function is defined on setting

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}):=\mathrm{Mel}\left(x^{-1}\mu^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\xi,\mathcal{X}}\right)\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma).

For any
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),
we denote by
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}},x)

the specialization of
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
at
x.
In particular,

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}},x_{0})=L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi).

More generally, let
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)
be a classical point corresponding to the stabilization of a cuspidal eigenform
f^{\circ}_{x}.
Let us choose nonzero elements

\xi_{x,\pm}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f_{x}^{\circ})^{\pm}
. Then,

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}},x)=C_{x}\cdot L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f_{x}^{\circ},\xi_{x})

for some
C_{x}\in E
.

2.3.4.
The
\theta
-critical scenario

We now discuss the
\theta
-critical case, which is the main focus of the present article. To that end, we assume that
f_{\alpha}
belongs to the image of
\theta_{k}
. Let us set
X:=U_{p}-\alpha.
Then we can consider
X
as a function on
\mathcal{X}
,
and the generalized eigenspace
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}

carries a natural
E[X]
-module structure. In
[
Bel12
]
, Bellaïche proves the following results:

1.

The weight map
w\,:\,\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}
is ramified at
x_{0}.
Namely,
shrinking
\mathcal{X}
if necessary, one can assume that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}[X]/(X^{e}-Y)
with
e\geqslant 2
. See
[
Bel12
, Theorem 4]
.

2.

The generalized eigenspace
\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}^{\pm}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}
is isomorphic to
E[X]/(X^{e})
as
E[X]
-modules.

3.

The
E
-vector space
\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))[f_{\alpha}]\subset\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}
is 1-dimensional.

4.

The map
\rho_{k-2}
given as in (
2.30
) is surjective and

\rho_{k-2}\left(\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))[f_{\alpha}]\right)=0.

Until the end of this paper, we shall assume that
e=2
, so that

(2.36)

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}[X]/(X^{2}-Y).

We remark that a well-known conjecture of Greenberg asserts that
f
has CM if and only if the associated
p
-adic representation of the local Galois group
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
decomposes into the direct sum of two characters. This conjecture implies, thanks to a deep result due to Breuil and Emerton
[
BE10
, Theorem 1.1.3]
, that any
\theta
-critical eigenform has CM. Moreover, if the critical point
x_{0}
has CM (in the sense that the corresponding eigenform
f_{\alpha}
has CM), then it follows from a conjecture of Jannsen formulated in
[
Jan89
]
that
e=2
(cf.
[
Bel
]
). Therefore, it is expected that (
2.36
) always holds true.

We are in the scenario of Section
2.2.1
since we assume
e=2
. In the what follows, we fix

\bullet

modular symbols
\Phi^{\pm}_{\xi}\in\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))_{(f_{\alpha})}
such that
\rho_{k-2}(\Phi^{\pm}_{\xi})=\varphi^{\pm}_{\xi}
(where
\varphi^{\pm}_{\xi}
are given as in §
2.3.2
) ,

\bullet

modular symbols

\Phi^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}\in\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathcal{X})^{\leqslant\nu}

which are lifts of
\Phi^{\pm}_{\xi}
under the map (
2.35
).

Let us put
\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}:=\Phi^{+}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}+\Phi^{-}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}
and consider

\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}:=1\otimes X\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}+X\otimes\Phi_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathcal{X})^{\leqslant\nu}.

Following Bellaïche, we define the two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function

L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)
as

L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})=\mathrm{Mel}(x^{-1}\widetilde{\mu}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}),\qquad\widetilde{\mu}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi}((\infty)-(0)).

As in the non-critical case, we denote by
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}},x)
the specialization of
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
at
x\in\mathcal{X}(E).

Suppose that
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)
corresponds to the
p
-stabilization
f_{x}
of some eigenform
f_{x}^{\circ}
of level
N
. It follows from Lemma
2.9
that the specialization map (
2.18
) sends
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}
to a modular symbol

\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,x}\in\mathrm{Symb}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}_{w(x)-2}^{\dagger}(E))[f_{x}].

This implies that
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
interpolates

p
-adic
L
-functions of one variable on
\mathcal{X}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
. In more precise terms, we have

L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}},x)=C_{x}\cdot L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha(x)}(f_{x}^{\circ},\xi_{x}),\qquad x\neq x_{0},

for some constant
C_{x}.

Let us set

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi):=L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}},x_{0})\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).

Proposition 2.17
.

The following assertions are valid.

The function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
does not depend on the choice of
the lift
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}.
Moreover

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)=\mathrm{Mel}(x^{-1}\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}^{[0]}),\qquad\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}^{[0]}:=\Phi^{[0]}_{\xi}((\infty)-(0)),

where
\Phi^{[0]}_{\xi}:=X\Phi_{\xi}
and
\Phi_{\xi}=\Phi_{\xi}^{+}+\Phi_{\xi}^{-}
with
\Phi_{\xi}^{\pm}
as above.

The function

L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
can be written in the form

L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell_{i}^{\iota}\right)\cdot L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi),

where
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)\in\Lambda_{E}
is bounded.
In particular, for any finite character
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
we have

L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{r})=0,\qquad 1\leqslant r\leqslant k-1.

Proof.

The proof of the first assertion is straightforward and is omitted here.
The second assertion has been noted in
[
Bel12
, Section 1.4.3]
, we briefly record an explanation here. According to a classical result due to Vishik
[
Viš76
]
, the growth order of a distribution associated to an overconvergent modular eigensymbol on which
U_{p}
acts with slope
\nu
is at most
\nu
. This shows that the distribution
\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}^{[0]}
has growth order bounded from above by
k-1
. Since each logarithmic factor
\ell_{i}
is the Mellin transform of a distribution with growth order
1
, it follows that
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
is indeed bounded.
∎

2.3.5.
The infinitesimal thickening of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions

We continue to work in the
\theta
-critical scenario (as in §
2.3.4
) and maintain previous notation and conventions. Let us set

(2.37)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi}&:=1\otimes X\Phi_{\xi}+X\otimes\Phi_{\xi}\\
&=1\otimes\Phi^{[0]}_{\xi}+X\otimes\Phi_{\xi}\in E[X]/(X^{2})\otimes_{E}\mathrm{Symb}^{\pm}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))\end{aligned}

and

L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi}):=\mathrm{Mel}(x^{-1}\widetilde{\mu}_{\xi}),\qquad\widetilde{\mu}_{\xi}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi}((\infty)-(0))\,.

We remark that

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi})=L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})\pmod{X^{2}}\,.

Lemma 2.18
.

Suppose that
\Psi_{\xi}^{\pm}
are another pair of lifts of the modular symbols
\varphi^{\pm}_{\xi}
and let us put
\Psi_{\xi}=\Psi^{+}_{\xi}+\Psi^{-}_{\xi}
. Then,

L^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Psi}_{\xi})=u^{\pm}(X)\,\widetilde{L}^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi})

for some
u^{\pm}(X)\in 1+EX
.

Proof.

For
\star\in\{+,-\},

the symbol
X\Phi^{\star}_{\xi}
generates

\mathrm{Symb}^{\star}_{\Gamma_{p}}(\mathbb{D}^{\dagger}_{k-2}(E))[f_{\alpha}],
and therefore

\Psi^{\star}_{\xi}=(1+a_{\star}X)\Phi^{\star}_{\xi}

for some
a_{\star}\in E
.
A direct computation then shows that

\widetilde{\Psi}^{\star}_{\xi}=(1+a_{\star}X\otimes 1)\widetilde{\Phi}^{\star}_{\xi}.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

When the choice of
\Phi_{\xi}
(which in turn determines
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi}
, cf. (
2.37
)) is not specified, we will write
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
in place of
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi})
.

Observe that we can write
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
in the form

(2.38)

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)=L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)+XL^{[1]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi),\qquad L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\,,

and for that reason, we refer to
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
as the infinitesimal thickening of
L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
.

Proposition 2.19
.

The function
L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
verifies the following interpolation property: There exists a constant
C_{\mathrm{S}}\in E^{\times}
such that

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi,\rho\chi^{j})=C_{\mathrm{S}}\cdot{(j-1)!}\,e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)\,\,\frac{L(f,\rho^{-1},j)}{(2\pi i)^{j+1-k}\Omega_{f}^{\pm}}\qquad\qquad\textrm{\,if\, $\rho(-1)=\mp(-1)^{j}\,,$}

where
e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)
is given as in (
2.34
).

Proof.

See
[
Bel12
]
.
∎

2.4.
Euler systems and
p
-adic
L
-functions

In this section, we review the Perrin-Riou-style construction of
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to
p
-stabilized normalized eigenforms and compare these
p
-adic
L
-functions to those constructed using modular symbols (cf. §
2.3
). The scenario when the slope of the
p
-stabilized eigenform is non-critical, which we review in §
2.4.1
and §
2.4.3
, is covered by Kato’s fundamental work
[
Kat04
]
. The sought-after comparison in the setting when the
p
-stabilized eigenform is of critical slope but non-
\theta
-critical is established in
[
BB22
, Proposition 7.3]
and recorded as part of Proposition
2.20
below.

The scenario when the
p
-stabilized eigenform is
\theta
-critical is much more involved and the comparison of two different constructions of the
p
-adic
L
-functions in this set-up is one of the main results of the present article (cf. §
2.4.4
and §
2.4.5
).

2.4.1.
Kato’s
p
-adic
L
-functions

In this section, we maintain the notation and conventions of §
2.2
and §
2.3
. Let
f
denote a normalized newform of weight
k
and level
N
. Fix an odd prime
p
such that
p\nmid N.
To simplify notation, we set

V_{f}:=\mathcal{M}_{p}(f)\qquad\textrm{(the $p$-adic realization of $\mathcal{M}(f)$).}

Since
p\nmid N,
the
p
-adic Galois representation
V_{f}
is crystalline at
p
. We have the canonical pairings

(2.39)

\displaystyle V_{f^{*}}\otimes_{E}V_{f}\longrightarrow E(\chi^{1-k}),

\displaystyle[\,,\,]\,:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}})\times{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})\longrightarrow{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(E(\chi^{1-k}))\simeq E

induced from Poincaré duality. The embedding of
S_{k}(N)[f]
in
M_{\mathrm{dR}}(f)
fixes a canonical element

\omega_{f}\in\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})
via the comparison isomorphisms.

In
[
Kat04
]
, Kato gave the construction of the Beilinson–Kato Euler systems, which live in the first Galois cohomology of
V_{f^{*}}
. Let us fix a basis
\xi
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f)
and denote by
\xi^{*}

the dual basis of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f^{*}).

We denote by

\mathbf{z}(f^{*},\xi^{*}):=\mathbf{z}^{+}(f^{*},\xi^{*})+\mathbf{z}^{-}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f^{*}}(k))

the Beilinson–Kato element, which comes naturally associated to the class
\xi^{*}
. We remark that our
\mathbf{z}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
is denoted by
\mathbf{z}_{\gamma}^{(p)}(f^{*})(k)
in
[
Kat04
]
. The Beilinson–Kato elements play a central role in §
2.4
.

Recall that
\alpha
and
\beta
stands for the Hecke eigenvalues of
f
at
p
. Then,
\alpha^{*}=p^{k-1}/\beta
and
\beta^{*}=p^{k-1}/\alpha
are the Hecke eigenvalues
of
f^{*}
at
p
. We recall that we assume
v_{p}(\beta)\leqslant v_{p}(\alpha)
and therefore
v_{p}(\beta^{*})\leqslant v_{p}(\alpha^{*})
.

Until the end of §
2.4.1
, we assume that the
p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}
is not
\theta
-critical, so that
\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})\cap{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})^{\varphi=\alpha}=\{0\}
. This is clear in the non-critical-slope scenario (i.e. when
v_{p}(\alpha)<k-1
) and it follows from a deep result of Breuil and Emerton
[
BE10
]
in the scenario when
v_{p}(\alpha)=k-1
but
f_{\alpha}
is not
\theta
-critical.

As a result, there exists a unique element
\eta_{f}^{\alpha}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})^{\varphi=\alpha}
such that

\left[\omega_{f^{*}},\eta_{f}^{\alpha}\right]=1.

The first pairing in (
2.39
) gives rise to the pairing

V_{f^{*}}(k)\otimes V_{f}\longrightarrow E(\chi)\,,

and we have the following induced pairings on the
p
-local Iwasawa cohomology:

\bigl<\,,\,\bigr>_{V_{f}}\,:\,\left(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda[1/p]}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f^{*}}(k))\right)\times\left(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\otimes_{\Lambda[1/p]}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f})^{\iota}\right)\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).

Let us consider the Perrin-Riou-style
p
-adic
L
-function

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi):=\left<\textup{res}_{p}(\mathbf{z}(f^{*},\xi^{*})),c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f},0}\left(\widetilde{\eta_{f}^{\alpha}}\right)^{\iota}\right>_{V_{f}}\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma),\qquad\widetilde{\eta_{f}^{\alpha}}=\eta_{f}^{\alpha}\otimes(1+\pi),

which can be realized as the Mellin transform of a distribution
\mu_{\mathrm{K},\xi}
with growth order
v_{p}(\alpha)
. It follows from Kato’s explicit reciprocity laws that

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
also verifies the interpolation property (
2.33
).

Proposition 2.20
.

If
f_{\alpha}
is not
\theta
-critical, then

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)=L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)\,.

Equivalently,

\mu_{\mathrm{K},\xi}=x^{-1}\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}.

Proof.

In the case
v_{p}(\alpha)<k-1
(or equivalently, when
v_{p}(\alpha^{*})<k-1
), this proposition is proved in
[
Kat04
]
. Namely, since both distributions
\mu_{\mathrm{K},\xi}
and
\mu_{\mathrm{S},\xi}
have growth order strictly smaller than
k-1
, this follows from the fact that

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi,\rho\chi^{r})=L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi,\rho\chi^{r})

for all
1\leqslant r\leqslant k-1
and
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
of finite order.

The case
v_{p}(\alpha)=k-1
follows from
[
BB22
, Proposition 7.3]
. The proof relies on the interpolation of Beilinson-Kato elements in families. More precisely, let us put

(2.40)

\mathcal{E}_{N}(f):=\underset{\ell\mid N}{\prod}(1-a_{\ell}\sigma_{\ell}^{-1})\in\Lambda,

where
\sigma_{\ell}\in\Gamma
is the unique element such that
\chi(\sigma_{\ell})=\ell
. Set
\mathbf{z}:=\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)\cdot\mathbf{z}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
and define

L(\mathbf{z}):=\left<\textup{res}_{p}(\mathbf{z}),c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f},0}\left(\widetilde{\eta_{f}^{\alpha}}\right)\right>_{V_{f}}.

Proposition 7.3 and Formula (68) in op. cit. imply that

L(\mathbf{z})=\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi).

Since
L(\mathbf{z})=\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi),
this concludes the proof of our proposition.
∎

Let us set
\mathcal{E}_{N}(f;\rho\chi^{r}):=\rho\chi^{r}\circ\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)
. Then,

(2.41)

\mathcal{E}_{N}(f;\rho\chi^{r})=\underset{\ell\mid N}{\prod}(1-a_{\ell}\rho(\sigma_{\ell})^{-1}\ell^{-r}).

2.4.2.
Critical points on the eigencurve

In this section, we assume that
f_{\alpha}
is
\theta
-critical. The
p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}
of
f
with respect to
\alpha
corresponds to a point on
the cuspidal eigencurve
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
, which we denote by
x_{0}
and will also say that
x_{0}
is
\theta
-critical (or sometimes simply
critical
). Our objective is to use the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic Beilinson–Kato element we have introduced in
[
BB22
, Definition 6.14]
to give an “étale” construction of Bellaïche’s
p
-adic
L
-function.

We fix a sufficiently small neighborhood
\mathcal{X}
of
x_{0}
and denote by
w\,:\,\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}
the weight map. As in Section
2.3.4
, we assume
that
e=2
and therefore
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}[X]/(X^{2}-Y).

For each classical crystalline point

x\in\mathcal{X}(E),
we denote by
f_{x}
the specialization of
\mathbf{f}
at
x
and
by
f_{x}^{\circ}
the newform of level prime to
p
whose
p
-stabilization is
f_{x}
. In particular,
f_{x_{0}}^{\circ}=f
.

Recall that the overconvergent étale cohomology provides us with a family of

p
-adic Galois representations
V_{\mathcal{X}}
and
V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
of rank
2
over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}

(given as in
[
BB22
]
, Equation (51)), that interpolate Deligne’s representations (homological and cohomological, respectively) at classical points. In particular,
V_{x_{0}}\simeq V_{f}
.

Proposition 2.21
.

The
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation
V_{\mathcal{X}}
is equipped with a triangulation satisfying the conditions (
C1)
)–(
C3)
).

Recall that
e=2
. Then the pairing (
0.5
)

(\,,\,)\,:\,V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\otimes V_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}

satisfies the condition (
Adj)
).

The pairing
(\,,\,)
is perfect.

We remark that we do not need (iii) in the present work, but we have included it for the sake of completeness.

Proof.

The properties (
C1)
) and (
C2)
) follow from the results of Faltings and Saito on the
p
-local properties of
p
-adic representations arising from modular forms.

For the first half of the property (
C3)
), we note that
\operatorname{Fil}^{w(x)-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x})\cap{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x})^{\varphi=\alpha(x)}\neq\{0\}
at any non-critical classical point
x
, as a result of the weak admissibility of
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x})
.
If
x=x_{0}
, the restriction of the representation
V_{x_{0}}=V_{f}
to the decomposition group at
p
is reducible (cf.
[
BE10
]
, Theorem 1.1.3). Therefore,
V_{x_{0}}=V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)}\oplus V_{x_{0}}^{(\beta)},
where
V_{x_{0}}^{(\alpha)}
and
V_{x_{0}}^{(\beta)}
have Hodge–Tate weights
-(k-1)
and
0
, respectively. This implies that

\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x_{0}})={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{st}}(V_{x_{0}})^{\varphi=\alpha}
, which is the second part of the property (
C3)
).

This portion follows from the standard properties of the Atkin–Lehner operator
U_{p}
, cf.
[
BB22
, Proposition 6.6(iii)]
.

Consider the natural morphism

\mathscr{F}:\,V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}(V_{\mathcal{X}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}})\,,\qquad v\xrightarrow{\,\mathscr{F}\,}(w\mapsto(v,w))\,,\quad v\in V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},w\in V_{\mathcal{X}}\,.

Note that both the source and the target of the morphism
\mathscr{F}
are free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-modules of rank
4
.

Since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
is a PID, it follows that
\ker(\mathscr{F})
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-module. Moreover, for any classical weight
w\in\mathcal{W}^{\rm cl}(E)
, we have an exact sequence

\ker(\mathscr{F})_{w}\longrightarrow V_{w}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\,\mathscr{F}_{w}}{\rm Hom}_{E}(V_{w},E)\,,

where for any
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-module
M
, we write as before
M_{w}
to denote
M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}},w}E
. When
w>k
, it follows from control theorems utilized as in the proof of
[
BB22
, Theorem 5.6]
and the perfectness of Poincaré duality that the map
\mathscr{F}_{w}
is an isomorphism; in particular,
\ker(\mathscr{F})_{w}=\{0\}
. Since
\ker(\mathscr{F})
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-module, we conclude that
\ker(\mathscr{F})=0
. This shows that the pairing
(\,,\,)
is non-degenerate on the left, and a similar argument it is also non-degenerate on the right (therefore, non-degenerate).

We also infer that
{\rm coker}(\mathscr{F})
is a torsion
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-module. Our argument in the preceding paragraph shows that
{\rm coker}(\mathscr{F})
has no support at any classical point
w>k
. As a result, on shrinking
\mathcal{W}
as necessary, we may assume with out loss of generality that
{\rm coker}(\mathscr{F})
has no support away from
k
. To prove that
\mathscr{F}
is an isomorphism (and therefore, that the pairing
(\,,\,)
is perfect as required), it suffices to show that
{\rm coker}(\mathscr{F})_{k}=\{0\}
.

Let us consider the exact sequence

V_{k}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\,\mathscr{F}_{k}}{\rm Hom}_{E}(V_{k},E)\longrightarrow{\rm coker}(\mathscr{F})_{k}\longrightarrow 0\,,

and choose an
E[X]/(X^{2})
-module bases
\{E_{1},E_{2}\}
and
\{E_{1}^{\prime},E_{2}^{\prime}\}
of
V_{k}
and
V_{k}^{\prime}
, respectively. Let us denote by
e_{i}^{\prime}\in V_{x_{0}}=V^{\prime}_{k}/(X)
the image of
E_{i}^{\prime}
, so that
\{e_{1}^{\prime},e_{2}^{\prime}\}
is an
E
-basis of
V_{x_{0}}^{\prime}\simeq V_{f}^{\prime}
. Let us similarly define the
E
-basis
\{e_{1},e_{2}\}
of
V_{x_{0}}\simeq V_{f}
. Recall that
XV_{k}^{\prime}\simeq V_{f}^{\prime}
and
XV_{k}\simeq V_{f}
, and we shall treat
\{XE_{1}^{\prime},XE_{2}^{\prime}\}
and
\{XE_{1},XE_{2}\}
as
E
-bases of
V_{f}^{\prime}
and
V_{f}
, respectively, via these isomorphism.

It follows from Part (ii) that
(E_{i}^{\prime},XE_{j})_{k}=(e_{i}^{\prime},XE_{j})_{f}
and
(XE_{i}^{\prime},E_{j})_{k}=(XE_{i}^{\prime},e_{j})_{f}
can be computed in terms of the Poincaré duality pairing
V_{f}^{\prime}\otimes V_{f}\xrightarrow{(\,,\,)_{f}}E
, and that
(XE_{i}^{\prime},XE_{j})=0
. As a result, the matrix of
\mathscr{F}_{k}
(in the ordered
E
-bases
\{XE_{1}^{\prime},XE_{2}^{\prime},E_{1}^{\prime},E_{2}^{\prime}\}
and
\{E_{1},E_{2},XE_{1},XE_{2}\}
of
V_{k}^{\prime}
and
V_{k}
, respectively) is block-diagonal:

\mathscr{F}_{k}\sim\begin{pmatrix}A&\star\\
0&B\end{pmatrix}\,,\qquad A=\begin{pmatrix}(XE_{1}^{\prime},e_{1})_{f}&(XE_{2}^{\prime},e_{1})_{f}\\
(XE_{1}^{\prime},e_{2})_{f}&(XE_{2}^{\prime},e_{2})_{f}\end{pmatrix}\,,B=\begin{pmatrix}(e_{1}^{\prime},XE_{1})_{f}&(e_{2}^{\prime},XE_{1})_{f}\\
(e_{1}^{\prime},XE_{2})_{f}&(e_{2}^{\prime},XE_{2})_{f}\end{pmatrix}\,.

Since
\det A\neq 0\neq\det B
by the perfectness of the Poincaré duality pairing
(\,,\,)_{f}
, it follows that
\mathscr{F}_{k}
is an isomorphism, as desired.
∎

2.4.3.
Slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function

As a reflection of the
eigenspace-transition by differentiation principle
(cf. §
2.1
), one expects to relate the improved critical
p
-adic
L
-function to the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function. We indeed show that this is the case as part of Proposition
2.27
below.

With that purpose in mind, we review the Perrin-Riou style construction of the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function in §
2.4.3
, in a manner that is suitable to our purposes.

In
[
BB22
, Section 6.6.6]
, we gave a construction of an element
3
3

3

This element is denoted by
{\mathbb{BK}}^{[\mathcal{X}]}_{N}(j,\xi)
in
[
BB22
, Section 6.6.6]
.

\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in H^{1}(V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}\,\Lambda^{\iota}(1))

for each basis
\xi=\{\xi_{+},\xi_{-}\}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f)^{\pm}
, interpolating the classical Beilinson–Kato zeta elements that were introduced in
[
Kat04
]
. To be more precise, let us denote by
\mathbf{z}(x,\xi)
the specialization of
\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
at
x\in\mathcal{X}(E)
. Let us write

f_{x}:=\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}a_{n}(x)q^{n}

and define

(2.42)

\mathcal{E}_{N}\,:=\,\underset{\ell\mid N}{\prod}(1-a_{\ell}(x)\sigma_{\ell}^{-1})\in\Lambda_{\chi}.

Note that
\mathcal{E}_{N}(x_{0})=\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)
(cf. (
2.40
)).

The element
\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
has the following interpolation properties:

•

We have

(2.43)

\mathbf{z}(x_{0},\xi)=\mathcal{E}_{N}(x_{0})\,\mathbf{z}(f^{*},\xi^{*}).

•

For any classical point
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)
and any
basis
\xi_{x}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f^{\circ}_{x})
, we have

\mathbf{z}(x,\xi)^{\pm}=C_{x}^{\pm}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{N}(x)^{\pm}\cdot\mathbf{z}((f_{x}^{\circ})^{*},\xi^{*}_{x})^{\pm},\qquad\textrm{for some $C_{x}^{\pm}\in E^{*},$}

where
\mathbf{z}((f_{x}^{\circ})^{*},\xi^{*}_{x})\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{x}^{\prime}(1))
denotes the Beilinson–Kato zeta element associated to the pair
((f_{x}^{\circ})^{*},\xi^{*}_{x})
. Here, we recall from §

that

denotes the newform attached to the
p
-old form
f_{x}
. We remark that we have used the canonical isomorphism

V_{x}^{\prime}(1)\simeq V_{(f_{x}^{\circ})^{*}}(k)\,.

We shall fix canonical bases of
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})
and
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}})
in the
\theta
-critical scenario.
The differential forms associated to
f
and
f^{*}
fix bases
\{\eta_{f}^{\alpha}\}
of
\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\alpha)})
and
\{\eta_{f^{*}}^{\alpha}\}
of
\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}})={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha^{*})})
. Note then that
\eta_{f}^{\alpha}=\omega_{f}
and
\eta_{f^{*}}^{\alpha}=\omega_{f^{*}}
.
We choose
\eta_{f}^{\beta}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\beta)})
and
\eta_{f^{*}}^{\beta}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)})

in such a way that

[\eta_{f}^{\beta},\eta^{\alpha}_{f^{*}}]=1=[\eta_{f}^{\alpha},\eta^{\beta}_{f^{*}}]\,,

[\eta_{f}^{\beta},\eta^{\beta}_{f^{*}}]=0=[\eta_{f}^{\alpha},\eta^{\alpha}_{f^{*}}]\,.

Since
v_{p}(\beta)=0,
the
p
-adic
L
-function

L_{\mathrm{S},\beta}(f,\xi)\in\Lambda_{E}
coincides with the classical Manin–Višik
p
-adic
L
-function associated to the triple
(f,\beta,\xi)
.

We retain the notation of §
2.1
. Until the end of §
2.4.3
, we assume that condition (
GP)
) holds true.
Recall the transition map
\kappa_{0}\,:\,{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{k})\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x_{0}}^{(\beta)})
given as in Proposition
2.4
. We fix a generator
\eta\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

such that

\eta_{x_{0}}=\eta^{\alpha}_{f}.

To simplify our notation, we will write
\kappa_{0}(\eta)
in place of
\kappa_{0}(\eta_{k})
. We then have

(2.44)

\kappa_{0}(\eta)=C_{\mathrm{K}}\eta^{\beta}_{f}\quad\textrm{for some $C_{\mathrm{K}}\in E^{\times}.$}

Let us put

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} L_{\mathrm{K},{\kappa}_{0}(\eta)}(f,\xi)&:=L_{p,{\kappa}_{0}(\eta)}(\mathbf{z}(x_{0},\xi))\\
&=\left<\textup{res}_{p}(\mathbf{z}(x_{0},\xi)),c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{x_{0}},0}(\kappa_{0}(\widetilde{\eta}))^{\iota}\right>_{x_{0}}\,.\end{aligned}

We deduce on combining (
2.43
) and (
2.44
) that

(2.45)

L_{\mathrm{K},{\kappa}_{0}(\eta)}(f,\xi)=C_{\mathrm{K}}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)L_{\mathrm{K},\beta}(f,\xi)=C_{\mathrm{K}}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)L_{\mathrm{S},\beta}(f,\xi)\,.

2.4.4.
\theta
-critical
p
-adic
L
-functions

We introduce the Perrin-Riou-style critical
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to
\theta
-critical forms.

Recall that we fixed a generator
\eta
of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
such that
\eta_{x_{0}}=\eta_{f}^{\alpha}
. Recall also from (
2.23
) the element

{\bbeta}:=1\otimes X\eta+X\otimes\eta\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\,.

Definition 2.22

(Arithmetic
\theta
-critical
p
-adic
L
-function in two variables)
.

We call the element

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi):=L_{p,\eta}\left(\textup{res}_{p}\left(\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\right)\right)\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma),

where
L_{p,\eta}
is the abstract
p
-adic
L
-function
from Definition
2.10
(ii), the arithmetic
p
-adic
L
-function (in two variables) on the connected component
\mathcal{X}
of the eigencurve.

Recall that for each classical point
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
and a basis
\xi_{x}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{B}}(f_{x}^{\circ})
, we denote by
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha(x)}(f_{x}^{\circ},\xi_{x})
the Manin–Višik
p
-adic
L
-function associated to the stabilization
f_{x}
of
f_{x}^{\circ}
.
It follows from Proposition
2.11
and Kato’s results reviewed

that

(2.46)

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi,x)=A_{x}^{\pm}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(x)\,L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha(x)}^{\pm}(f_{x}^{\circ},\xi_{x})\quad\textrm{for some nonzero $A_{x}^{\pm}\in E.$}

Our first result concerns the behavior of
L_{p,\eta}
at the critical point
x_{0}.

Paralleling the notation of §
2.2.5
, we set

\displaystyle L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi):=L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi,x_{0}),

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi):=\left.\left(\frac{d}{dX}L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\right)\right|_{X=0}\,.

Evidently,
L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
depends only on the choice of
\xi
and it can be written in the form

L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)={\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)\,}\left<\mathbf{z}(f^{*},\xi^{*}),c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f},0}\left(\widetilde{\eta_{f}^{\alpha}}\right)^{\iota}\right>_{V_{f}}.

Therefore,

(2.47)

L^{[0]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)={\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)\,}L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi).

Here, we note that we have identified the pairings
\left<\,,\,\right>_{V_{f}}
and
\left<\,,\,\right>_{x_{0}}
. On the other hand,
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi)

depends also on the choice of the lift

of
\eta_{f}^{\alpha}
. We will write
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{[1]}(f,\xi)
in place of
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi)
whenever we want to stress this dependence.

The following result should be compared with Proposition
2.17
and Proposition
2.19
.

Theorem 2.23
.

Assume that
e=2
and that the condition (
GP)
) holds true.

There exist bounded
p
-adic
L
-functions

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
and

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)\in\Lambda_{E}
such that

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell^{\iota}_{i}\right)L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)\,,

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell^{\iota}_{i}\right)L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)\,.

In particular,

L_{p,\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})=0
for all integers
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
characters
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
of finite order.

The secondary
p
-adic
L
-functions

verify the following interpolation property: For every character
\rho\in X(\Gamma)
of finite order and integer

we have

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})=C_{\mathrm{K}}\cdot{(j-1)!}\,e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(f;\rho\chi^{j})\,\frac{L(f,\rho^{-1},j)}{(2\pi i)^{j+1-k}\Omega_{f}^{\pm}}\,,\qquad\qquad\textrm{\,if\, $\rho(-1)=\mp(-1)^{j}\,,$}

where
e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j)
is given as in (
2.34
) and

C_{\mathrm{K}}
as in (
2.44
).

We call
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
and

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)

that appear in the statement of Theorem
