(i) the cyclotomic improvement of the critical
p
-adic
L
-functions

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
and

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
, respectively.

Proof.

This portion is a restatement of Proposition
2.12
(ii) in the present setting.

Suppose that the condition (
GP)
) holds true. Then Theorem
2.16
yields

(2.48)

L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})={\frac{b(\rho,j)}{a(\rho,j)}}L_{\mathrm{K},\kappa_{0}(\eta)}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})

for all
1\leq j\leq k-1
and every finite-order character
\rho
with
p
-power conductor. We note that

and

in Equation (

) are defined in (

). On the other hand, it follows from (

) and the interpolation properties of the slope-zero

that

(2.49)

L_{\mathrm{K},{\kappa_{0}(\eta)}}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})={C_{K}\cdot}(j-1)!\,e_{p,\beta}(f,\rho,j)\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(f{;\rho\chi^{j}})\,\frac{L(f,\rho^{-1},j)}{(2\pi i)^{j+1-k}\Omega_{f}^{\pm}}

where

e_{p,\beta}(f,\rho,j)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{p^{j-1}}{\beta}\right)\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{p^{j}}\right)&\hbox{ if }\rho=\mathds{1},\\
\displaystyle\frac{p^{nj}}{{\beta^{n}}\tau(\rho^{-1})}&\hbox{ if }\rho\neq\mathds{1}.\end{cases}

An easy computation shows that

(2.50)

\frac{b(\rho,j)}{a(\rho,j)}e_{p,\beta}(f,\rho,j)=e_{p,\alpha}(f,\rho,j).

The assertion (ii) follows on combining (
2.48
)
with (
2.49
) and (
2.50
),
∎

As our second result, we prove that our two-variable function
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
agrees with Bellaïche’s
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
:

Theorem 2.24
.

Assume that
e=2
and condition (
GP)
) holds true.
Then there exist functions
u^{\pm}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\times}

such that

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)=u^{\pm}\,{\mathcal{E}}_{N}\,L_{\mathrm{S}}^{\pm}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})\,,

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{N}
is defined by (
2.42
).

Proof.

We have

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha(x)}^{\pm}(f_{x}^{\circ},\xi_{x})=B_{x}^{\pm}L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}^{\pm},x)\,\,\,,\qquad\textrm{for some $B_{x}^{\pm}\in E^{\times}\,.$}

for each classical non-critical point
x
. Comparing this fact with (
2.46
), we infer that

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)=\lambda_{x}^{\pm}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(x)\,L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}},x),\qquad\lambda_{x}^{\pm}:=A_{x}^{\pm}B_{x}^{\pm}\in E.

for each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
. On writing
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
and

L_{\mathrm{S}}^{\pm}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})
as power series with coefficients in
O_{\mathcal{X}}
, we see that there exist meromorphic functions
\lambda^{\pm}\in{\rm Frac}(O_{\mathcal{X}})
such that

(2.51)

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)=\lambda^{\pm}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}\,\,L_{\mathrm{S}}^{\pm}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}}).

We wish to prove that
\lambda^{\pm}(x_{0})\neq 0
. Evaluating (
2.51
) at the character
\rho\chi^{r}

(where
1\leqslant r\leqslant k-1
and
\rho
has finite order) we have

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi;\rho\chi^{r})=\lambda^{\pm}\,\mathcal{E}_{N}\,\,L_{\mathrm{S}}^{\pm}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}};\rho\chi^{r}).

It follows from Proposition
2.17
and Theorem
2.23
that the functions

L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi;\rho\chi^{r}),\,L_{\mathrm{S}}^{\pm}(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\pm}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}};\rho\chi^{r})\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}

both vanish at
x_{0}
. Therefore, considering the derivatives of both sides with respect to
X
at
x_{0}
, we obtain that

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{r})=\lambda^{\pm}(x_{0})\,\mathcal{E}_{N}(x_{0};\rho\chi^{r})\,L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{r}).

Using the non-vanishing results of Rohrlich in
[
Roh88
]
if
k=2
, we choose
\rho
and
r
such that
L(f,\rho^{-1},r)\neq 0
and
\mathcal{E}_{N}(x_{0};\rho\chi^{r})^{\pm}
. It follows from Proposition
2.19
and Theorem
2.23
that

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{r})
and

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{r})
do not vanish. Therefore
\lambda^{\pm}(x_{0})\neq 0
, and the theorem is proved.
∎

Corollary 2.25
.

Assume that
e=2
and condition (
GP)
) holds true. Then,

(2.52)

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi)\,={\mathcal{E}_{N}(f)}\,\lambda^{\pm}(f)\,L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi)\,,

where
\lambda^{\pm}(f):=\lambda^{\pm}(x_{0})\neq 0
.
Equivalently,

(2.53)

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi)\,=\,\lambda^{\pm}(f)\,L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi)\,.

Remark 2.26
.

The interpolation properties for the
p
-adic function

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi)
was proved by Bellaïche using the theory of modular symbols. In the present work, we recover it using an entirely different method, assuming the validity of (
GP)
).

We continue to assume that the eigenform
f_{\alpha}
is
\theta
-critical. Recall that
\beta
denotes the other root of the Hecke polynomial of
f
at
p
, so that
v_{p}(\beta)=0
and the
p
-stabilized eigenform
f_{\beta}
has slope zero.

As another incarnation of the eigenspace transition by differentiation principle (cf. §
2.1
), one can explicitly compare the improved
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
and the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta}(f,\xi)
. This comparison involves an Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}
(cf. Proposition
2.27
below), whose appearance is a reflection of the extreme exceptional zero phenomena the
\theta
-critical
p
-adic
L
-functions exhibit. The construction and the properties of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}
, which we believe are of independent interest, will be given in §
3.2.3
.

To motivate for the latter portions of our paper (and for the completeness of our discussion of
p
-adic
L
-functions), we record this comparison of the improved
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
and the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta}(f,\xi)
here, deferring its proof to §
3.2.3
.

Proposition 2.27
.

The
p
-adic
L
functions
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
and

L_{\mathrm{K},\beta}(f,\xi)
are related via the identity

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)=(-1)^{k-1}\,\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f^{*}}(k))\,L_{\mathrm{K},\beta}(f,\xi)\,,

where
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f^{*}}(k))
is the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant defined in §
3.2.3
.

It seems that there is no direct way to prove the analogue of the comparison in Proposition
2.27
for the
p
-adic
L
-functions
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
and
L_{\mathrm{S},\beta}(f,\xi)
that are constructed by interpolating Betti cohomology (i.e. without appealing to (
2.52
) and (
2.45
)). This serves as an independent justification to pursue a Perrin-Riou style construction of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions.

2.4.5.
Infinitesimal thickening of
p
-adic
L
-functions revisited

We will next discuss an extension of the comparison in Theorem
2.24
in an infinitesimal neighborhood of a
\theta
-critical point
x_{0}
(cf. Proposition
2.32
below). This refinement is relevant to the Iwasawa theoretic study of these
p
-adic
L
-functions in §
3
.
Throughout §
2.4.5
, we assume that
f_{\alpha}
is a
\theta
-critical normalized eigenform and retain the conventions of §
2.4.4
.

If the condition (
GP)
) does not hold, then
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi;\rho\chi^{j})=0
for all
\rho
and
j
as above. This can be proved in a very similar manner to the proof of Theorem
2.23
and we omit details. This in comparison with Bellaïche’s interpolation result (cf. Proposition
2.19
and Theorem
2.24
) leads us to the following:

Conjecture 2.28
.

The condition (
GP)
) holds at any
\theta
-critical point.

Recall that we assume
e=2
and that the condition (
GP)
) holds.
Then Corollary
2.25
tells us that

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi)=\lambda^{\pm}(f)\,L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi)\,,\qquad\lambda^{\pm}(f)\in E^{\times}\,.

The following is another important open problem in the theory of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions:

Conjecture 2.29
.

The functions
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
and
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
are nonzero.

Recall that
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)
and
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
both depend only on the choice of
\xi
. It is also natural to raise the following

Question 2.30
.

Can we explicitly determine
\lambda^{\pm}(f)
?

Let us put
\widetilde{E}:=E[X]/(X^{2})
and fix a uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. Let us define for each natural number
n
the ring

\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}=\mathcal{O}_{E}+\varpi_{E}^{-n}\mathcal{O}_{E}X\,\subset\widetilde{E}.

It is easy to see that
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
is a unital
4
4

4

An algebra
A
is called unital if it contains a multiplicative unit
1_{A}
. A subalgebra
B
of such
A
is called a
unital subalgebra
if
1_{A}\in B
.

\mathcal{O}_{E}
-subalgebra of
\widetilde{E}
.

Lemma 2.31
.

Let
A
be a unital
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-subalgebra of
\widetilde{E}

such that the natural projection
\widetilde{E}\rightarrow E
induces a surjective homomorphism of rings
A\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{E}
. Then either
A
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
for some
n\geqslant 0
, or

A=\mathcal{O}_{E}+EX
.

Proof.

Let
I
denote the kernel of the map
A\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{E}.

Then
I
is an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-submodule of
EX.
Hence either
I=\varpi_{E}^{-n}\mathcal{O}_{E}X
or

I=EX.
Since
\mathcal{O}_{E}\subset A
(recall that
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-algebra
A
is unital by assumption) the proof of our lemma follows.
∎

Set
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma):=\widetilde{E}\otimes_{E}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).

Recall from §
2.3.5
the infinitesimal thickening of the critical
p
-adic
L
-function

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi):=L_{\mathrm{S}}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\xi,\mathcal{X}})\pmod{X^{2}}\quad\in\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma).

As we have noted in (
2.38
), it can be written in the form

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)=L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)+XL_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi).

Analogously, we define the infinitesimal thickening

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi):=L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0]}(f,\xi)+XL_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi)\in\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\,.

of the arithmetic critical
p
-adic
L
-function. Let us set
\Lambda^{(n)}:=\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda
and

\Lambda^{(\infty)}:=\varinjlim_{n}\Lambda^{(n)}.

Proposition
2.32
below, where we compare the two infinitesimal thickenings of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions (
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
and
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
) in
\Lambda^{(\infty)}
is an infinitesimal refinement of the comparison in (
2.52
).
Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}\in\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}=\Lambda_{E}\otimes_{E}E[X]/(X^{2})
denote the image of
{\mathcal{E}}_{N}\in\Lambda_{\mathcal{X}}
given as in (
2.40
).

Proposition 2.32
.

Assume that
e=2
and the condition (
GP)
) holds. Then the
\Lambda^{(\infty),\pm}
-free modules generated by
{\widetilde{L}}^{\pm}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)
and
\lambda^{\pm}(f)\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}\cdot{\widetilde{L}}^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi)
coincide.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.24
, we have

{\widetilde{L}}^{\pm}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}(f,\xi)=u^{\pm}(X)\cdot\lambda^{\pm}(f)\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}\cdot{\widetilde{L}}^{\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha}(f,\xi),

with
u^{\pm}(X)\in 1+EX.
Let us choose
n\gg 0
so that
u^{\pm}(X)\in\Lambda^{(n)}
. Then
u^{\pm}(X)
is invertible in
\Lambda^{(n)}
and the proof of our proposition follows.
∎

Chapter 3
Main Conjectures for the punctual eigencurve and the improved critical
p
-adic
L
-functions

In this chapter, we will introduce and study the algebraic counterparts of
\theta
-critical
p
-adic
L
-functions, with our goal to extend Perrin-Riou’s theory to this particular setting.

Along the way, we introduce our
critical
\mathcal{L}
-invariants
in §
3.2.3
, study its basic properties in this portion with the aid of the earlier portions of §
3.2
, and present in §
3.2.3
a proof of Proposition
2.27
which we stated in the previous chapter.

We study various Selmer groups (relevant to our discussion) in §
3.2.4
in terms of objects of arithmetic interest (e.g. Tate–Shafarevich groups and regulators), which we also define in this portion.

In §
3.3
, we introduce modules of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions building on Perrin-Riou’s philosophy and formulate in §
3.4
the Iwasawa main conjectures for
\theta
-critical forms in terms of these. Our main result in this portion, Proposition
3.34
, tells us that the Iwasawa main conjectures for either of the
p
-stabilizations,

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

, are equivalent to one another provided that our Iwasawa theoretic
\mathcal{L}
-invariant does not vanish.

The nomenclature “the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions” is due to Perrin-Riou (cf.
[
PR95
]
, §2). This terminology is motivated by the expectation that this module should be generated by an appropriate (analytic)
p
-adic
L
-function (cf.
[
PR95
, §4.2.2]
; see also

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

below). This expectation, in fact, is a form of the relevant Iwasawa main conjecture, which generalizes the classical Main Conjectures of Mazur and Greenberg to the non-critical non-ordinary setting. In this sense, the module of
p
-adic
L
-functions generalizes the notion of the characteristic polynomial of the Iwasawa theoretic Selmer group of an elliptic curve at an ordinary prime.

A further justification is the fact that they recover, via Iwasawa descent, the conjectural leading terms of the
p
-adic
L
-functions that the
p
-adic variants of the conjectures of Beilinson–Bloch–Kato predict (see
[
PR95
, Theorem 3.5.2]
; see also Theorems
3.45
and
3.47
below). Note that the theory of Selmer complexes provides a natural framework for Iwasawa descent, and the modules of
p
-adic
L
-functions are, in a certain sense, more natural objects than characteristic polynomials of classical Iwasawa modules. This point of view was stressed in
[
Nek06
]
and
[
Ben14
]
.

The final section in this chapter (§
3.5
), which is also the lengthiest, is where we develop Iwasawa descent for our modules of
p
-adic
L
-functions, which are then employed to prove our leading term formulae for these. Our main results in this portion are Theorem
3.45
(which concerns both non-critical and non-central critical values) and Theorem
3.47
(which concerns central critical values). We would like to draw the attention of the reader also to Theorem
3.44
, which illustrates a key discrepancy between modules of
p
-adic
L
-functions attached to the
\theta
-critical
p
-stabilization and that to the slope-zero
p
-stabilization, exhibiting itself in the difference between their orders of vanishing at the central critical point. In view of the Iwasawa main conjectures

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

combined with Proposition
2.27
, this difference is accounted by the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathcal{L}
-invariant, which is expected to acquire a pole at the central critical point to compensate this discrepancy.

3.1.
Iwasawa theory for
\theta
-critical forms: Preliminaries

3.1.1.

Throughout this chapter we use the following notation and conventions.
Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N),\varepsilon_{f})
be a newform. We fix a prime number
p\geqslant 3
and
denote by
\alpha
and
\beta
the roots of its Hecke polynomial
X^{2}-a_{p}X+p^{k-1}\epsilon_{f}(p)
of
f
at
p
. Let
\Gamma_{p}:=\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p)
and let

f_{\alpha},f_{\beta}\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{p},\varepsilon_{f})
be the
p
-stabilizations of
f
with respect to
\alpha
and
\beta
respectively.
We will always assume, as in §
2.4.2
, that
v_{p}(\alpha)=k-1
and that
f_{\alpha}
is
a
\theta
-critical newform of weight
k
and nebentypus
\varepsilon_{f}
.
We let
V_{f}
denote Deligne’s
p
-adic Galois representation attached to
f
, so that we have
V_{f}\simeq V_{x_{0}}\simeq V_{f_{\alpha}}
in the notation of Chapter
2
.
We denote by
f^{*}
the complex conjugate of
f
(see Section
2.3.1
). We recall from (
2.39
) the canonical
non-degenerate pairings

\displaystyle V_{f^{*}}\otimes_{E}V_{f}\longrightarrow E(\chi^{1-k}),

\displaystyle[\,,\,]\,:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}})\times{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})\longrightarrow{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(E(\chi^{1-k}))\simeq E\,.

3.1.2.

Recall that the restriction of
V_{f}
on the decomposition group at
p
decomposes into a direct sum of
p
-adic representations

V_{f}=V_{f}^{(\alpha)}\oplus V_{f}^{(\beta)},

where
V_{f}^{(\alpha)}
and
V_{f}^{(\beta)}
are crystalline one-dimensional representations of Hodge–Tate weights
1-k
and
0
respectively.
More precisely,

V_{f}^{(\alpha)}=E(\nu_{\alpha}\chi^{1-k}),\qquad V_{f}^{(\beta)}=E(\nu_{\beta}),

where
\nu_{\alpha}
and
\nu_{\beta}
are non-trivial unramified characters
such that
\nu_{\alpha}\nu_{\beta}=\varepsilon_{f}.

In particular,

{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\alpha)})=\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}).

This property has important consequences on the structures of various Selmer groups, which we discuss in the present Chapter.

The roots of the Hecke polynomial of the dual form
f^{*}
at
p
are

\alpha^{*}:=p^{k-1}/\beta=\varepsilon_{f}^{-1}(p)\alpha\,,\qquad\beta^{*}=p^{k-1}/\alpha:=\varepsilon_{f}^{-1}(p)\beta\,.

To ease our notation, we will write
f^{*}_{\alpha}
and
f^{*}_{\beta}
to denote the stabilizations
f^{*}_{\alpha^{*}}
and
f^{*}_{\beta^{*}}
.

The eigenform
f^{*}_{\alpha}
is also
\theta
-critical.
As a reflection of this fact, the restriction of
V_{f^{*}}
to the decomposition group at
p
factors as

V_{f^{*}}=V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}\oplus V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}\,,

where
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}\left(V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}\right)=\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}}).
The duality (
2.39
)
gives rise to the isomorphisms

(3.1)

V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}\simeq\left(V_{f}^{(\beta)}\right)^{*}(1-k)\,,\qquad V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}\simeq\left(V_{f}^{(\alpha)}\right)^{*}(1-k).

We recall that the differential forms associated to
f
and
f^{*}
fix bases
\{\eta_{f}^{\alpha}\}
of
\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}\left(V_{f}^{(\alpha)}\right)
and
\{\eta_{f^{*}}^{\alpha}\}
of
\operatorname{Fil}^{k-1}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}})
, respectively. As in Section
2.4.3
, we choose
\eta_{f}^{\beta}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\beta)})
and
\eta_{f^{*}}^{\beta}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)})

in such a way that

[\eta_{f}^{\beta},\eta^{\alpha}_{f^{*}}]=1=[\eta_{f}^{\alpha},\eta^{\beta}_{f^{*}}]\,,

[\eta_{f}^{\beta},\eta^{\beta}_{f^{*}}]=0=[\eta_{f}^{\alpha},\eta^{\alpha}_{f^{*}}]\,.

For any
\eta\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f})
and
j\in\mathbb{Z},
we denote by
\eta[j]\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}(j))
the twist of
\eta
by the canonical generator
d_{j}:=t^{-j}\otimes\varepsilon^{\otimes j}
of
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(j))
; cf. Section
1.1.5
.

3.1.3.

We denote by
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})
the Iwasawa cohomology
with coefficients in
V_{f}
, namely,

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}):=\left(\underset{n}{\varprojlim}H^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{n}})},T_{f})\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E\,,

where
T_{f}\subset V_{f}
is any
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice; cf. (
1.32
). Recall that the restriction map

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f})

is injective thanks to Kato
[
Kat04
]
and we may identify
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})
with its image in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f})
. We let

\textup{res}_{p,\star}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\star)}(k))\,,\qquad\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}

denote the composition of the restriction map
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k))\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k))
with the projection onto
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\star)}(k)).
Recall the Beilinson–Kato
elements
\mathbf{z}(f,\xi)\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k)).
It follows from (
3.1
) that Kato’s
p
-adic
L
-functions of
f^{*}
can be written in the form

(3.2)

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=\left<\textup{res}_{p,\alpha}(\mathbf{z}(f,\xi)),c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)},0}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{f^{*}}^{\beta}\right)^{\iota}\right>_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}}\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma),\qquad\widetilde{\eta}_{f^{*}}^{\beta}=\eta_{f^{*}}^{\beta}\otimes(1+\pi)\,,

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=\left<\textup{res}_{p,\beta}(\mathbf{z}(f,\xi)),c\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)},0}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{f^{*}}^{\alpha}\right)^{\iota}\right>_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}}\in\mathscr{H}(\Gamma),\qquad\widetilde{\eta}_{f^{*}}^{\alpha}=\eta_{f^{*}}^{\alpha}\otimes(1+\pi)\,.

Let us put

z(f,\xi,j):=\mathrm{pr}_{0}\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{j-k}(\mathbf{z}(f,\xi))\right)\in H^{1}(V_{f}(j))\,,

where
\mathrm{pr}_{0}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\rightarrow H^{1}(V_{f}(j))

denotes the canonical projection.

The following set of statements will be used repeatedly.

Proposition 3.1
.

We have

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)})=\{0\}.

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\textup{res}_{p}\bigl(z(f,\xi,j)\bigr)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\,\iff\,L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{k-j})=0.

For all but finitely many
j\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

\textup{res}_{p}\bigl(z(f,\xi,j)\bigr)\notin H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j)).

Proof.

This assertion follows from (
3.2
) together with the fact that
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}=L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}
are non-zero.

We have

L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{k-j})=\left(\textup{res}_{p,\alpha}\bigl(z(f,\xi,j)\bigr),c\circ\mathrm{pr}_{0}\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{k-j}\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)},0}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{f^{*}}^{\beta}\right)^{\iota}\right)\right)_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}(k-j)},

where
(\,,\,)_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}(k-j)}
denotes the local duality.
By Theorem
1.2
(ii), we have

\mathrm{pr}_{0}\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{k-j}\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)},0}\right)=(-1)^{k-j}\mathrm{pr}_{0}\left(\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}(k-j),k-j}(\partial^{j-k}\otimes d_{k-j})\right)\,.

Together with the explicit formulae in (
1.23
), this
implies that

\mathrm{pr}_{0}\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{k-j}\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)},0}\left(\widetilde{\eta}_{f^{*}}^{\beta}\right)^{\iota}\right)

does not vanish, since the Euler-like factor

(1-\beta^{-1}p^{k-j-1})(1-\beta p^{j-k})^{-1}

does not vanish for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
(note that
v_{p}(\beta)=0
and
\beta\neq 1
).
Therefore,

\textup{res}_{p}\bigl(z(f,\xi,j)\bigr)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\,\iff\,L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{k-j})=0\,\iff\,L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{k-j})=0.

This proves the second portion of our proposition.

It follows from Kato’s fundamental work (cf. Proposition
2.20
) that

L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}
coincides with
L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}
, which is non-zero and bounded. Therefore,
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}

can have only finitely many zeroes. Part (iii) of the proposition now follows from (ii).
∎

3.2.
Selmer groups and critical
{\mathscr{L}}
-invariants

3.2.1.

According to the general philosophy (which manifests itself in
p
-adic Bloch–Kato–Beilinson conjectures in concrete form), the
p
-adic
L
-function associated to
f^{*}_{\alpha^{*}}
should carry arithmetic information that is conjecturally encoded by the Selmer group attached to the Galois representation
V_{f}
. Fix an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice
T_{f}
of
V_{f}
stable under the Galois action. In what follows, we shall introduce Selmer groups associated to various local conditions. At
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}
(where we recall that
S
is the set of prime divisors of
Np
) we will always take the unramified local condition

H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j)):=\ker\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}^{\rm ur},V_{f}(j))\right)

as the local conditions. We will consider the following local conditions at
p:

\bullet

Bloch–Kato condition:
H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
.

\bullet

H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j)):=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\alpha)}_{f}(j))
.

\bullet

H^{1}_{\beta}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j)):=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))
.

\bullet

Relaxed local condition:
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j)):=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
.

\bullet

Strict local condition:
H^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j)):=0.

Definition 3.2
.

For
\star\in\{{\rm f},\alpha,\beta,\{p\},0\},
we denote by

H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}(j))
,
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}(j))
and
H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))

the Selmer groups associated to local conditions

\{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j))\,:p\neq\ell\in S\}
and
H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
; cf. Section
1.2.3
.

We remark that

(3.3)

H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}(j))=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}\right),

where

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j)):=\ker\left({H^{1}_{S}(V_{f}(j))}\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\bigoplus}\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j))}\right)

is the relaxed Selmer group.

By Proposition
1.8
(Poitou–Tate global duality), the Bloch–Kato Selmer group
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(j))
sits in the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}\longrightarrow\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))}{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\right)^{*}\longrightarrow 0\,.

Moreover, one has

(3.4)

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(j))-\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=\dim_{E}t_{V_{f}(j)}-1,

where
t_{V_{f}(j)}
denotes the Bloch–Kato tangent space of
V_{f}(j)
. We remark that (
3.4
) is a particular case of the general formula proved in
[
FPR94
, Section II,2.2.2]
.

3.2.2.

We outline below what is already known to the experts and what is expected concerning the dimensions of various Selmer groups.

Non-central critical case:
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
j\neq\frac{k}{2}

In this scenario, we have
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(j))=0
and
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))=1
, cf.
[
Kat04
]
.

Non-critical case
j\geqslant k

In this case,
H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
and therefore
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V(j))=H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V(j))
. The Beilinson–Bloch–Kato conjecture predicts that

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V(j))=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V(j))=1.

We remark that this agrees with the
p
-adic variants of this conjecture. More precisely, let us consider the condition

p
Bei)

L_{{\mathrm{S}},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},{\xi^{*};\chi^{j}})\neq 0
.

This condition holds for all but finitely many
j
, since the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{{\mathrm{S}},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},{\xi^{*};\chi^{j}})
is bounded. The
p
-adic Beilinson conjecture of Perrin-Riou predicts that (

p

Bei)
)
holds for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{\frac{k}{2}\}
(in particular, for all
j\geq k
, which is the case of interest in §
3.2.2
).

Lemma 3.3
.

If (

p

Bei)
) holds true at
k-j
, then so do the conditions (
B)
) and (
J)
) below:

B)

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=0

and

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(j))=1
(Beilinson–Bloch–Kato conjecture) .

J)

The restriction map

\textup{res}_{p}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))

is injective (Jannsen’s conjecture, cf.
[
Jan89
]
) .

We remark that the conclusion of (
J)
) is equivalent to the vanishing of the strict Selmer group
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(j))
.

Proof.

The fact that
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=0
follows if one can show the existence of an Euler system

\{c_{\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{n})}\}_{n\nmid N}
for the dual
V_{f}(j)
of the Galois representation
V_{f^{*}}(k-j)
, for which we have
c_{\mathbb{Q}}\neq 0

[
Rub00
, Theorem II.2.3]
. If the eigenform form
f
does not have CM, then thanks to Kato’s explicit reciprocity laws (cf.
[
Kat04
]
, Theorem 16.6), the Beilinson–Kato element Euler system verifies this property whenever
L_{{\mathrm{S}},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{k-j})\neq 0
. If the eigenform
f
has CM, then one utilizes elliptic units in place of the Beilinson–Kato elements and proceeds in an identical fashion. Since
H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=0
for
j\geqslant k,
we deduce that
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=0
. Moreover, since
\dim_{E}t_{V_{f}(j)}=2
for
j\geq k
, it follows from (
3.4
) that
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{{\rm f}}(V_{f}(j))=1
. This discussion shows that the condition (
B)
) holds true.

In order to verify (
J)
), one only needs to show that the map
\textup{res}_{p}
is not identically zero, as we have already explained that
H^{1}_{\rm{f}}(V_{f}(j))
is an
E
-vector space of dimension one under our running assumption that (

p

Bei)
) holds true for
k-j
. In other words, one needs to exhibit a single element
c\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(j))
such that
\textup{res}_{p}(c)\neq 0
. It follows from Proposition
3.1

that we can take
c=z(f,\xi,j)
and this concludes our proof.
∎

Non-critical case
j\leqslant 0

In this case, the Beilinson–Bloch–Kato conjecture predicts that

(3.5)

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V(j))=\{0\},\quad\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V(j))=1.

Using (
3.4
), we deduce that the conclusion of (
B)
) for the
dual
eigenform
f^{*}
implies (
3.5
). In particular, (
3.5
) follows from the non-vanishing of
L_{{\mathrm{S}},\beta}(f,\xi;\chi^{k-j}).

In summary, it is known or expected that
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))
is a one-dimensional vector space which injects into
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
whenever
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
.

We turn our attention to the groups
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))
and
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))
. Our analysis of these Selmer groups will play a crucial role in §
3.2.3
, where we introduce our critical
\mathscr{L}
-invariants.

Since we have

H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\,,\qquad\textrm{if $1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1,$}

we also have

H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(j))\,,\qquad\textrm{if $1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1$\,.}

In particular,
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=0
if
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
j\neq\frac{k}{2}.

If
j\geqslant k
, Lemma
3.3
together with
Proposition
3.1
imply that

(3.6)

L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*},\chi^{k-j})\neq 0\,\,\implies\,\,H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=0\,,

and therefore

(3.7)

H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))=\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(j))\right)\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\,.

Thanks to the implication (
3.6
), we deduce that
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=0
for all but finitely many
j\geq k
.

We remark that the condition (
3.7
) is equivalent to the regularity of the submodule

{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\subset{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}(j))

in the sense of
[
Ben14
]
.

In summary, it is expected that
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=0
for all
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
.

To analyze the Selmer group
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))
, we will assume that
f
has CM by an imaginary quadratic field
K
. More precisely,
f=\theta(\psi)
is the theta-series of a Hecke character
\psi:K^{\times}\textbackslash\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow L^{\times}\xhookrightarrow{\iota_{\infty}}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
of an imaginary quadratic field
K
with infinity type
(1-k,0)
and conductor coprime to
p
, where
L
is the number field generated by the image of
\psi
on the finite idèles
\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\infty,\times}
over
K
. Since we work in the setting that
f
admits a
\theta
-critical
p
-stabilization
f_{\alpha}
, it follows that the prime
p=\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{p}^{c}
splits in
K/\mathbb{Q}
, where
c
denotes the unique non-trivial element of
\operatorname{Gal}(K/\mathbb{Q})
. Recall also the embedding
\iota_{p}:\overline{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathbb{C}_{p}
we have fixed and assume that the prime
\mathfrak{p}
is induced from this embedding. Note also that the Hecke field of
f
is contained in
L
, and we enlarge
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
if necessary so that it contains
\iota_{p}(L)
.

In this paragraph, we closely follow the discussion in
[
Kat04
, §15.8]
and adopt the normalizations therein; see also
[
LLZ13
, §1.3]
and
[
LLZ15
, §5.2]
. Let
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}
denote the
p
-adic avatar of
\psi
, determined by the embedding
\iota_{p}
. Namely, for
x\in\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\infty,\times}
, we put
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}(x):=x_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1-k}\cdot\iota_{p}\circ\psi(x)
. In particular,
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is ramified at
\mathfrak{p}
. We denote by
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}
also the associated Galois character via the geometrically-normalized Artin map
K^{\times}\textbackslash\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\infty,\times}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{a}}{\rm Gal}(K^{\rm ab}/K)
of class field theory. In more precise terms, we define
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathfrak{a}(x)):=\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}(x)^{-1}
for
x\in\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\infty,\times}
. We then have

V_{f}\simeq{\rm Ind}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\,.

Note that
\iota_{p}
induces an identification
G_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Since
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}
is ramified at
\mathfrak{p}
, it follows that
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
acts on
V_{f}^{(\alpha)}
via
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and in turn also that it acts on
V_{f}^{(\beta)}
via
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
. In particular,
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathfrak{p}^{c})=\beta
.
Shapiro’s lemma allows us to identify
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))
canonically with the Selmer group

H^{1}_{\emptyset,0}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j}):=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})\longrightarrow H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}^{c}},\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})\right)\,.

Let us also define the dual Selmer group

(3.8)

H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j}):=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j})\longrightarrow H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j})\right)\,.

Let
\mathfrak{f}
denote the conductor of
\psi
and let

L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}\in{W}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})[[H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}]]
denote the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function (which depends on the choice of the embedding
\iota_{p}
) of tame conductor
\mathfrak{f}
, where
{W}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is the Witt vectors of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
(which coincides with the
p
-adic completion of the maximal unramified extension
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\mathrm{ur}}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
),
H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}:=\varprojlim H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{n}}
and
H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{n}}
is the ray class group of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{f}p^{n}
.

Remark 3.4
.

At first sight, the definition (
3.8
) may seem unnatural. We discuss its significance in this remark.

Note that whenever
1\leq j\leq k-1
, so that

L(f^{*},j)=L(\overline{\psi},j)=L(\psi^{-1}\mathbf{N}_{K}^{1-j},0)

is a critical value (where
\mathbf{N}_{K}:=\mathbf{N}\circ\mathbb{N}_{K/\mathbb{Q}}
is the adelic norm character), the Selmer group
H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})
coincides with the Bloch–Kato Selmer group. More generally, if
\nu
is a Hecke character of
K
such that
L(\nu^{-1},0)=L(\overline{\nu},1)
is critical and
\nu_{\mathfrak{p}}
denotes its
p
-adic avatar induced by
\iota_{p}
, then the Selmer group
H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\nu_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi)
defined in a manner analogous to (
3.8
) is critical. In other words, the Iwasawa theoretic Selmer group

H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}]]):=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}]])\longrightarrow H^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}]])\right)

interpolates the Bloch–Kato Selmer groups of critical Hecke characters of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}
.

Since
L(\psi\mathbf{N}_{K}^{j},0)
is not critical for any
j
, the Selmer group
H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j})
given as in (
3.4
) need not coincide with the Bloch–Kato Selmer group. However, it still arises as a specialization of the Iwasawa theoretic Selmer group
H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[H_{\mathfrak{f}p^{\infty}}]])
, and as such, the Iwasawa main conjectures (which were proved by Rubin under the additional hypothesis that
|H_{\mathfrak{f}}|
is coprime to
p
) show that its size is controlled by the value
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})
of Katz’
p
-adic
L
-function outside its range of interpolation. In more precise terms,

(3.9)

L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})\neq 0\hbox{ and }p\nmid|H_{\mathfrak{f}}|\qquad\implies\qquad H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j})=0\,.

We may use the observations in Remark
3.4
to establish a vanishing criterion for
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))=0
.

Proposition 3.5
.

Suppose that the order of the ray class
H_{\mathfrak{f}}
modulo
\mathfrak{f}
is coprime to
p
and that
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})\neq 0
. Then
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))=0
.

Proof.

Given an
E
-valued character
\xi
of
G_{K}
, let us denote by
E(\xi)
the one-dimensional
E
-vector space on which
G_{K}
acts by
\xi
. We note that
E(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j})
is the Tate-dual of
E(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})
and the Selmer groups

H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))\simeq H^{1}_{\emptyset,0}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})
and
H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j})
are related by the Euler–Poincaré characteristic formula, which reads in this case

(3.10)

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))={\rm dim}_{E}H^{1}_{\emptyset,0}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{0,\emptyset}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{1-j})\,.

The proof of our proposition follows on combining (
3.9
) and (
3.10
).
∎

It would be tempting to use the conclusion of Proposition
3.5
to deduce that
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))=0
for all but finitely many
j
(paralleling our discussion in §
3.2.2
regarding the Selmer group
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))
). However, this is not immediate (as in the case of
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))
) for the following reason. Since the function
\xi\mapsto L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\xi)
on the characters
\xi
of
\operatorname{Gal}(K(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/K)
is an Iwasawa function, it follows that
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j_{0}})\neq 0
for
some
integer
j_{0}
if and only if
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})\neq 0
for all but finitely many integers
j
. This observation combined with Proposition
3.5
tells us that
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))=0
for all but finitely many
j
provided that
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j_{0}})\neq 0
for
some
integer
j_{0}
.

The problem is that, as we have noted above, no character of the form
\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\xi
(where
\xi
is a character of
\operatorname{Gal}(K(\mu_{p^{\infty}})/K)
) belongs to the interpolation range of the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}
(since the Hodge–Tate weights of characters of this form at
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{c}
are
(1-k+j,j)
for some integer
j
, whereas the interpolation range for the Katz
p
-adic
L
-function consists of those characters with Hodge–Tate weights
(r,s)
with
r\geq 1
and
s\leq 0
) and we have no direct way of checking whether
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j_{0}})\neq 0
for a single integer
j_{0}
.

The values
L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})
are governed by Perrin-Riou’s
p
-adic Beilinson conjectures, in view of which, we conjecture that

p
Bei
′
)

L_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\psi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{j})\neq 0
for
j\gg 0
.

3.2.3.
Critical
\mathscr{L}
–invariants

Let us assume that (

p

Bei)
) is valid for a fixed integer
j
, so that the conditions (
B)
) and (
J)
) hold for the eigenforms
f
and
f^{*}
for the given
j
(recall that (

p

Bei)
) is indeed valid for all
j\gg 0
). Then
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))
is a one-dimensional
E
-vector space,
which injects into the two-dimensional
E
-vector space

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
equipped with the canonical decomposition
into the direct sum of one-dimensional spaces
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\alpha)}(j))
and
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\beta)}(j)).

This will allow us, in §
3.2.3
,
to define analogues of Greenberg’s
\mathscr{L}
-invariants in this scenario. On the analytic side, the interpolation formula in Theorem
2.23
(i) shows that the critical

p
-adic
L
-function vanishes on the critical strip.
Our
\mathscr{L}
-invariants appear in leading term formulae for these critical
p
-adic
L
-functions (cf. Proposition
2.27
, whose proof we present in §
3.2.3
below), as a justification to view this as an extremal incarnation of the exceptional zero phenomena.

The case
j\geq k

Let us consider the commutative diagram

(3.11)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern where both exponential maps are isomorphisms (since
j\geq k
), and
\lambda_{\alpha}
and
\lambda_{\beta}
denote the unique maps making the diagram commute. Note that
\lambda_{\alpha}
(resp.
\lambda_{\beta}
) is an isomorphism
if and only if
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))
(resp.
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))
) vanishes.

Definition 3.6
.

For any
j\geq k
such that (

p

Bei)
) holds (so that
\lambda_{\alpha}
is an isomorphism), we define the critical
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\in E
as the unique quantity that validates the identity

\lambda_{\beta}\circ\lambda_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\eta^{\alpha}_{f}[j]\right)={\mathscr{L}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))\cdot\eta_{f}^{\beta}[j].

This definition can be recast in the following equivalent form. Let
z\in H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))
be a nonzero cohomology class. Let us write

\textup{res}_{p}(x)=a\,\exp(\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[j])+b\,\exp(\eta_{f}^{\beta}[j])\,.

Then
{\mathscr{L}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))=\frac{b}{a}\in E
. Note again that
a\neq 0
since (

p

Bei)
) holds. It is also clear from definitions that

(3.12)

{\mathscr{L}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))\neq 0\quad\iff\quad b\neq 0\quad\iff\quad H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))=0\,.

Definition 3.7
.

For any
1\leq j\leq k-1
such that (

p

Bei)
) holds (so that
\lambda_{\alpha}
is an isomorphism), we define the critical
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\in E
as the unique quantity for which the following equality holds true:

\lambda_{\beta}\circ\lambda_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\eta^{\alpha}_{f}[j]\right)={\mathscr{L}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))\cdot\eta_{f}^{\beta}[j].

The interpolation formula for the slope-zero
p
-adic

-function shows that (

p

Bei)
) holds true if
1\leq j\leq k-1
and

As before, this definition can be recast in the following equivalent form. Let

be a nonzero cohomology class. If we write

then

. As in (

and
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
or when
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0
)

(3.14)

{\mathscr{L}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))\neq 0\quad\iff\quad b\neq 0\quad\iff\quad H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))=\{0\}\,.

by definitions.

Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariants

We next explain that the
\mathscr{L}
-invariants
{\mathscr{L}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))
we have introduced above interpolate to an Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant.

Recall that by Kato’s fundamental result
[
Kat04
]
, the
\Lambda[1/p]
-module
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
is free of rank one, and for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
(note that we no longer require that the condition (

p

Bei)
) holds for this choice of
j
), the restriction map gives rise to an injective map

\textup{res}_{p}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))\oplus H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\,.

Consider the exponential maps given as in (
1.22
):

(3.15)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j),j-k+1}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j),j}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\,.

To simplify the notation, we will denote them by
\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}
and
\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,j}
, respectively. One then has a commutative diagram
