where
\lambda_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{Iw}}

and
\lambda_{\beta}^{\mathrm{Iw}}
denote the unique maps making the diagram commute.
As we have noted above, the restriction map
\textup{res}_{p}
is injective by
[
Kat04
]
.

Let
\mathbf{z}
be a generator of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
. Then

\textup{res}_{p}(\mathbf{z})=F_{j}\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[j])+G_{j}\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,j}(\eta_{f}^{\beta}[j])

for some
F_{j},G_{j}\in\Lambda_{E}.

Definition 3.8
.

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z},
we define the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
by

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))=G_{j}/F_{j}\in\mathrm{Frac}(\Lambda_{E}).

Recall the Euler-like factors we have defined in Section
2.3
:

e_{p,\alpha}(f,\mathds{1},j)=\left(1-\frac{p^{j-1}}{\alpha}\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{\beta}{p^{j}}\right),\qquad e_{p,\beta}(f,\mathds{1},j)=\left(1-\frac{p^{j-1}}{\beta}\right)\cdot\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{p^{j}}\right).

Proposition 3.9
.

\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))
is well-defined for every integer
j
and we have

\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))=\mathrm{Tw}_{j}\circ\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}).

For all
j\gg 0
, we have
F_{j}(0)\neq 0
and
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
is well-defined. Moreover, for all such
j
,

(3.17)

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))(0)=\frac{(j-k)!}{(j-1)!}\cdot\frac{e_{p,\alpha}(f,\mathds{1},j)}{e_{p,\beta}(f,\mathds{1},j)}\cdot\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\,.

If
1\leq j\leq k-1
with
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
(or if
j=\frac{k}{2}
and
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0
), then we have

(3.18)

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))(0)=\frac{(-1)^{k-j}}{(j-1)!(k-j-1)!}\cdot\frac{e_{p,\alpha}(f,\mathds{1},j)}{e_{p,\beta}(f,\mathds{1},j)}\cdot\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\,.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
3.1
that
\textup{res}_{p}(\mathbf{z}(f,\xi))\notin H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(k))
and therefore,
F_{k}\neq 0
.
Moreover, for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have, by definitions,

(3.19)

\textup{res}_{p}(\mathrm{Tw}_{m}(\mathbf{z}))=\mathrm{Tw}_{m}(F_{k})\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,k+m}(\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[k+m])+\mathrm{Tw}_{m}(G_{k})\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,k+m}(\eta_{f}^{\beta}[k+m]).

Hence,
F_{k+m}=\mathrm{Tw}_{m}(F_{k})
and therefore,
F_{j}\neq 0
for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. Finally, it follows from (
3.19
) that

(3.20)

\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(k+m))=\mathrm{Tw}_{m}\circ\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k))

and the proof of Part (i) follows.

Since the projection map

\mathrm{pr}_{0}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))

is injective and
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
is
\Lambda_{E}
-free, it follows that the image
z:=\mathrm{pr}_{0}(\mathbf{z})\in H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))
of the generator
\mathbf{z}
of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
is nonzero. The formulae (
1.23
) shows that for each
j\gg 0
we have

(3.21)

\textup{res}_{p}(z)=(j-k)!\left(\frac{1-p^{j-1}\alpha^{-1}}{1-p^{-j}\alpha}\right)F_{j}(0)\cdot\exp\left(\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[j]\right)+(j-1)!\left(\frac{1-p^{j-1}\beta^{-1}}{1-p^{-j}\beta}\right)G_{j}(0)\cdot\exp\left(\eta_{f}^{\beta}[j]\right).

For
j\gg 0
the condition (

p

Bei)
) holds and for such
j
, it follows from the description of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))
that
F_{j}(0)\neq 0
and that (
3.17
) is valid.

The proof of (
3.18
) is virtually identical to that of (
3.17
) and for that reason, it will be omitted.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

Suppose that
f
has CM. It follows from Proposition
3.5
that we have the implication

\eqref{item_pBCM}\quad\implies{\quad\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})\neq 0}\,.

As we have noted in Remark
0.4.3
(ii), this implication can be also deduced from
[
LLZ13
, Theorem 3.2]
.

Proof of Proposition
2.27

We are now ready to present a proof of Proposition
2.27
, where we compare the improved
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f,\xi)
and the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta}(f,\xi)
in terms of our Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}
.
In order not to change our notation, we shall prove Proposition
2.27
for the dual eigenform
f^{*}
:

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=(-1)^{k-1}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(k))\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\,.

This statement is illustrative of the extreme exceptional zero phenomena that the
\theta
-critical
p
-adic
L
-functions exhibit.

Proof of Proposition
2.27
.

Let us write

\textup{res}_{p}(\mathbf{z}(f,\xi))=F_{k}^{\rm BK}\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(k),1}(\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[k])+G_{k}^{\rm BK}\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\beta)}(k),k}(\eta_{f}^{\beta}[k])\,,

where
F_{k}^{\rm BK},G_{k}^{\rm BK}\in\Lambda[1/p].

Then,

(3.22)

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=G_{k}^{\rm BK}\left<\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\beta)}(k),k}(\eta_{f}^{\beta}[k]),\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)},1-k}(\eta_{f^{*}}^{\alpha})^{\iota}\right>=(-1)^{k-1}G_{k}^{\rm BK}.

where the final equality follows from the explicit reciprocity law together with the fact that
V_{f}^{(\beta)}(k)
is the Tate dual of
V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}
. The same argument also yields

(3.23)

L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=F_{k}^{\rm BK}\left<\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(k),k}(\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[k]),\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)},0}(\eta_{f^{*}}^{\beta})^{\iota}\right>=F_{k}^{\rm BK}\,.

The proof of Proposition
2.27
follows on comparing (
3.22
) and (
3.23
), and noting that

G_{k}^{\rm BK}/F_{k}^{\rm BK}=G_{k}/F_{k}=\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k)).

∎

3.2.4.
Tate–Shafarevich groups, regulators and Tamagawa numbers

In this section, we use our
\mathscr{L}
-invariants to compare Tate–Shafarevich groups
\Sha_{\star}
to one another, arising from local conditions associated to
\star\in\{0,\alpha,\beta\}.

Fix a
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice
T_{f}
of
V_{f}
stable under the action of
G_{\mathbb{Q}}.

Proposition 3.11
.

For
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta,{\rm f}\}
, the Poitou–Tate exact sequence induces the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}\longrightarrow\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\right)^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0\,,

where we recall that
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))
and
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))
denote the relaxed and the strict Selmer groups, respectively.

Proof.

This is a particular case of Proposition
1.8
, since for each
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta,{\rm f}\},
the vector spaces
H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
and
H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f^{*}}(k-j))
are orthogonal complements of one another under the local Tate duality pairing
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\otimes H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f^{*}}(k-j))\rightarrow E
.
∎

In the remainder of §
3.2.4
, we assume that
j\geqslant 1

and
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
. The case
j\leqslant 0
can be handled using duality and relying on the case
j\geq k
(which we will investigate here). The central critical case
j=\frac{k}{2}
will be studied in §
3.2.5
.

Let us consider the following condition:

S)

H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(j))=\{0\}
.

Recall from §
3.2.3
that the critical
\mathcal{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}(V_{f}(j))
is defined whenever the hypothesis (

p

Bei)
) is valid. The condition (

p

Bei)
) holds for all but finitely many
j
(cf. §
3.2.2
) and for all
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
with
1\leq j\leq k-1
(cf. §
3.2.3
). Whenever (

p

Bei)
) holds, recall from (
3.12
) that the condition (
S)
) is equivalent to the non-vanishing of
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))
.

Definition 3.12
.

For any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module
M
, we denote by
M_{\mathrm{tf}}
the quotient of
M
by its maximal
p
-torsion submodule.

Lemma 3.13
.

Suppose
j\geqslant 1,

j\neq\frac{k}{2}
, and assume that (
S)
) holds true. If
j\geqslant k,
we assume, in addition,
the validity of the hypothesis (

p

Bei)
). Then the following assertions hold true:

For
\star\in\{0,\alpha,\beta\}
,
the group
H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))

has finite cardinality, and
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}=0.

In particular,

\Sha_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))=H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j)),\qquad\star\in\{0,\alpha,\beta\}.

ii) For
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\},
we have

\#\Sha_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))=\#\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))\cdot\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}+H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]\,.

Proof.

Fix
j\geqslant 1
such that
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
. It follows from (
S)
) that
H^{1}_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}=0
. If
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
, the vanishing of
H^{1}_{\beta}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}
follows from Kato’s theorem recalled in Section
3.2.2
. If
j\geqslant k
, the same vanishing follows from (
3.6
).
Since
H^{1}_{0}(T_{f}(j))\subset H^{1}_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j),
we deduce that
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}=0
for all
\star\in\{0,\alpha,\beta\}
.

We claim that
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=\{0\}.
For
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
this follows again from Kato’s theorem (applied to the form
f^{*}
), whereas for
j\geqslant k
, we use Lemma
3.3
.
By the definition of the Tate–Shafarevich group
\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))
, we have an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))\longrightarrow\Sha_{0}(T_{f_{*}}(k-j))\longrightarrow 0\,.

Since
\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))
is finite by Proposition
1.8
, we conclude that
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))\simeq\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))
is finite.

Let
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}.
It follows from Lemma
3.3
when
j\geq k
and §
3.2.2
in the remaining cases that
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))
is of rank one and the map

(3.24)

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}}

is an injection (thanks to the vanishing
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}=0
) with rank-one target. This shows that its cokernel has finite cardinality. Proposition
3.11
tells us that the cokernel of the map (
3.24
) is isomorphic to
\left(\dfrac{H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\right)^{\vee}
, thence

[{H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}:{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}]<\infty\,.

Since we have already verified that
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))
has finite cardinality, this concludes the proof that
H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))
has finite cardinality as well.
Hence, by the definition of the Tate-Shafarevich groups, we have
\Sha_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))=H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))

for
\star\in\{0,\alpha,\beta\}
.

This portion follows from Proposition
3.11
, on noting that the quotient
\dfrac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}
is torsion-free as per the definition of
H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))
(cf. Definition
3.2
).
∎

For
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\},
we denote by
N_{\star}[j]\subset{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\star)}(j))
the
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice generated by
\eta_{f}^{(\alpha)}[j]
. Recall that
|\,\cdot\,|
denotes the absolute value on
E
normalized so that
|p|=p^{-[E:\mathbb{Q}_{p}]}
. Recall from §
1.1.7
that we have put

(3.25)

\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star}):=|a|,

where
a\in E^{*}
is such that
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(T_{f}^{(\star)})=aN_{\star}
. The quantities
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})
should be thought of as
p
-adic periods. We remark that
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})=\left[{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(T_{f}^{(\star)}):N_{\star}\right].

For any
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-Galois module
T
we set

w(T):=\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E/\mathcal{O}_{E}).

The following proposition yields an interpretation of
the critical
\mathscr{L}
-invariant in terms of Tate–Shafarevich groups.

Proposition 3.14
.

Suppose
\frac{k}{2}\neq j\geqslant 1
, and
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
. Assume the validity of the hypotheses (

p

Bei)
) and (
S)
). Then

\bigl|\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\bigr|\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}{\#\Sha_{\beta}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}=\frac{w(T_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}(k-j))\cdot w(T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))}{w(T_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}(k-j))\cdot w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))}\cdot\frac{\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\beta)},N_{\beta})}{\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\alpha)},N_{\alpha})}\,.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.13
that

\frac{\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}{\#\Sha_{\beta}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}=\frac{\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]}{\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]}.

We have,

\displaystyle\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]=\left[N_{\beta}[j]:\lambda_{\beta}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right)\right]\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:\exp\left(N_{\beta}[j]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]=\left[N_{\alpha}[j]:\lambda_{\alpha}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right)\right]\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:(\exp^{*})^{-1}\left(N_{\alpha}[j]\right)\right].

Note that

\frac{\left[N_{\beta}[j]:\lambda_{\beta}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right)\right]}{\left[N_{\alpha}[j]:\lambda_{\alpha}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right)\right]}=\bigl|\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\bigr|^{-1}.

Using the formulae we have proved as part of Lemma
1.6
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:\exp\left(N_{\beta}[j]\right)\right]=|(j-1)!|\cdot\left|\frac{1-\beta^{-1}p^{j-1}}{1-\beta p^{-j}}\right|\cdot\frac{w(T_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}(k-j))}{w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))}\cdot\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\beta)},N_{\beta}),

\displaystyle\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:\left(\exp^{*}\right)^{-1}\left(N_{\alpha}[j]\right)\right]=|(k-j-1)!|^{-1}\cdot\left|\frac{1-\alpha^{-1}p^{j-1}}{1-\alpha p^{-j}}\right|\cdot\frac{w(T_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}(k-j))}{w(T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))}\cdot\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\alpha)},N_{\alpha}).

Combining this with Proposition
3.9
, we deduce that

\bigl|\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\bigr|\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}{\#\Sha_{\beta}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}=\frac{w(T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))w(T_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha)}(k-j))}{w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))w(T_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}(k-j))}\cdot\frac{\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\beta)},N_{\beta})}{\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\alpha)},N_{\alpha})}\,,

as required.
∎

Definition 3.15
.

Assume that the conditions (

p

Bei)
) and (
S)
) hold.

Let
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}.
For all
j\geqslant k,
we define the
p
-adic regulator map as the natural isomorphism

r_{V_{f}(j),\star}\,:\,H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}(j))/{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{\star}(j))

induced from the composition

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j)){=}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\xrightarrow{\log}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}(j))\,,

where
\log
is the Bloch–Kato logarithm and the equality
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))=H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
is because
j\geq k
.

For
\star=\alpha
(resp.
\star=\beta
), we put

R_{\star}(T_{f}(j)):=\left|\det(r_{V_{f}(j),\star})\right|^{-1}\,,

where we compute determinant of
r_{V_{f}(j),\star}
with respect to the lattice

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\subset H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))
and the lattice generated by
\eta_{f}^{\beta}[j]
(resp. by
\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[j]
).

The following is an interpretation of the critical
\mathcal{L}
-invariant in terms of regulators when
j\geqslant k
.

Proposition 3.16
.

Assume that the conditions (

p

Bei)
) and (
S)
) hold.
Then

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))=\frac{\det(r_{V_{f}(j),\alpha})}{\det(r_{V_{f}(j),\beta})}\,,

and therefore,

\left|\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\right|=\frac{R_{\beta}(T_{f}(j))}{R_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))}\,.

In the remainder of this paper, we will consider the Tamagawa numbers of the
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representations
V=V_{f}(j),V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j),V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j)
with respect to the
the bases
\omega
consisting of the vectors
\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[j]
and
\eta_{f}^{\beta}[j]
. For this reason, we will omit
\omega
from notation and write
\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{p}(T_{f}(j)),

\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{p}(T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j)),

\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{p}(T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))
for the corresponding Tamagawa numbers. We remark that

\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)}(j))=\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{p,\mathrm{can}}(T_{f}^{(\star)}(j))\cdot\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})

where the canonical Tamagawa numbers
\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{p,\mathrm{can}}
are as in §
1.1.7
.

Proposition 3.17
.

Under the assumptions (

p

Bei)
) and (
S)
), the following formulae hold true for all
j\geqslant k
:

\displaystyle\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))=\frac{R_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}_{p}(T^{(\beta)}_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}/T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))}\cdot\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f}(j)),

\displaystyle\#\Sha_{\beta}(T_{f}(j))=\frac{R_{\beta}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}_{p}(T^{(\alpha)}_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\alpha)}/T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))}\cdot\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f}(j)).

Proof.

We have, by the definitions of the relevant objects, that

(3.26)

\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}+H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]=\frac{R_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}_{p}(T^{(\beta)}_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}/T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))}.

The first formula follows from Lemma
3.13
and the proof of the second formula is entirely analogous.
∎

3.2.5.
The central critical scenario

The case
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0

In this section, we assume that
k
is even and study the Selmer groups
H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}(k/2))
. Recall that
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))
,
and that

\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)\geqslant 1\quad\iff\quad z(f,\xi,k/2)\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2)).

Assume that
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0.
Then
z(f,\xi,k/2)\notin H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2)),

and the situation is very similar with the non-central scenario
studied in Section
3.2.4
. Namely, one has

H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))=\{0\}.

By Proposition
3.11
,
we have an isomorphism

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(k/2))\simeq\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))}\,.

Therefore
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(k/2))
is the one-dimensional vector
space generated by
z(f,\xi,k/2).
We expect that the condition (
S)
)
holds in this case, namely that
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(k/2))=\{0\}
. Note that it is equivalent to the
condition

z(f,\xi,k/2)\notin H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(k/2)).

In particular, (
S)
) implies that
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(k/2))\neq 0.

The case
\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=1

In the remainder of Section
3.2.5
we assume that

L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})=0.
First we turn our attention to the case
\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=1.

Throughout §
3.2.5
–§
3.2.5
, let us denote by
c_{\rm Iw}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
either the Beilinson–Kato class if
f
does not have CM, or the collection of (twisted) elliptic units
1
1

1

We note if
f
has CM, the associated Galois representation in general fails the big image hypothesis (condition 12.5.2 in
[
Kat04
]
; see also Remark 12.8 in op. cit.) that is required to employ the Euler system machinery with the Beilinson–Kato classes. That is the reason why we need to appeal to the elliptic unit Euler system in this case (as Kato also did in
[
Kat04
]
, §15). See also
[
LLZ13
, Theorem 2.28]
for a comparison of elliptic units and Beilinson–Kato classes.
along the cyclotomic tower if
f
has CM. Recall that conjecturally, any eigenform that admits a
\theta
-critical
p
-stabilization has CM. Therefore, conjecturally, we are always in the latter scenario, but we will not rely on this expectation in what follows. Let us put
{\rm pr}_{0}(c_{\mathrm{Iw}})=:c\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
.
The following assertion is often referred to as Perrin-Riou conjecture:

PR)

\textup{res}_{p}(c)\neq 0
.

Note that (
PR)
) holds in the scenario when
\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=0
thanks to Kato’s work
[
Kat04
]
.

Remark 3.18
.

The hypothesis (
PR)
) holds true when
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=1
and
f=f_{A}
is the newform attached to an elliptic curve
A/\mathbb{Q}
by Eichler and Shimura, thanks to the results
[
BDV22
,
BPS
]
on Perrin-Riou’s conjecture and the finiteness of
\Sha_{A/\mathbb{Q}}[p^{\infty}]
(thanks to Kolyvagin) in this scenario.

When
k>2
(but still
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=1
), the main results of
[
BDV22
,
BPS
]
allow us to relate (
PR)
) to the non-vanishing of the image of a Heegner cycle (which is a non-trivial element of
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
, thanks to Gross–Zagier–Zhang) under the map
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
. This problem can be recast in terms of the injectivity of a
p
-adic Abel–Jacobi map, which seems to be presently out of reach.

Thanks to Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna
[
BDP12
]
and Kato
[
Kat04
, §15]
, the condition (
PR)
) holds if
\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)\leq 1
and

CM)

f
has complex multiplication (as it is conjectured by Greenberg to be always the case).

When
k=2
, Zhang
[
Zha14
]
shows (extending the earlier works of Serre
[
Ser68
]
, Emerton
[
Eme97
]
and Ghate
[
Gha04
]
) that
f
has CM. In particular, the condition (
PR)
) holds in this particular case whenever
\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)\leq 1
.

We denote by

(3.27)

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\star}:\quad H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))\otimes H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}(k/2))\longrightarrow E,\qquad\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}

the
p
-adic height pairing associated to the splitting submodules
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\star)}{(\frac{k}{2})})\subset{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
and

{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}}^{(\star)}{(\frac{k}{2})})\subset{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f^{*}}(\frac{k}{2}))

in the sense of
[
Ben20
]
.
Note that

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\beta}:\quad H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))\otimes H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))\longrightarrow E

coincides with the classical
p
-adic height pairing defined in
[
Nek93
,
PR92
]
.

We begin our analysis of the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))
at the trivial character when
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=1
.

Proposition 3.19
.

Suppose that
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=1
and assume that the hypothesis (
PR)
) holds. Then:

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=1
and

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2})).

H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=\{0\}.

G_{\frac{k}{2}}(0)\neq 0
.

Assume in addition that one of the conditions (
SZ1)
) and (
SZ2)
) holds true.

SZ1)

f
has CM (conjecturally, this is always the case) by an imaginary quadratic field
K
,
\mu_{p^{\infty}}(K)=\{1\}
and
p
does not divide the order of the torsion subgroup of the ray group of
K
modulo
\mathfrak{f}
, where
\mathfrak{f}
is the conductor of the Hecke character attached to
f
.

SZ2)

f
does not have CM (conjecturally, this never happens),
k\equiv 2\pmod{p-1}
, the residual representation
T_{f}/\mathfrak{m}_{E}T_{f}
is irreducible, and either there exists a prime
q||N
such that the inertia at
q
acts non-trivially on
T_{f}/\mathfrak{m}_{E}T_{f}
, or there exists a real quadratic field
F
verifying the conditions of
[
Wan15
, Theorem 4]
.

Then
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))
has a simple pole at the trivial character if and only the
p
-adic height pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\beta}
is nonzero.

We remark that the assumptions (
SZ1)
) and (
SZ1)
) are to ensure the validity of the
slope-zero
main conjecture for
f
; cf.
[
SU14
,
Wan15
]
,
[
Ski16
, Theorem 2.5.2]
,
[
Kat04
, Theorem 15.2]
.

Proof.

The assertion in (ii) that
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=0
follows from the Euler system machinery and our running assumption (
PR)
). Moreover, since we have
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=0
, it follows that the natural map

(3.28)

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{\beta}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))

is an injection. Since
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\beta}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))=1
and
c\in H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))
is a nonzero element, it follows that the map (
3.28
) is an isomorphism. This shows that
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=1
. The fact that
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=0
and the Euler–Poincaré characteristic formula show that
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=1
as well. Hence, the natural inclusion

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))

is an equality. Thence,

(3.29)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))&=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}\right)\\
&=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}\right)\\
&=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))\cap H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}\right)\\
&=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{\beta}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))\cap H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}\right)\\
&=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))=0\,,\end{aligned}

and this completes the proof of Parts (i) and (ii).

We next prove (iii). Let
{\bf z}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\setminus(\gamma-1)\cdot H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
be any element and let
z:={\rm pr}_{0}({\bf z})\in H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
denote its image. The formulae (
1.23
) show that

(3.30)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \textup{res}_{p}(z)=(-1)^{\frac{k}{2}}(k/2-1)!\left(\frac{1-p^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\alpha^{-1}}{1-p^{-\frac{k}{2}}\alpha}\right)\,&\,F_{\frac{k}{2}}(0)\cdot(\exp^{*})^{-1}\left(\eta_{f}^{\alpha}[{k}/{2}]\right)\\
&+({k}/{2}-1)!\left(\frac{1-p^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\beta^{-1}}{1-p^{-\frac{k}{2}}\beta}\right)G_{\frac{k}{2}}(0)\cdot\exp\left(\eta_{f}^{\beta}[{k}/{2}]\right).\end{aligned}

Using Equation (
3.30
) with
\mathbf{z}=c_{\mathrm{Iw}}
and combining with the fact that
H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=\{0\}
from (ii) (which tells us that
\textup{res}_{p}(c)
cannot fall inside
H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
), we infer that
G_{\frac{k}{2}}(0)\neq 0
, as required.

It remains to prove (iv). In view of Part (iii) and the definition of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
, we only need to check that
F_{\frac{k}{2}}
has a simple zero at the trivial character.

By (
3.23
) and noting in our setting that
F_{\frac{k}{2}}=A\cdot F^{\rm BK}_{\frac{k}{2}}
for some
A\in\Lambda
coprime to
(\gamma-1)
(where
F^{\rm BK}_{\frac{k}{2}}={\rm Tw}_{-\frac{k}{2}}F^{\rm BK}_{k}
and
F^{\rm BK}_{k}
is given as in §
3.2.3
), this is equivalent to checking that the slope-zero
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
has a simple zero at
\chi^{\frac{k}{2}}
under our running hypotheses.
Our argument below to check this is based on the theory we develop
in this book, and we refer the reader to
[
PR92
]
for a more classical
treatment.

In
[
PR95
, Section 2.4.7]
, it is explained that in the slope-zero case, the Main Conjecture in the formulation of Perrin-Riou (cf. Conjecture
3.32
below) is equivalent to the classical Iwasawa Main Conjecture of Mazur and Greenberg. The latter conjecture is proved in
[
Rub91
,
Kat04
,
SU14
,
Ski16
,
Wan15
]
. Therefore, the order of vanishing of
L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
at
k/2
is equal to the order
r(M)
of the zero at
\gamma_{1}-1
of the characteristic polynomial of the Iwasawa module
M:=\mathbf{R}^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)
given as in Section
3.3
. Recall that
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))=1
under our assumptions. Combining
Lemma
3.39
and Theorem
3.44
below (which do not rely on the present proposition), we deduce that the height pairing is non-zero (in the setting of our proposition) if and only if
r(M)=1
, as required.

∎

Corollary 3.20
.

Suppose that
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)=1
and the condition (
PR)
) holds. Then the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})
is nonzero.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Proposition
3.19
(iii).
∎

Corollary 3.21
.

Suppose that
f=f_{A}
is the newform attached to an elliptic curve
A/\mathbb{Q}
. If
{\rm ord}_{s=1}L(A,s)=1
, then the assertions (i)–(iii) of Proposition
3.19
hold unconditionally. Moreover, the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))
is nonzero and has a simple pole at the trivial character.

Proof.

If
f=f_{A}
, then the condition (
PR)
) holds (see Remark
3.18
). Since
f
admits a
\theta
-critical
p
-stabilization, then by a result of Serre
[
Ser68
]
, the elliptic curve
A
has complex multiplication. In this case, Bertrand in
[
Ber84
]
proved that for any
P\in E(\mathbb{Q})
of infinite order, its
p
-adic height
\langle P,P\rangle_{\beta}
is nonzero. This shows that the hypothesis on the non-triviality of the
p
-adic height pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\beta}
in Proposition
3.19
(iv) is readily verified in this scenario. We remark that the slope-zero main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

was proved by Rubin, cf.
[
Rub92
, Theorem 12.4]
when
p>2
.
∎

The case
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)>1

We next study the Iwasawa theoretic
\mathscr{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))
at the trivial character when
{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)>1
. As we shall see in Proposition
3.23
, our conclusions in this scenario are conditional on various folklore (but very difficult) conjectures. This, of course, parallels the very gloomy state of affairs concerning the Bloch–Kato conjectures when the analytic rank is strictly greater than
1
. We will rely on the following notion of semi-simplicity (see also §
3.5.1
for an extensive discussion):

Definition 3.22
.

A
\Lambda(\Gamma_{1})
-module
M
is called semi-simple at
(\gamma_{1}-1)
if its localization at
(\gamma_{1}-1)
is semi-simple as a
\Lambda(\Gamma_{1})_{(\gamma_{1}-1)}
-module.

Proposition 3.23
.

Suppose that
r_{\mathrm{an}}:={\rm ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f^{*},s)>1
. Assume also that

BK)

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=r_{\mathrm{an}}
(Bloch–Kato conjecture) ,

\Sha
)

the map
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(k/2))\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))
is not the zero map ,

ht)

the
p
-adic height pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\beta}:\quad H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))\otimes H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(k/2))\longrightarrow E

is non-degenerate.

Then:

We have

H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}({k}/{2})),\quad\hbox{ and }\quad H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}({k}/{2})).

Moreover,
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
have rank
r_{\mathrm{an}}-1
.

Assume in addition that either (
SZ1)
) or else (
SZ2)
) holds true. Then:

c_{\rm Iw}\in(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}-1}\cdot H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\setminus(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}}\cdot H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\,.

Let
d_{\mathrm{Iw}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))

denote the unique element with
c_{\mathrm{Iw}}=(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}-1}d_{\mathrm{Iw}}
and let us put
\partial c:={\rm pr}_{0}(d_{\mathrm{Iw}})\in H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
to denote the image of
d_{\mathrm{Iw}}
. Then
0\neq\partial c\in H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(k/2))
.

Proof.

Since
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))=1
, our running assumption (

X

)
) shows that the map

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(k/2))\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))

is surjective. Since
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))
is the kernel of this map, it follows that

(3.31)

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(k/2))-1\,.

We also have

(3.32)

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(k/2))-1\,.

by the global Euler–Poincaré characteristic formula. Combining (
3.31
) and (
3.32
), we deduce that the natural containment
H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\subseteq H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
is indeed an equality. This proves the first asserted equality. We may now argue as in (
3.29
) and prove the second claimed equality. The final assertion follows from (
3.31
) and the second asserted equality, combined with our running hypothesis (
BK)
).

Let
a
be the natural number such that

c_{\rm Iw}\in(\gamma_{1}-1)^{a}\cdot H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\setminus(\gamma_{1}-1)^{a+1}\cdot H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\,.

Note that such
a
uniquely exists since
c_{\rm Iw}
is nonzero. It follows from the validity of the slope-zero main conjectures for
f
(thanks to our assumption that either (
SZ1)
) or (
SZ2)
) holds; cf.
[
Kat04
, Theorem 15.2]
if
f
has CM, or
[
SU14
,
Wan15
]
,
[
Ski16
, Theorem 2.5.2]
otherwise) that

(3.33)

{\rm char}\left(H^{2}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\right)\subset(\gamma_{1}-1)^{a}\Lambda_{E}\setminus(\gamma_{1}-1)^{a+1}\Lambda_{E}\,.

Moreover, since
H^{2}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
is a quotient of the Iwasawa theoretic Greenberg Selmer group, which is semi-simple at
(\gamma_{1}-1)
thanks to our running assumption (
ht)
) (cf.
[
PR92
]
; see also Theorem
3.44
(iii) below), it follows that
H^{2}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))
is semi-simple at
(\gamma_{1}-1)
as well. This fact combined with (
3.33
) shows that

H^{2}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\simeq H^{2}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\Gamma}\simeq E^{a}\,.

On the other hand, we have

a=\dim_{E}H^{2}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))=r_{\mathrm{an}}-1

by global duality and Part (i). This shows that
a=r_{\rm an}-1
, as required.

It follows from our running assumptions (
BK)
) and (
ht)
) combined with the truth of Iwasawa main conjectures that

(3.34)

e_{\mathds{1}}{\rm Tw}_{\frac{k}{2}}L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\in(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}}\Lambda_{E}\setminus(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}+1}\Lambda_{E}\,,

where
e_{\mathds{1}}=\frac{1}{|\Delta|}\sum_{\delta\in\Delta}\delta
is the idempotent corresponding to the trivial character of
\Delta
. It follows from
Proposition
2.20
and the definition of
\partial c
that

0\equiv e_{\mathds{1}}{\rm Tw}_{\frac{k}{2}}{L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})}\equiv C\cdot{\left(\exp^{*}_{V_{f}(\frac{k}{2})}(\textup{res}_{p}(\partial c)),\eta_{f^{*}}^{\beta}[k/2]\right)_{V_{f}(\frac{k}{2})}}(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}-1}\mod(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}}

for some explicit
C\in E^{\times}
whose exact form we do not need here. This shows that
\partial c\in H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
, as required.

Moreover, since
c_{\mathrm{Iw}}\notin(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
, it also follows that
\partial c\neq 0
.
∎

Remark 3.24
.

We comment on the hypotheses in Proposition
3.23
.
When
f=f_{E}
is the newform attached to an elliptic curve
E/\mathbb{Q}
, then the hypothesis (

X

)
) follows from the finiteness of
\Sha_{E/\mathbb{Q}}[p^{\infty}]
(which is unknown in this level of generality) combined with (
BK)
).

We consider the following higher-rank version of the
Perrin-Riou conjecture:

h-PR)

\textup{res}_{p}(\partial c)\neq 0
(higher-rank Perrin-Riou conjecture)

Note that (
h-PR)
) implies (

X

)
).

Proposition 3.25
.

Assume that the conditions (
BK)
),
(
ht)
) and (
h-PR)
) hold.

We have

(3.35)

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\oplus E\cdot\partial c\,.

In particular,

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\oplus H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}.

G_{\frac{k}{2}}(0)\neq 0
.

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))
has a simple pole at the trivial character.

Proof.

The proof of (
3.35
) is immediate whenever (
h-PR)
) holds, since
\partial c\in H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\setminus H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
. As the first Iwasawa cohomology
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
is a free
\Lambda_{E}
-module of rank one, the
E
-vector space of coinvariants
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))_{\Gamma}
is one-dimensional, and therefore it is generated by
\partial c
in that case.

Let
\mathbf{z}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
be a generator and let us put
z:=\mathrm{pr}_{0}({\bf z})\in H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
as before. Suppose on the contrary that
G_{\frac{k}{2}}(0)=0
. Then
\textup{res}_{p}(z)\in H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
, or equivalently,
z\in H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
where the second equality has been verified in Proposition
3.23
. This shows that

(3.36)

\textup{res}_{p}(z)=0\,.

Let
d_{\mathrm{Iw}}
be as in the statement of Proposition
3.23
. Since, by definitions, we have
d_{\mathrm{Iw}}=A\cdot\mathbf{z}
for some
A\in\Lambda
such that
e_{\mathds{1}}A\in\Lambda(\Gamma_{1})
is coprime to
(\gamma_{1}-1)
, it follows from (
3.36
) that
\textup{res}_{p}(\partial z)=0
, contrary to our running assumption (
h-PR)
). This shows that
G_{\frac{k}{2}}(0)\neq 0
, as required.

In view of Part (ii) and the definition of
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
, we only need to check that
F_{\frac{k}{2}}
has a simple zero at the trivial character. Let
\mathbf{z}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
be a generator as above. Since
c_{\mathrm{Iw}}=A\cdot(\gamma_{1}-1)^{r_{\mathrm{an}}-1}\cdot\mathbf{z}
for some
A\in\Lambda
with
e_{\mathds{1}}A
coprime to
(\gamma_{1}-1)
, it follows from (
3.23
) that this requirement is equivalent to (
3.34
), which we have verified under our running assumptions.
∎

Tate–Shafarevich groups

We next study the Tate–Shafarevich groups at the central critical point. The exact sequence in the statement of Proposition
3.11
reads

(3.37)

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))}\longrightarrow\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}\right)^{\vee}\longrightarrow 0\,.

We first consider the case

a)

H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\neq H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
. In this scenario, by dimension considerations, the map

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}({k}/{2}))}

is surjective and it follows from the exact sequence (
3.37
) that

(3.38)

{\rm rank}\,H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))^{\vee}={\rm rank}\,H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))^{\vee}\,.

Moreover, it follows using
[
MR04
, Lemma 4.1.1]
and (
3.38
) that

H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\,.

Therefore,

\Sha_{\star}(T_{f}^{*}({k}/{2}))=\mathrm{coker}\left(H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\right).

This fact plugged in the exact sequence (
3.37
) yields (once again, relying on the fact that the quotient
\dfrac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}
is torsion-free):

(3.39)

\#\Sha_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))=\#\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\cdot\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}+\frac{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}}{H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}}\right]^{-1}.

The identity (
3.39
) can be thought of as an analogue of that in Lemma
3.13
(ii).

We now consider the case

b)

H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
. In this scenario, the exact sequence (
3.37
) reduces to an isomorphism

(3.40)

\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))}\xrightarrow{\sim}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}\right)^{\vee}

of free
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-modules of rank one. This in particular shows that

(3.41)

{\rm rank}\,H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))^{\vee}={\rm rank}\,H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))^{\vee}+1\,.

which in turn shows, using
[
MR04
, Lemma 4.1.1]
, that

(3.42)

\dim\,H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))=\dim\,H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))+1\,.

On passing to Pontryagin duals in (
3.40
) and using local Tate duality, we obtain the exact sequence

(3.43)

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow 0\,

and in turn the commutative diagram

(3.44)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 157.33705pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 261.4657pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern where the surjection on the first row at the very right is a consequence of (
3.42
) together with the fact that
\dim H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))=1
, and the vertical surjection on the very right follows from the definitions.

Using the snake lemma with the diagram (
3.44
), we deduce that

(3.45)

\#\Sha_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}(k/2))\,=\,\#\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k/2))\cdot\left[H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\rm tf}:\frac{H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\rm tf}}{H^{1}_{0}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\rm tf}}\right]^{-1}\,.

Remark 3.26
.

In view of our analysis in Propositions
3.19

and
3.23
, we expect that

•

H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
if and only if
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})=0
,

•

H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))\neq H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
in all cases.

3.3.
Modules of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions

3.3.1.
Selmer complexes for modular forms

In this subsection, we define Perrin-Riou’s modules of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions associated to the Hecke eigenvalues
\alpha
and
\beta
, using the formalism of Selmer complexes as in
[
Ben14
]
. The reader will notice that the general constructions could be greatly simplified and made more explicit for the slope-
0
eigenvalue
\beta
. However, for our further purposes, it is important to present a coherent picture allowing the unified treatment of both cases, working with abstract objects with finer functorial properties.

Let us fix an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice
T_{f}
of
V_{f}
and for
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
, let us set
D^{(\star)}[j]:={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\star)}(j))
and denote by
N_{\star}[j]\subset D^{(\star)}[j]
the
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice generated by
\eta_{f}^{\star}[j].
We consider the following local conditions
\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j),\star)\right)_{\ell\in S}
on the level of complexes:

•

For all
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}
, we shall take the unramified local condition (cf. (
1.33
)):

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j),\star):=\left[(V_{f}(j)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Lambda)^{I_{\ell}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Fr}_{\ell}-1}(V_{f}(j)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\Lambda)^{I_{\ell}}\right]\,.

Recall that

\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j),\star)=\begin{cases}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j))&\text{if $i\neq 1,$}\\
0&\text{if $i\neq 1.$}\end{cases}

•

The local condition at
p
is given by the complex

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j),\star):=D^{(\star)}[j]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda[-1]

concentrated in degree
1
, together with the morphism
(
1.22
)

\mathbf{Exp}_{\star,j}\,:\,D^{(\star)}[j]\otimes\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\,.

Here, we have used the quasi-isomorphism

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\simeq\left[\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}(V_{f}(j))\xrightarrow{\psi-1}\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}(V_{f}(j))\right]

to define the exponential map on the level of complexes.

We denote by
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)

and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)
the Selmer complexes
associated to the diagrams

(3.46)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 32.06229pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.06229pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.37187pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.37187pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j)))}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 56.06229pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j),\alpha)\,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.59456pt\raise-15.50012pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 32.06229pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.06229pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.2237pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.2237pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j)))}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 56.06229pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j),\beta)\,\,.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.44638pt\raise-15.50012pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

We remark that, from the point of view of Perrin-Riou’s theory
[
PR95
]
, the complex

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)
is the algebaric counterpart of the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*}).
In Section
3.4
, we will see that
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)

is naturally an algebraic counterpart of the cyclotomic improvement
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
of the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
. One may also introduce “a non-improved version” of this complex on replacing
\mathbf{Exp}_{\alpha,j}
by the map

\mathbf{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j),j}\,:\,D^{(\alpha)}{[j]}\otimes\Lambda\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)

and considering the Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
of
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
-modules
associated to the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(V_{f}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\bigl(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j)))\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\bigr)}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\bigl(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j),\alpha)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\bigr)\,\,,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

in which the component at
p
of the vertical map is
\mathbf{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j),j}.

Recall that the maps
\mathbf{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j),j}
and
\mathbf{Exp}_{\alpha,j}

are related by the formula

(3.47)

\mathbf{Exp}_{V_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j),j}=\ell(V_{f}(j))\cdot\mathbf{Exp}_{\alpha,j}\,,\qquad\qquad\ell(V_{f}(j))=\underset{i=1-k+j}{\overset{j-1}{\prod}}\ell_{i}.

For
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
, set
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})=|b|,
where
b\in E^{*}
is such that
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(T_{f}^{(\star)})={b}N_{\star}
(cf. Section
1.1.7
). If we choose

T_{f}
such that
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*}
, then thanks to (
1.22
), we have an integral version of the diagrams
(
3.46
):

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 31.65083pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-31.65083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.96042pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.96042pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j)))}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 55.65083pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\alpha)\,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 117.77165pt\raise-15.50012pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 31.65083pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-31.65083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.81224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.81224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j)))}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 55.65083pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\beta)\,\,.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 119.62347pt\raise-15.50012pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Whenever
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*}
, we let
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)
and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)
denote the Selmer complexes associated to these diagrams.

The basic properties of these complexes are summarized in the following:

Proposition 3.27
.

The
\Lambda
-module

\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j)):=\ker\left(H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw},S}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\right)

is finitely generated and
\Lambda
-torsion.

Assume that the condition (
S)
) holds true for some integer
j=j_{0}
, and let
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
. Then the following statements are valid:

For all
j\in\mathbb{Z},
the map

f_{\star}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\star)}(j))\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}\,+\,\iota_{\star}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))

is injective. Here,
\iota_{\star}
is the morphism induced from the natural injection
T_{f}^{(\star)}\to T_{f}
.

\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)=\{0\}=\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)
if
i\neq 2,3.

The
\Lambda
-modules
\mathbf{R}^{2}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)
and
\mathbf{R}^{2}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)

sit in the exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow 0\,,

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,j}(N_{\beta}[j]\otimes\Lambda)}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\longrightarrow\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow 0\,.

\mathbf{R}^{3}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\simeq H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))^{\wedge}\simeq\mathbf{R}^{3}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\,.

The analogous properties hold for the complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta).
In addition,

\mathbf{R}^{3}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)=\{0\}=\mathbf{R}^{3}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\,.

Proof.

This statement is proved in
[
Kat04
, Theorem 12.4]
.

For each
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
, we have

\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\right)\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\star)}(j))=\{0\}

inside
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
. Indeed, this claim holds unconditionally if
\star=\beta
, and when
\star=\alpha
, it follows from condition (
S)
) (cf. Section
3.2.3
).
By
[
Kat04
, Theorem 12.4]
,
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))
is a torsion-free
\Lambda
-module. Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\star)}(j))
is a free
\Lambda
-module of rank one, we deduce that

\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\right)\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\star)}(j))=\{0\}.

This is a restatement of (ii).

These portions follow easily from earlier remarks; cf.
[
Ben14
, Theorem 4]
for similar arguments. For the reader’s convenience, we reproduce these arguments of op. cit. below.

It follows from the definition of a Selmer complex that we have an exact triangle

\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))[-1]\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\alpha)\right).

The associated long exact sequence of cohomology reads

(3.48)

\begin{aligned} \ldots\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{i-1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow H^{i}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\alpha)\\
\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{i}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\ldots\end{aligned}

Thanks to this exact sequence, it is clear that

\mathbf{R}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)=0
. Moreover,

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)\rightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\right)\subset\ker(f_{\alpha})=0

by Part (ii) of our proposition.

Note that for all
\ell\neq p
, we have

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}})/\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},(\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}T_{f}(j)^{I_{\ell}})^{\iota})=:\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\alpha),

where
I_{\ell}
denotes the inertia group at
\ell
(cf.
[
PR92
]
, Section 2.2.4). Using the sequence (
3.48
) with
i=2
, we obtain the exact sequence

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\,.

We therefore conclude that the induced sequence

0\longrightarrow\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Finally, using (
3.48
) with
i=3
, we have

H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{3}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow 0\,.

On comparing this exact sequence with the Poitou–Tate exact sequence,
we deduce that

\mathbf{R}^{3}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\simeq H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))^{\wedge}.

The proof of these facts for other complexes is completely analogous.
We also note that

\mathbf{R}^{3}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\star)=H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),V_{f^{*}}(k-j))^{*}=0

because
V_{f^{*}}^{(\alpha^{*})}\simeq E(\nu_{\alpha^{*}}\chi^{1-k})
and
V_{f^{*}}^{(\beta^{*})}\simeq E(\nu_{\beta^{*}})
with
non-trivial
unramified characters
\nu_{\alpha^{*}}
and
\nu_{\beta^{*}}.
This completes the proof of our proposition.
∎

3.3.2.
Projective resolutions and determinants

In this subsection, we recall some basic facts about
projective resolutions over the rings
\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]],

\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]][1/p]
, and
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1}),
which will be used in the rest of the book.

Let
R
be an integral domain. Throughout, projective
R
-module always means a
finitely generated
projective
R
-module. Recall that an
R
-module
M
is perfect if there exists a finite projective resolution
P_{\bullet}\rightarrow M\rightarrow 0
of
M
. If
P_{\bullet}=(P_{i})_{i\geqslant 0}
is finite, its length
is defined as the largest
i
such that
P_{i}\neq 0
.

Every finitely generated module over a principal ideal domain is perfect and admits a projective resolution of length
1
.
If
R
is a regular local ring of dimension
d,
then, by a classical theorem of Serre (cf.
[
Mat89
]
, §19) every finitely generated
R
-module admits a projective resolution of length
d
.
In particular, every finitely generated module over
\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]]
admits a projective resolution of length
2
.

An integral domain is a Bézout ring if every finitely generated ideal of
R
is principal. It follows immediately From this definition that a noetherian Bézout ring is principal. An archetypal example of a non-principal Bézout ring is the ring of analytic functions on the open unit disc over a complete non-archimedean field (cf.
[
Laz62
]
). In particular,
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
is a Bézout ring.

Some results about finitely generated modules over a principal ideal domain extend, with some modifications, to modules over a Bézout ring. For example, every finitely generated torsion-free module over a Bézout ring
R
is free (cf.
[
Kap49
]
). Recall that an
R
-module is finitely presented if it is isomorphic to a quotient of the form
F/N
, where
F
and
N
are finitely generated and
F
is free. As a result, we deduce that every finitely presented module over a Bézout ring admits a free resolution of length
1
.
We need a very slight extension of this fact.

Lemma 3.28
.

Every finitely presented
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
-module admits a projective resolution of length
1
.

Proof.

Recall the canonical decomposition (
1.1
):

\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)=\underset{\eta\in X(\Delta)}{\oplus}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})_{\eta}\,.

Note that
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})_{\eta}=\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})\delta_{\eta}
are isomorphic to
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
as rings.
Correspondingly, any
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
-module
M
decomposes into a direct sum

M=\underset{\eta\in X(\Delta)}{\oplus}M^{(\eta)},

where each
M^{(\eta)}
is an
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)_{\eta}
-module. Since an
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
-module
P
is projective if and only if
P^{(\eta)}
is
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)_{\eta}
-projective for all
\eta\in X(\Delta),
the proof of our lemma follows from the case of Bézout rings.
∎

Remark 3.29
.

The same argument proves the well-known fact that any finitely generated
\Lambda
-module admits a projective resolution of length
2
.

In this subsection, we recall for the convenience of the reader the trivialization of the determinant of a torsion module over an integral domain, and refer to
[
KM76
]
for the proofs and further details.

Let
R
be a domain and let
Q(R)
denote its field of fractions. Let
C^{\bullet}
be a perfect complex of
R
-modules. Assume that
C^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}Q(R)
is acyclic. Suppose that
P^{\bullet}
is a complex of projective modules quasi-isomorphic to the perfect complex
C^{\bullet}.
Then
P^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}Q(R)
is acyclic, and therefore has a canonical trivialization

{\det}_{Q(R)}^{-1}\left(P^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}Q(R)\right)\xrightarrow{\,\,\sim\,\,}Q(R).

(We work with
{\det}^{-1}
rather than with
\det
. This is because
{\det}^{-1}
is a natural generalization of the characteristic polynomial of an Iwasawa module.)
Composing this isomorphism with the canonical map

{\det}^{-1}_{R}(C^{\bullet})\simeq{\det}^{-1}_{R}(P^{\bullet})\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}{\det}^{-1}_{Q(R)}(P^{\bullet}\otimes Q(R))

we obtain a trivialization

(3.49)

\vartheta_{C^{\bullet}}\,:\,{\det}^{-1}_{R}(C^{\bullet})\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}Q(R).

Note that if
P^{\prime\bullet}
is another complex of projective modules
quasi-isomorphic to
C^{\bullet},
we then have a diagram

\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{Q(R)}\bigl(P^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}Q(R)\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}\simeq}
\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{R}(C^{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q(R)\,.}
\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{Q(R)}\bigl(P^{\prime\bullet}\otimes_{R}Q(R)\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The triangle on the left of this diagram commutes tautologically, and the commutativity of the triangle on the right follows easily from the definition
of the trivialization map
[
KM76
, pp. 32–34]
.
Therefore,
\vartheta=\vartheta_{C^{\bullet}}
does not depend on the choice of
P^{\bullet}
.

We need the following partial generalization of the
previous construction to the case of an arbitrary ring
R
(is not necessarily an integral domain). Let
M
be a torsion
R
-module that admits a projective resolution
P_{\bullet}=(P_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}P_{0})
of length one by finitely generated
free
modules of rank
r
. The map
\det{(\phi)}\,:\,\wedge^{r}P_{1}\rightarrow\wedge^{r}P_{0}
induces a map

{\det}_{R}^{-1}\left(P_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}P_{0}\right)={\det}_{R}(P_{1})\otimes{\det}_{R}^{-1}({P}_{0})\xrightarrow{\det(\phi)}{R},

which we also denote by
\det(\phi).
The arguments of
[
KM76
]
apply verbatim and show that the composition

(3.50)

\vartheta_{M}\,:\,{\det}^{-1}_{R}(M)\simeq{\det}^{-1}_{R}(P_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}P_{0})\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}R

does not depend on the choice of
P_{\bullet}
. If
R
is an integral domain,

\vartheta_{M}
coincides with the map (
3.49
)
where
M
is viewed as a complex concentrated in degree
0.

3.3.3.
Modules of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let us assume that the hypothesis (
S)
) holds true for some choice of
j=j_{0}
. Then by Proposition
3.27
, the cohomology of the complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)

are
\Lambda_{E}
-torsion, and we have injective maps

i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha),\mathrm{imp}}\,:\,{\det}_{\Lambda_{E}}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{E}\,,\qquad i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}\,:\,{\det}_{\Lambda_{E}}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\hookrightarrow\Lambda_{E}

for every integer
j
.
The Iwasawa theoretic version of the fundamental line of
T_{f}(j)
associated to
local conditions
\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\star)\right)_{\ell\in S}

is given as follows, cf.
[
PR95
,
Ben14
]
:

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\star}[j]):={\det}^{-1}_{\Lambda}\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\bigoplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\star))\right)\right)\otimes{\det}_{\Lambda}\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\right).

It follows from definitions that we have canonical morphisms

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha),\mathrm{imp}}\,:\,\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])\hookrightarrow{\det}_{\Lambda_{E}}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha),

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}\,:\,\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j])\hookrightarrow{\det}_{\Lambda_{E}}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta).

Definition 3.30
.

Assume that the hypothesis (
S)
) holds true for some
j=j_{0}
. Then for any integer
j
, the free
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
-modules

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j]):=i^{(\alpha),\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}}\circ\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha),\mathrm{imp}}\bigl(\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])\bigr),

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j]):=i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}\circ\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\bigl(\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j])\bigr)

of rank one are called the modules of (algebraic)
p
-adic
L
-functions.

We remark that the Iwasawa theoretic properties of
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j])
associated to the slope-zero eigenvalue
\beta
are covered by Perrin-Riou’s general theory developed in
[
PR95
]
. In contrast, the module
\mathbf{L}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])
associated to the critical eigenvalue
\alpha
does not fit in the framework of op. cit. and will be studied in the remainder of this section.

We record the first properties of these modules in the following:

Proposition 3.31
.

Assume that
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*}
for

\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}.

The following statements hold true for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, under the validity of (
S)
) for some integer
j=j_{0}
:

One has

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(m),N_{\alpha}[m])=\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(m))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(m))+\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,m}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)}\right)\cdot\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}(\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(m)))\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(m),N_{\beta}[m])=\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(m))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(m))+\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,m}(N_{\beta}[j]\otimes\Lambda)}\right)\cdot\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}(\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(m)))\,.

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(m),N_{\alpha}[m])=\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(m))\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(m),N_{\beta}[m]).

Proof.

This follows immediately from Proposition
3.27
. Note that

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-m))
is finite and hence

\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\mathbf{R}^{3}\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(m),\alpha)\right)=\Lambda=\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\mathbf{R}^{3}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(T_{f}(m),\beta)\right).

It follows from the definition of the Iwasawa theoretic critical
\mathcal{L}
-invariant
and the elementary properties of determinants that

\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(m))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(m))+\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[m]\otimes\Lambda)}\right)=\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(m))\cdot\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(m))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(m))+\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,m}(N_{\beta}[m]\otimes\Lambda)}\right).

This combined with Part (i) proves the asserted equality.
∎

3.4.
Main Conjecture for
\theta
-critical forms

3.4.1.

In §
3.4
, we will always assume that
T_{f}
is chosen so that

\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*},\qquad\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}\,.

The slope-zero (
p
-ordinary) Iwasawa Main Conjecture in the formulation of Perrin-Riou reads:

Conjecture 3.32

(
p
-ordinary Iwasawa Main Conjecture)
.

\mathbf{MC}(f,\beta)

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k),N_{\beta}[k])=(L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota})\,.

Proposition
3.31
allows us to translate

MC

(
f
,
β
)

to the classical version of the slope-zero Iwasawa main conjecture. Furthermore, under the validity of (
SZ1)
) or (
SZ1)
), the conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

holds true; cf.
[
SU14
,
Wan15
]
,
[
Ski16
, Theorem 2.5.2]
,
[
Kat04
, Theorem 15.2]
.

Note that

MC

(
f
,
β
)

is invariant under cyclotomic twists, namely, it is equivalent to the assertion that

i_{\mathrm{Iw},T_{f}(k-j)}^{(\beta)}\circ\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(k-j)}^{(\beta)}\left(\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(k-j),\beta)\right)=(\mathrm{Tw}_{j}\circ L_{\mathrm{S},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}),\qquad\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}\,.

(We recall that
\mathrm{Tw}_{j}\circ F(\gamma-1)^{\iota}:=F(\chi^{j}(\gamma)\gamma-1)^{\iota}
.)

We next formulate the critical Iwasawa Main Conjecture in the style of Perrin-Riou:

Conjecture 3.33

(Critical Iwasawa Main Conjecture)
.

We have
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})\neq 0
and

\mathbf{MC}(f,\alpha)

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k])^{\pm}=\left(\lambda(f^{*})^{\pm}L^{\mathrm{imp},\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right)\,,

where
\lambda(f^{*})^{\pm}
is the constant defined in
Corollary
2.25
.

We may of course rewrite

MC

(
f
,
α
)

in the following equivalent form:

MC

(
f
,
α
)

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k])=\left(L^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right),

where
L^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
is defined in Theorem
2.23
. It is in its original formulation where the
p
-adic
L
-function
L^{[0],\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
, which is constructed in a purely analytic manner, makes an appearance.

Moreover, Proposition
3.31
,
allows to rewrite

MC

(
f
,
α
)

in the following explicit form:

{\rm char}_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(k))+\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,k}(N_{\alpha}[k]\otimes\Lambda)}\right)^{\pm}\cdot\mathrm{char}_{\Lambda}(\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(k)))^{\pm}={\left(\lambda(f^{*})^{\pm}L^{[0],\mathrm{imp},\pm}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\right).}

We may link the main conjectures

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

with one another:

Proposition 3.34
.

Assume that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f})\neq 0
. Then the conjectures

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

are equivalent.

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
2.27
and
3.31
.
∎

We remind the reader that the non-vanishing
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f})\neq 0
of the Iwasawa theoretic critical
\mathcal{L}
-invariant is equivalent to the requirement that the conditions (

p

Bei)
) and (
S)
) holds true for some integer
j=j_{0}
. See §
3.2.2
where we have an extensive discussion on the validity of (
S)
) in the scenario when the eigenform
f
has CM.

Remark 3.35
.

Proposition
3.34

tells us that the Iwasawa theory of the critical stabilization of
f^{*}
is equivalent to that of its ordinary stabilization. In Chapter
4
, we shall formulate a refined version of

MC

(
f
,
α
)

that relates the infinitesimal thickening
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
of the critical
p
-adic
L
-function to the Iwasawa theoretic invariants of the infinitesimal deformation of
V_{f}
along the eigencurve.

3.4.2.

We may formulate the critical Main Conjecture in an equivalent form which involves the non-improved
p
-adic
L
-function

L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{{[0]}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
and the non-improved Selmer complex

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k),\alpha).
Namely, it follows from the identity (
3.47
) (where also the factor
\ell(V_{f}(j))
is defined) that we have a commutative diagram

(3.51)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
