0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\,.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

for the local conditions at
p
.

The diagram (
3.51
) induces a canonical morphism

(3.52)

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\,.

Proposition 3.36
.

Assume that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})\neq 0
.
Then the following statements hold true:

i) We have

(3.53)

\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)=0\quad\textrm{ if $i\neq 2.$}

ii) The
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
-module
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
is a torsion module. Moreover, it is perfect and admits a projective resolution of length
1
.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f})\neq 0
.
Mimicking the proof of Proposition
3.27
, we deduce that

\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)=0
if
i\neq 2
and that
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
is
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
-torsion. Let us consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\otimes\mathscr{H}}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\otimes\mathscr{H}+\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}(j),j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\mathscr{H})}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\otimes\mathscr{H}\longrightarrow 0,

where we write
\otimes\mathscr{H}
in place of
\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
to save space.

For each
\eta\in X(\Delta)
, the
\eta
-isotypic components of
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
,
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
and
\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
are finitely generated modules over the principal ideal domain
\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]][1/p].
Since
N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
is free of rank one, the
\eta
-components of the left and right terms of the exact sequence above are finitely presented and therefore admit projective resolutions of length 1. The behaviour of the projective dimension in short exact sequences (cf.
[
Sta23
, Lemma 10.109.9(ii)]
) tells us that the projective dimension of
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)^{(\eta)}
is bounded by
1
for each
\eta
.
The proposition now follows from Lemma
3.28
.
∎

From Proposition
3.36
, we obtain a canonical trivialization

i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\,:\,{\det}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)

and a canonical map

\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\,:\,\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])\hookrightarrow{\det}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha).

Lemma 3.37
.

Set

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j]):=i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\circ\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\bigl(\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])\bigr).

Then

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])=\ell(V_{f}(j))\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j]).

Proof.

Consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))\otimes\mathscr{H}}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\otimes\mathscr{H}+\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}(j),j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\mathscr{H})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\otimes\mathscr{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\left(\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))+\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)}\right)\otimes\mathscr{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\otimes\mathscr{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\otimes\mathscr{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

The second row of this diagram is the exact sequence computing the second cohomology of
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
(cf. Proposition
3.27
) tensored with
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
. Here, we have written
\otimes\mathscr{H}
in place of
\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
to save space. The first row is the analogous exact sequence for
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha),
and the vertical maps are induced by the morphism (
3.52
). Since

\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}(j),j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes_{E}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma))=\ell(V_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes_{E}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)),

the proof of the lemma follows by taking the determinants in the diagram above.
∎

Therefore, the conjecture

MC

(
f
,
α
)

can be written in the following equivalent form:

(3.54)

\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k])^{\pm}=\left(\lambda(f^{*})^{\pm}L_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right)=\left(L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right),

where
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
is defined in Theorem
2.23
.

3.5.
Iwasawa theoretic descent

In this final section of the present chapter, we develop Iwasawa descent for the modules of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions (cf. Definition
3.30
), which we utilize to prove the leading term formulae for these. Our main results in this regard are Theorem
3.45
, which applies to both non-critical and non-central critical values, and Theorem
3.47
, which focuses on central critical values. We would like to emphasize also Theorem
3.44
, which highlights a significant difference between the modules of
p
-adic
L
-functions associated with the
\theta
-critical
p
-stabilization and that attached to the slope-zero
p
-stabilization. This disparity becomes apparent, for example, in their orders of vanishing at the central critical point (cf. Theorem
3.44
(iv)). Considering the Iwasawa main conjectures

MC

(
f
,
α
)

and

MC

(
f
,
β
)

in conjunction with Proposition
2.27
, this difference can be accounted by a pole of our Iwasawa theoretic
\mathcal{L}
-invariant at the central critical point.

3.5.1.
General formalism

We recall the formalism of Iwasawa descent. To simplify notation, let us set
z=\gamma_{1}-1
. Let
\mathscr{K}(\Gamma_{1})
denote the field of fractions of
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
.

Let
C^{\bullet}
be a perfect complex of
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
-modules.
Assume that
C^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}\mathscr{K}(\Gamma_{1})
is acyclic and let us write

\vartheta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\,\,:\,\,{\det}^{-1}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}C^{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathscr{K}(\Gamma_{1})

for the associated trivialization map (
3.49
). We then have

\vartheta_{\mathrm{Iw}}({\det}^{-1}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}C^{\bullet})=f\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1}),

for some
f\in\mathscr{K}(\Gamma_{1})
. Let
r
be the unique integer such that

z^{-r}f\in\mathscr{H}_{0}(\Gamma_{1})^{\times}
is a unit in the localization
\mathscr{H}_{0}(\Gamma_{1})
of
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
with respect to the principal ideal
z\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
.

Let us define
C^{\bullet}_{0}:=C^{\bullet}\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}E
, where the tensor product is with respect to the augmentation map
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})\to E
.
We have a natural distinguished triangle

C^{\bullet}\xrightarrow{[z]}C^{\bullet}\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}_{0}\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}[1]\,.

In degree
n
, this triangle gives rise to a short exact sequence

(3.55)

0\longrightarrow H^{n}(C^{\bullet})_{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow H^{n}(C^{\bullet}_{0})\longrightarrow H^{n+1}(C^{\bullet})^{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow 0.

We say that
C^{\bullet}
is semi-simple (at the augmentation ideal) if the composition of the natural map

(3.56)

\mathrm{Bock}_{n}\,:\,H^{n}(C^{\bullet})^{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow H^{n}(C^{\bullet})\longrightarrow H^{n}(C^{\bullet})_{\Gamma_{1}}

is an isomorphism for all
n
. If
C^{\bullet}
is semi-simple, then there exists a
canonical trivialisation of
{\det}_{E}C^{\bullet}_{0}
given by

(3.57)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \vartheta_{0}\,\,:\,\,{\det}_{E}^{{-1}}C^{\bullet}_{0}&=\underset{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}{\det}_{E}^{(-1)^{{n-1}}}H^{n}(C_{0}^{\bullet})\\
&\xrightarrow[\eqref{eqn_2022_05_06_1540}]{\sim}\underset{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}\left({\det}^{(-1)^{{n-1}}}_{E}H^{n}(C^{\bullet})_{\Gamma_{1}}\otimes{\det}^{(-1)^{{n-1}}}_{E}H^{n+1}(C^{\bullet})^{\Gamma_{1}}\right)\\
&\xrightarrow{\,\,\,\sim\,\,\,}\underset{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\otimes}\left({\det}^{(-1)^{{n-1}}}_{E}H^{n}(C^{\bullet})_{\Gamma_{1}}\otimes{\det}^{(-1)^{{n}}}_{E}H^{n}(C^{\bullet})^{\Gamma_{1}}\right)\xrightarrow[\eqref{eqn: semisimplicity projection}]{\sim}E\,.\end{aligned}

Note that the complex
C^{\bullet}_{0}
is not necessarily acyclic and that
\vartheta_{0}

differs from the trivialization (
3.49
) in general.

Lemma 3.38
.

Let
C^{\bullet}
be a perfect complex of
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
-modules. Assume that
C^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}\mathscr{K}(\Gamma_{1})
is acyclic as well as that
C^{\bullet}
is semi-simple. Then,

r=\displaystyle\underset{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{\sum}(-1)^{n}\dim_{E}H^{n}(C^{\bullet})^{\Gamma_{1}}\,,

and there exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}C^{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{z^{-r}\vartheta_{\mathrm{Iw}}}
\scriptstyle{\otimes_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}^{\mathbf{L}}E}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}_{0}(\Gamma_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{E}{C^{\bullet}_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta_{0}}
\textstyle{E}

in which the right vertical arrow is the augmentation map.
The same holds for complexes of
\Lambda_{E}
-modules.

Proof.

See
[
BG03
, Lemma 8.1]
. This is a particular case of Nekovář’s
descent theory
[
Nek06
]
. Note that Burns and Greither consider complexes over
\Lambda_{E}
but since
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
is a Bézout ring, all their arguments work in our case and are omitted here.
∎

The following classical result is a particular case of
Lemma
3.38
. Let
M
is a finitely generated torsion
\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]]
-module. Then
M_{E}:=M\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E
is of finite dimension over
E
, and the tautological exact sequence

0\longrightarrow M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow M_{E}\xrightarrow{{[z]}}M_{E}\longrightarrow(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow 0

shows that
\dim_{E}(M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})=\dim_{E}(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}
. In particular,

(3.58)

M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}=\{0\}\,\iff\,(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}=\{0\}\,.

Let
g=g({z})\in\Lambda{(\Gamma_{1})}
be a generator of the characteristic ideal of
M
, and let
r(M)
denote the order of vanishing of the formal power series
g
at
z=0
. Assume that
M_{E}
is semi-simple, i.e. that the natural map

M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}

is an isomorphism. Then,

(3.59)

r(M)=\dim_{E}M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\,,

and

(3.60)

\lim_{s\to 0}\frac{g(\chi(\gamma_{1})^{s}-1)}{s^{r(M)}}\sim_{p}(\log\chi(\gamma_{1}))^{r(M)}\left[M_{\Gamma_{1}}:M^{\Gamma_{1}}\right]\,,

where “
\sim_{p}
” means equality up to multiplication by a
p
-adic unit.

We will repeatedly use these general observations in what follows. The following lemma was used in the proof of Proposition
3.19
.

Lemma 3.39
.

Let
M
be a finitely generated torsion
\Lambda(\Gamma_{1})
-module. Assume that

M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}
and
(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}
are one-dimensional
E
-vector spaces. Then the natural map
M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\rightarrow(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}
is an isomorphism if and only if
r(M)=1.

Proof.

Let
A
denote the
z
-primary component of
M_{E}.

Since
\Lambda_{E}
is principal,
M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}=A^{\Gamma_{1}}
and
(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}=A_{\Gamma_{1}}.

Since
A
is a direct sum of cyclic modules of the form
E[z]/(z^{m}),

from our assumptions if follows immediately that
A\simeq E[z]/(z)

if and only if the map
M_{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\rightarrow(M_{E})_{\Gamma_{1}}

is an isomorphism.
∎

3.5.2.
The non-central case

In this section, we apply Lemma
3.38

to the trivializations

i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha),\mathrm{imp}}\,:\,{\det}^{-1}_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Lambda_{E}\,,\qquad\qquad i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}\,:\,{\det}^{-1}_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\longrightarrow\Lambda_{E},

Let us set

\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}^{\mathbf{L}}E\,,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}^{\mathbf{L}}E\,.

Let us denote by
\mathbf{L}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1})

(resp.
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1})
)
the isotypic component
of the
\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}
-module
\mathbf{L}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j])
(resp.
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j])
) that corresponds to the trivial character
\mathds{1}\in X(\Delta)
.

Let
f_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}({z})\in\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]])
(resp.
f_{\beta}({z})\in\mathrm{Frac}(\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\Gamma_{1}]])
) denote a generator of
\mathbf{L}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1})
(resp.
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1})
) and let
r_{\alpha}(j)
(resp.
r_{\beta}(j)
) denote the order of vanishing of
f_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}({z})
(resp. of
f_{\beta}(z)
) at
z=0
. We set

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp},*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)=\lim_{s\to 0}s^{-r_{\alpha}(j)}f_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(\chi(\gamma_{1})^{s}-1),

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}^{*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0)=\lim_{s\to 0}s^{-r_{\beta}(j)}f_{\beta}(\chi(\gamma_{1})^{s}-1).

These quantities depend on the choices of
f_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}},

f_{\beta},
and
\gamma_{1}
only up to multiplication by a unit in
\mathcal{O}_{E}
.

For
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}
, let us set

(3.61)

\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\star)=\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\star)\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{O}_{E}\,.

Then, assuming that the Selmer complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)
are semi-simple, Lemma
3.38
provides us with a pair of commutative diagrams

(3.62)

This would in turn furnish us with the following interpretation of special values of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions:

(3.63)

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp},*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)=i_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha),\mathrm{imp}}\left(\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\right),

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}^{*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0)=i_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}\left(\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\right).

We now employ this approach in variety of scenarios.

Proposition
3.27
provides us with the following exact
sequences, which will play a key role in the remainder of this chapter:

(3.64)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow 0\,,

(3.65)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\longrightarrow 0\,,

where

(3.66)

{g}_{\star}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\oplus N_{\star}[j]\otimes\Lambda\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}\,+\,\mathrm{Exp}_{\star,j}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j)),\qquad\qquad\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}\,.

Proposition 3.40
.

Suppose that
j\neq k/2
is a strictly positive integer.

Assume that we are in one of the following situations:

a)

1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
.

b)

j\geqslant k
and the conjecture (

p

Bei)
) holds.

Then the following statements hold true:

1)

\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)
is acyclic and
r_{\beta}(j)=0.

2)

The cohomology groups of
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(T_{f}(j),\beta):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{O}_{E}
have finite cardinality, and we have

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}^{*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\frac{\#\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(T_{f}(j),\beta)}{\#\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(T_{f}(j),\beta)}.

Assume that we are in one of the following scenarios:

a)

1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and the condition (
S)
) holds .

b)

j\geqslant k
and the the both conditions (
S)
) and (

p

Bei)
) hold.

Then the statements 1-2) above also hold for the complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha),

\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)
and the module

\mathbf{L}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j]).

In particular,
r_{\alpha}(j)=0
and

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp},*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\frac{\#\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)}{\#\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)}\,.

Proof.

We prove (i), treating the scenarios (a) and (b) separately. Our proof uses two statements well-known to the experts, Lemma
3.42
and Lemma
3.43
, whose proofs are deferred until §
3.5.2
.

Consider the short exact sequence (
3.55
), which reads

0\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{i}(V_{f}(j),\beta)_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{i+1}(V_{f}(j),\beta)^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow 0\,.

in our case.
Under our running assumptions,
\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta){=\{0\}}
if
i\neq 2
. Therefore, we have natural identifications

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)=\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)^{\Gamma}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)=\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)_{\Gamma}.

Let us apply the snake lemma to the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

that we obtain from the exact sequence (
3.65
) tensored with
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, where
\gamma
is a topological generator and the vertical arrows are the multiplication-by-
(\gamma-1)
maps. Noting that, under our assumptions, we have
\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))^{\Gamma}={\{0\}}=\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}
by Lemma
3.43
below, we deduce that

(3.67)

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)^{\Gamma}=\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})^{\Gamma}=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\oplus H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)_{\Gamma}=\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})_{\Gamma}=\mathrm{coker}\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\oplus H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\right).

Using Lemma
3.42
, we infer that

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}=H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\,\,,\qquad H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))_{\Gamma}=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\,.

Hence, (
3.67
) can be recast as

(3.68)

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)^{\Gamma}=\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})^{\Gamma}=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\right),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)_{\Gamma}=\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})_{\Gamma}=\mathrm{coker}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\right).

Since we have
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=0
thanks to our running hypotheses, we deduce that

\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)^{\Gamma}=\{0\}=\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\beta)_{\Gamma}\,.

This completes the proof that the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)
is acyclic.

It follows from the acyclicity of
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)
that
r_{\beta}(j)=0
and the map
i_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}
coincides with the composition of the isomorphisms

{\det}_{E}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigotimes_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}{\det}_{E}^{(-1)^{i-1}}\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\xrightarrow{\sim}E.

Since
\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{i}(T_{f}(j),{\beta})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E=\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{i}(V_{f}(j),{\beta})=\{0\}
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, it follows that the cohomology groups of the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(T_{f}(j),{\beta})
have finite cardinality. The part (i) of the proposition follows from the commutativity of the diagram (
3.62
) and the fact that

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\simeq{\det}_{\Lambda}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\beta)
.

The proof of part (ii) is entirely analogous, and we omit it here.
∎

Let us apply Lemma
3.38
with the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
. This computation will be used in Chapter
4
.

Let us put

\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\otimes_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}^{\mathbf{L}}E.

Proposition 3.41
.

Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
be an integer such that
j\neq k/2
. Assume that condition (
S)
) holds.
Then the following statements hold true:

We have

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\simeq D^{(\alpha)}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\simeq\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j)}{\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j)))}\,,

and
\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)=0
for
i\neq 1,2.

The complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
is semi-simple. The Bockstein map together with the isomorphisms in Part (i) give rise to a commutative diagram

(3.69)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 35.17743pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-35.17743pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Bock_{2}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.36943pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 122.1424pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 122.1424pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{D^{(\alpha)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 135.39526pt\raise-14.96248pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 135.39526pt\raise-24.36943pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-35.17743pt\raise-32.95392pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.17743pt\raise-32.95392pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.17743pt\raise-32.95392pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern where the map
\delta
is given by

\delta(d)=\ell^{*}(V_{f}(j))\cdot\left(\mathrm{pr}_{0}\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(d)\right)\,,\qquad\ell^{*}(V_{f}(j))=\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}(i+j)

or, in more explicit form,

\delta(d)=-(j-1)!\left(\frac{1-p^{j-1}\alpha^{-1}}{1-p^{-j}\alpha}\right)\left(\exp^{*}_{V^{(\alpha)}_{x_{0}}(j)}\right)^{-1}(d)\,.

Both squares in the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha),\mathrm{imp}}}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ell^{*}(V_{f}(j))}
\textstyle{\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{E.}

Proof.

The proof is purely formal. We remark that
the map

\mathrm{Exp}_{V(j),j,0}\,:\,D^{(\alpha)}\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))

is the zero-map and therefore, the kernel and the cokernel of the map

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\oplus D^{(\alpha)}\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}+\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}(j)j,0}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}

are isomorphic to
D^{(\alpha)}
and
\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}{\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j)))}
, respectively. The proof of (i) can be completed mimicking the arguments used in the proof of Proposition
3.40
.

It follows from the definition of the Bockstein map that

\delta(d)=\mathrm{pr}_{0}(y)\mod H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j)),\quad\textrm{where $\mathrm{Exp}_{V_{f}(j),j}(y)=d.$}

Taking (
3.47
) into account together with the formulae (
1.23
), we conclude the proof of Part (ii).

This assertion directly follows from definitions.
∎

We prove give a proof of the two lemmas used in the proof of Proposition
3.40
.
Both statements are well-known (cf.
[
PR95
, Proposition 3.4.2(i)]
), but we record them here for the reader’s convenience.

Lemma 3.42
.

For any
p
-adic representation
W
of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
with
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W^{*}(1))=0
, we have a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(W)_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(W)\longrightarrow\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(W)^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow 0\,.

Proof.

Consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(W)^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(W)_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(W)^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\oplus}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},W)_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\oplus}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\oplus}H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},W)^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\,.}

The rows and the middle column of this diagram are exact. We explain that the right-most column is also exact. We have

H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W)^{\Gamma}\simeq\left(H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),W^{*}(1))_{\Gamma}\right)^{*}

by local duality. It follows from (
3.58
) that

\dim_{E}H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),W^{*}(1))_{\Gamma}=\dim_{E}H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),W^{*}(1))^{\Gamma}=\dim_{E}H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W^{*}(1))=0,

where the final vanishing is our running assumption. Hence
H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},W)^{\Gamma}=0
. This shows that the right-most column in the diagram above is indeed exact, and in turn, allows us to define the dotted arrow. Moreover, we have

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},W)_{\Gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},W)

for all
\ell\neq p
. The lemma follows from this fact via a diagram chase.
∎

Lemma 3.43
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\simeq H^{1}_{\mathrm{0}}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))

Assume that
H^{1}_{\mathrm{0}}(V_{f^{*}}(k-j))=0.
Then,

\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))^{\Gamma}=\{0\}=\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}

and
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}=H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j)).

Proof.

Part i) is proved in
[
PR95
, Section 3.3.4]
(see also
[
Ben14
]
, Section 6.1.3).
Part ii) follows from this fact and Lemma
3.42
.
∎

3.5.3.
The central critical case

In this section, we assume that
k
is even and consider the case
j=\frac{k}{2}
. Proposition
3.41
applies also with
j=\frac{k}{2}
when
r_{\rm an}(\frac{k}{2})=0
. We therefore assume
r_{\rm an}(\frac{k}{2})\geq 1
.

Recall the
p
-adic heights

\left<\,,\,\right>_{\star}:H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\otimes H^{1}_{\star}(V_{f}({k}/{2}))\longrightarrow E,\qquad\qquad\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\},

associated to splitting submodules
D_{\star}[k/2]\subset{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}(k/2))
, cf.
[
Ben20
]
. Recall that
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f^{*}}({k}/2))

and
\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}
coincides with the classical
p
-adic height
pairing introduced in
[
Nek93
]
.

Let us define the associated regulator

R_{\star}(T_{f}({k}/{2})):=\left|\det\left<e_{i}^{*},e_{j}\right>_{\star}\right|^{-1}\,,

(as above, the subscript “
\star
” stands for
\alpha
or
\beta
) for any choice of bases
\{e_{i}^{*}\}
of
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}

and
\{e_{i}\}
of
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}
. It is clear that
R_{\star}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))
depends on the choice of these bases only up to multiplication by a
p
-adic unit.

In what follows, whenever
M
and
N
are free submodules of
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}
and
H^{1}_{\star}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}
of the same rank and with bases
\{m_{i}\}
and
\{n_{j}\}
, respectively, we write
\left<M,N\right>_{\star}
as a shorthand for

\det\left<m_{i},n_{j}\right>_{\star}
.

Theorem 3.44
.

Assume that the conditions (
BK)
) and (
h-PR)
) hold true. Then:

H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))
and
the pairing
\left<\,,\,\right>_{\alpha}
coincides with the restriction of

\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}
on
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2)).

There exist canonical isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\simeq H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\simeq H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*}

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\simeq H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\simeq H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*}.

The Bockstein maps are given by the diagrams

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Bock}_{2}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left<\,,\,\right>_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*}}

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Bock}_{2}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*}}

where the right vertical maps in both diagrams are induced by the
p
-adic height pairing.

In particular, the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}({k/2}),\alpha)

(resp.
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)
) is semi-simple if and only if the
p
-adic height pairing
\left<\,,\,\right>_{\alpha}
(resp.
\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}
)
is non-degenerate.

We have

r_{\alpha}(k/2)=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\,,\qquad\qquad r_{\beta}(k/2)=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f}(k/2))\,.

If in addition (
ht)
) holds, we have
r_{\beta}(k/2)=r_{\alpha}(k/2)+1
.

Proof.

We refer the reader to
[
PR95
, Proposition 3.4.2(ii) and Section 3.3.4]
and
[
Col00
]
for the proofs of the results (
1)
)–(
3)
) recorded below.

1)

H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))
and
H^{1}_{u}(V_{f}(k/2)):=H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\cap H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))
are orthogonal to one another under the pairing
\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}
. Therefore,

(3.70)

\left<H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(T_{f^{*}}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}},H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right>_{\beta}=\left<H^{1}_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}},\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}}{H^{1}_{u}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}}\right>_{\beta}\left<\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(T_{f^{*}}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}}{H^{1}_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}},H^{1}_{u}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right>_{\beta}.

2)

The Iwasawa-theoretic version of the Poitou–Tate exact sequence
provides us with a map

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*}\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))\,.

Since
H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}(\frac{k}{2}))=0,
we have
H^{1}(V_{f^{*}}(\frac{k}{2}))\simeq H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),V_{f^{*}}(\frac{k}{2}))^{\Gamma}.

This gives rise to a commutative diagram

(3.71)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern
