where the vertical morphisms are surjective and the horizontal ones are
isomorphisms.

3)

Consider the composition of morphisms

\mu\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*},

where the first map is provided by Lemma
3.42
,
the middle map is the natural projection and the
last map is provided by diagram (
3.71
). Then

\mu

coincides with the pairing induced by
\log^{-1}\chi(\gamma)\left<\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,\right>_{\beta}
.

By Proposition
3.23
, we have

H^{1}_{\alpha}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\,,

and by Proposition
3.25
,

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\cap H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))=\{0\}\,.

We note that the proofs of these portions of Proposition
3.23
and Proposition
3.25
do not rely on the hypothesis (
ht)
). Therefore, Lemma
3.42
gives rise to an isomorphism

H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\simeq\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))^{\Gamma}\,.

Consider the map (
3.66
) for
j=\frac{k}{2}.

Since

\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\right)\cap H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))=\{0\},

we infer that
\ker({g}_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and
\mathrm{coker}({g}_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
vanish.
Tensoring the exact sequence (
3.64
) with
\mathbb{Q}_{p}

and applying the snake lemma, we obtain an exact sequence

(3.72)

0\longrightarrow\mathrm{coker}({g}_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))^{\Gamma}\\
\longrightarrow\mathrm{coker}({g}_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow 0\,.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\simeq\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))^{\Gamma}\simeq H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2)),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\simeq\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\simeq H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*}.

A diagram chase shows that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Bock}_{2}}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}}

commutes. This fact, combined with (
1)
)–(
3)
), concludes the proof of the portion of the theorem concerning the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{imp}}(V_{f}(k/2),\alpha).

We now turn our attention to the case of
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta).
Recall that we have

(3.73)

H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(k/2))

by Proposition
3.25
; we remark once again that the proof of this portion of Proposition
3.25
does not rely on the hypothesis (
ht)
). Since we have
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\subset H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(k/2))
by
Lemma
3.42
, it follows from (
3.73
) that

(3.74)

H^{1}_{u}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}.

Moreover, we have a canonical decomposition

(3.75)

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\oplus H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\,.

We therefore have canonical isomorphisms

\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})^{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\simeq H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\simeq\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))}.

The Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)
is associated to the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{S}(V_{f}(k/2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(k/2))}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(k/2),\beta),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(k/2),\beta):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(k/2))

for
\ell\neq p
and the local condition at
p
is given by

\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2),\beta):=D^{(\beta)}[k/2]

together with the map

D^{(\beta)}[k/2][-1]\xrightarrow{\mathbf{Exp}_{\beta,k/2,0}}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))\,.

The map
\mathbf{Exp}_{\beta,k/2,0}
induces

\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,k/2,0}\,:\,D^{(\beta)}[k/2]\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))\,,

and the image of the map
\mathrm{Exp}_{\beta,k/2,0}
is the Bloch–Kato subgroup
H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))
. Therefore,

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\simeq H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(k/2))\,.

It is not difficult to see that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})^{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(k/2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\,,}

where the bottom row is induced by the decomposition (
3.75
) and all vertical maps are isomorphisms. Since the bottom row is exact, then so is the upper row. Therefore, we have the following commutative diagram
with exact rows:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})^{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Bock_{2}}}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))^{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Note that the right-most vertical map in this diagram admits an interpretation in terms of the
p
-adic height pairing, cf. (
3)
). The left-most vertical map fits in the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})^{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\beta})_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))},}

where
\Upsilon
is the unique map that ensures that this diagram commutes. We have the following interpretation of the right vertical map in this diagram
in terms of
p
-adic height pairing, cf.
[
PR95
]
: The diagram

\textstyle{\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))}{H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))}\times H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{(\textup{res}_{p},{\Upsilon})}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f^{*}}(k/2))\times\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))}{H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(k/2))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
local duality
\textstyle{E}

commutes.

Putting these facts together, we see that
\mathrm{Bock}_{2}
is an isomorphism
if and only if the pairing
\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}
is non-degenerate.
This completes the proof of the portion of the theorem concerning the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k/2),\beta)
.
∎

3.5.4.
Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer-style formulae

In §
3.5.4
, we prove
p
-adic Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer style formulae for our algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions. These results will not be used in the remainder of this paper, but we include them here for the sake of completeness. We remark that they were proved at a great level of generality in
[
PR95
]
in the non-
\theta
-critical case (see also
[
Ben14
]
), and the proofs in the
\theta
-critical scenario (which we present below) are very similar granted the input from the earlier portions of §
3.5
.

As before, let us set

e_{p,\alpha}(f,\mathds{1},j)=\left(1-\alpha^{-1}p^{j-1}\right)\cdot\left(1-\beta p^{-j}\right)\,,\qquad e_{p,\beta}(f,\mathds{1},j)=\left(1-\beta^{-1}p^{j-1}\right)\cdot\left(1-\alpha p^{-j}\right).

We denote by

\mathrm{Tam}^{0}(T_{f}(j)):=\underset{\ell\in S}{\prod}\mathrm{Tam}_{\ell}(T_{f}(j))

the product of local Tamagawa numbers of
V_{f}(j)
and by

\mathrm{Tam}_{\{p\}}^{0}(T_{f}(j)):=\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\prod}\mathrm{Tam}_{\ell}(T_{f}(j))

the product of local Tamagawa numbers away from
p
.
Recall that we have set
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star}):=|b|,
where
b\in E^{*}
is such that
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(T_{f}^{(\star)})=bN_{\star}
. Throughout §
3.5.4
, we will assume that
\Omega_{p}(T_{f}^{(\star)},N_{\star})\in\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*}
for
\star\in\{\alpha,\beta\}.

Theorem 3.45
.

Suppose
j\geqslant k
.
Assume that the conditions (

p

Bei)
) and (
S)
) hold.
Then
r_{\alpha}(j)=0
and

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\left|(j-k)!\cdot e_{p,\alpha}(f,\mathds{1},j)\right|^{-1}\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f}(j))\cdot R_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}.

Suppose
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
. Assume that the condition(
S)
) holds. Then
r_{\alpha}(j)=0
and

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\frac{\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\cdot w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\cdot w(T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}(k-j)),

where
w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))=\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}/T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))
and

w(T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}(k-j))=\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}/T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}(k-j)).

iii) Suppose
j\geqslant k.
Assume that the condition(

p

Bei)
)
holds and
H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=0.
Then
r_{\beta}(j)=0
and

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\left|(j-1)!\cdot e_{p,\beta}(f,\mathds{1},j)\right|^{-1}\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f}(j))\cdot R_{\beta}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}.

iv) Suppose
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
. Then
r_{\beta}(j)=0
and

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\left|(j-1)!\cdot e_{p,\beta}(f,\mathds{1},j)\right|^{-1}\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}.

Remark 3.46
.

The formulae for
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)

and
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0)
in Theorem
3.45

are concurrent with Propositions
3.14
and
3.16
, in view of

MC

(
f
,
α
)

,

MC

(
f
,
β
)

and Proposition
2.27
.
Note that, we can express
\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}
also in the form

\left|(j-k)!\cdot e_{p,\alpha}(f,\mathds{1},j)\cdot\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\right|^{-1}\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f}(j))\cdot R_{\beta}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}

in the situation of Theorem
3.45
(i), and

\left|\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\right|^{-1}\frac{\#\Sha_{\mathrm{f}}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\cdot w(T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))\cdot w(T^{(\alpha)}_{f^{*}}(k-j))

in the situation of Theorem
3.45
(ii). These expressions are akin to those that appear in the
p
-adic Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture in the non-critical scenario, cf.
[
MTT86
,
Ben14
]
.

Proof.

Applying the snake lemma to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

with exact rows which we obtain from the exact sequence (
3.64
), we have an exact sequence

(3.76)

0\rightarrow\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\rightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))^{\Gamma}\\
\rightarrow\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\rightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\rightarrow 0.

By Proposition
3.40
, all terms of this sequence are finite groups under our running assumptions. Moreover,
r_{\alpha}(j)=0
and

(3.77)

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\frac{\#\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)}{\#\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)}=\frac{\#\mathrm{coker}({g}_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}}{\#\mathrm{coker}({g}_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}}\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}}{\#\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))^{\Gamma}}.

The arguments of
[
PR95
, Section 3.3.4]
(see also
[
Col00
]
, computations following Proposition 3.9, pp. 34-35)
show that

(3.78)

\frac{\#\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}}{\#\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))^{\Gamma}}=\frac{\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j)):H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\right]}{\left[H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j)):H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\right]}\cdot\frac{{\#\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\cdot\mathrm{Tam}_{\{p\}}^{0}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\,.

On the other hand, the snake lemma gives

(3.79)

\frac{\#\mathrm{coker}({g}_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}}{\#\mathrm{coker}({g}_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}}=\left[H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}:H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}+\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)_{\Gamma}\right].

Since
\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)_{\Gamma}
is
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-free,
we have

(3.80)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j)):H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))+\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)_{\Gamma}\right]=\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}+H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt\times\frac{\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\left[H^{1}_{\star}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:\mathrm{Exp}_{\star,j}(N_{\star}[j]\otimes\Lambda)_{\Gamma}\right]}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))}.\end{aligned}

Combining formulae (
3.77
),
(
3.78
)
(
3.79
) and (
3.80
) with
Lemma
3.13
(ii), we deduce that

(3.81)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{\mathrm{imp}}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\frac{\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\cdot\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)_{\Gamma}\right].\end{aligned}

Assume first that
j\geqslant k
. It follows from the definition of the Tamagawa numbers and (
1.23
) that

(3.82)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T^{(\alpha)}_{f}(j))_{\mathrm{tf}}:\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)_{\Gamma}\right]&=|(j-k)!|^{-1}\left|1-\alpha^{-1}p^{j-1}\right|^{-1}\frac{\mathrm{Tam}_{p}(T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))}{\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\alpha)}/T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))}\,.\end{aligned}

Putting together (
3.81
) and (
3.82
) with Proposition
3.17
, we infer that

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{f}(j),\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\left|(j-k)!\cdot e_{p,\alpha}(f,\cdot\mathds{1},j))\right|^{-1}\frac{\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f}(j))\cdot R_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\,.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
3.45
(i).

Let us now assume that
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
j\neq\frac{k}{2}.

Since
\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}
establishes an isomorphism between
\mathfrak{D}(T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j))
and
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}^{(\alpha)}(j)),
we have

(3.83)

\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T^{(\alpha)}_{f}(j)):\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(N_{\alpha}[j]\otimes\Lambda)_{\Gamma}\right]=\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}/T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}(k-j)).

Therefore,

(3.84)

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{f}(j),\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\\
\frac{\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(j))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}(k-j))}\cdot\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}/T_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\cdot\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}/T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}(k-j)).

This completes the proof of Theorem
3.45
(ii).

The assertions in (iii) and (iv) concerning
\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0)
can be proved by the same argument (and, in any case, these statements are covered by Perrin-Riou’s general descent formalism).
∎

In §
3.5.4
, we assume that
k
is even and consider the scenario when
j=\frac{k}{2}
is the central critical point for the Hecke
L
-function of
f
.

Theorem 3.47
.

Assume that
k\geqslant 2
is even and that the conditions (
BK)
), (
ht)
) and (
h-PR)
) hold true. Then:

We have
r_{\beta}(\frac{k}{2})=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}(V_{f^{*}}(\frac{k}{2}))

and

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}^{*}(T_{f}({k}/{2}),N_{\alpha}[k/2],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=|({k}/{2}-1)!\cdot e_{p,\beta}(f,\mathds{1},k/2))|^{-1}\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{\rm f}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\cdot R_{\beta}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}({k}/{2}))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}.

We have
r_{\alpha}(\frac{k}{2})=\dim_{E}H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(\frac{k}{2})).

The
p
-adic height pairing

\left<\,,\,\right>_{\alpha}:\quad H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))\otimes H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))\longrightarrow E

is non-degenerate and coincides with the restriction of
\left<\,,\,\right>_{\beta}

to
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))
. Moreover,

(3.85)

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{f}({k}/{2}),N_{\alpha}[k/2],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}\\
=\frac{\#\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))\cdot R_{\alpha}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}({k}/{2}))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}\cdot w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}({k}/{2}))\cdot w(T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}({k}/{2})),

where

w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}({k}/{2})):=\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}/T_{f}^{(\beta)}({k}/{2}))\quad\hbox{ and }\quad w(T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}({k}/{2})):=\#H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}/T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}({k}/{2})).

Proof.

This portion is covered by the general descent formalism of Perrin-Riou (which our argument below to prove (ii) parallels), and its proof will be omitted.

We already proved as part of Theorem
3.44
all the assertions except for the leading term formula.

It follows from Lemma
3.38
and our explicit description of the Bockstein map in Theorem
3.44
(iii) that

(3.86)

\left|\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{f}({k}/{2}),N_{\alpha}[k/2],\mathds{1},0)\right|^{-1}=\left[\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\Gamma}:\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))^{\Gamma}\right]\cdot\left[\#\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}:\#\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}\right].

Recall that we have
H^{1}_{u}(T_{f}(k/2))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}
under our assumptions; cf. (
3.74
). We use the following computation from
[
PR95
, Section 3.3.4]
(see also
[
Col00
]
, the discussion following Proposition 3.5):

(3.87)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left[\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\Gamma}:\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))^{\Gamma}\right]=\\
&\hskip 19.91684pt(\log\chi(\gamma_{1}))^{-r_{\alpha}(k/2)}\cdot\frac{\#\Sha_{0}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))\mathrm{Tam}^{0}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2})):H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\Gamma}\right]}{\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}\\
&\hskip 227.62204pt\times\left|\left<H^{1}_{0}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}},\frac{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}}\right>_{\alpha}\right|^{-1}.\end{aligned}

Note that we have used here the fact that
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))=H^{1}_{\rm f}(T_{f}(\frac{k}{2}))
under our running assumptions. Let us write

(3.88)

\left<H^{1}_{0}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}},\frac{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))}{H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}}\right>_{\alpha}=\\
\frac{\left<H^{1}_{0}(T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}},H^{1}_{0}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right>_{\alpha}}{\left[H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}_{\mathrm{tf}}+H^{1}_{0}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]}\cdot\frac{\#H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}_{\mathrm{tor}}}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}(k/2))}\,.

The formula (
3.79
) applies in our case, and
mimicking the computations from the proof of Theorem
3.45
, we deduce that

(3.89)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{\#\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}}{\#\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}}\cdot&\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2})):H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\Gamma}\right]=w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}(k/2))w(T_{f^{*}}^{(\beta)}(k/2))\\
&\hskip 106.69783pt\times\frac{\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}_{\mathrm{tf}}+H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]}{\#H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}_{\mathrm{tor}}}\,.\end{aligned}

It is easy to check that

(3.90)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}_{\mathrm{tf}}+H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]}{\left[H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}(T_{f}(k/2))^{\rm u}_{\mathrm{tf}}+H^{1}_{0}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}\right]}=\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\left[H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}:H^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}+\frac{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\mathrm{tf}}}{H^{1}_{0}(T_{f}(k/2))_{\mathrm{tf}}}\right]\,.\end{aligned}

The formulae (
3.87
)–(
3.90
) together with
Proposition
3.39

show that

(3.91)

\left[\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))_{\Gamma}:\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}({k}/{2}))^{\Gamma}\right]\cdot\left[\#\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})_{\Gamma}:\#\mathrm{coker}(g_{\alpha})^{\Gamma}\right]\\
\qquad\quad\sim_{p}(\log\chi(\gamma_{1}))^{-r_{\alpha}(k/2)}\times\frac{\Sha_{\alpha}(T_{f^{*}}(k/2))\cdot R_{\alpha}(T_{f}(k/2))\cdot\mathrm{Tam}_{\{p\}}^{0}(T_{f}(k/2))}{\#H^{0}(V_{f}/T_{f}({k}/{2}))\cdot\#H^{0}(V_{f^{*}}/T_{f^{*}}({k}/{2}))}\cdot w(T_{f}^{(\beta)}({k}/{2}))\cdot w(T^{(\beta)}_{f^{*}}({k}/{2})).

Part (ii) of our theorem follows from this computation and (
3.86
).
∎

Chapter 4
Main Conjectures for the infinitesimal deformation

In this chapter, we develop Iwasawa theory for the infinitesimal deformation
V_{k}
of Deligne’s representation
V_{f}
along the eigencurve. This involves the definition of the
thick
fundamental line in §
4.1
in this context, which we then use to define the infinitesimal thickening of the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions; cf. Equation (
4.12
).

The infinitesimal thickening

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

of the main conjecture asserts that the infinitesimal thickening of the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions is generated by the thick
p
-adic
L
-function
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}
up to a canonical fudge factor.

To prove the leading term formulae for the infinitesimal thickening of the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions (cf. Theorem
4.14
), we rely on the Iwasawa descent formalism for the infinitesimal thickening of the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions, which we develop in §
4.2.5
. Our descent formalism dwells on the general discussion in §
4.2.3
and the integrality properties we establish in the opening section of this chapter (cf. Lemma
4.1
).

As a further motivation for the contents of the present chapter, we recall from Chapter
3
the degenerate behaviour of the Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f},\alpha)
when
f_{\alpha}
is
\theta
-critical.
The key insight in our study, as we shall illustrate in the present chapter as well as the next, is that the Selmer complex attached to the infinitesimal deformation
V_{k}
(which we call the thick Selmer complex) encodes strictly more information. This is illustrated by Theorems
4.14
and
4.15
, which tell us that the thick Selmer complex “sees”
also the special values of the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function
L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\pm}
.

Recall that we have
V_{f}\simeq V_{x_{0}}
, where
x_{0}
is the critical point on the eigencurve which corresponds to
f_{\alpha}
. We assume throughout this chapter that the ramification index of the weight map
\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}
at
x_{0}
is equal to
e=2
. Recall the tautological exact sequence

(4.1)

0\longrightarrow V_{f}\longrightarrow V_{k}\longrightarrow V_{f}\longrightarrow 0,

where the copy of
V_{f}
on the left arises from its identification with
V[x_{0}]
.

We note that
V_{k}
can be viewed as a Galois representation with coefficients in the ring
\widetilde{E}:=E[X]/(X^{2})
. One may also forget the Hecke-module structure and consider
V_{k}
as a
4
-dimensional Galois representation over
E
.

4.1.
The fundamental line

4.1.1.
Lattices

We recall various definitions from §
2.4.5
for the convenience of the reader. Let us put
\widetilde{E}:=E[X]/X^{2}
. For each
n\geqslant 0,
recall the unital
O_{E}
-subalgebra

\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}=O_{E}+\varpi_{E}^{-n}O_{E}X\,\subset E[X]/X^{2}

of
\widetilde{E}
, where
\varpi_{E}
is a uniformizer of
E
.
Set

\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(\infty)}:=\underset{n\geqslant 1}{\cup}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}.

Let
T_{f}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice in
V_{f}
which is stable under the action of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}.

Lemma 4.1
.

The following assertions hold true:

For any
n\gg 0
there exists a free
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
-submodule
T_{k}^{(n)}\subset V_{k}
of rank two such that

L1)

T_{k}^{(n)}
is stable under the action of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
,

L2)

T_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}=T_{f}
.

Suppose that the natural number
n
verifies the conclusions of Part (i). Let
m
be another natural number such that
T_{k}^{(m)}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}
-submodule of
V_{k}
satisfying the properties (
L1)
)–(
L2)
) with
n
replaced by
m
. Then there exists
s\geqslant\max\{m,n\}
such that

T_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}^{(s)}_{E}=T_{k}^{(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}}\mathcal{O}^{(s)}_{E}.

Proof.

Let
\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
be a basis of
T_{f}.
Fix arbitrary lifts

\widetilde{v}_{1},\widetilde{v}_{2}\in V_{k}
of
v_{1},v_{2}
under the surjection
V_{k}\twoheadrightarrow V_{f}
and set
\widetilde{T}=\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(0)}\widetilde{v}_{1}+\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(0)}\widetilde{v}_{2}
. Note that we have

g\cdot\widetilde{v}_{i}=a_{i,1}(g)\widetilde{v}_{1}+a_{i,2}(g)\widetilde{v}_{2}+X(b_{i,1}(g)\widetilde{v}_{1}+b_{i,2}(g)\widetilde{v}_{2})

with
a_{i,j}(g)\in\mathcal{O}_{E}
for any
g\in G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
, since
\widetilde{v}_{i}\mapsto v_{i}
under the
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
-equivariant morphism
V_{k}\twoheadrightarrow V_{f}
. This discussion shows that

g\cdot\widetilde{v}_{i}\in\bigcup_{m\geq 0}(\widetilde{T}+\varpi_{E}^{-m}X\widetilde{T})\,.

It follows from the compactness of the Galois group that there exists
n\geqslant 0
such that

g\cdot\widetilde{v}_{i}\in\widetilde{T}+{\varpi_{E}^{-n}}X\widetilde{T},\qquad\forall g\in G_{\mathbb{Q},S},\quad i=1,2.

Set
T_{k}^{(n)}=\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}\widetilde{v}_{1}+\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}\widetilde{v}_{2}
. Thanks to our choice of
n
,
the
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}^{(n)}
-module
T_{k}^{(n)}
is
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
-stable. Furthermore, we have
T_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}O_{E}=T_{f}
by construction and our proof is complete.

It is clear that for each
m\geq n
, the
\ \mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}
-module
T_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}
verifies the properties (
L1)
)–(
L2)
). This proves the first part of the lemma.

It follows from the compactness that there exists
s\geqslant\max\{m,n\}

such that

T_{k}^{(n)}\subset T_{k}^{(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(s)}
and
T_{k}^{(m)}\subset T_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(s)}.

Then

T_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(s)}\subset T_{k}^{(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(s)}\subset T_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(s)},

and the proposition is proved.
∎

Recall that
\Lambda^{(n)}:=\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda
and let us set
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)=\widetilde{E}\otimes_{E}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
. We have decompositions of these algebras into
\Delta
-isotypic components:

\Lambda^{(n)}=\underset{i=1}{\overset{p-1}{\oplus}}\Lambda^{(n)}_{\omega^{i}},\qquad\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)=\underset{i=1}{\overset{p-1}{\oplus}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{\omega^{i}}(\Gamma).

Let us put

\Lambda^{(\infty)}:=\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(\infty)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda=\varinjlim_{n}\Lambda^{(n)}.

4.1.2.
Complexes

Let us fix a natural number
n
for which the properties (
L1)
)–(
L2)
) are verified. Recall that
\iota
denotes the canonical involution on
\Lambda.

We consider the global and local Iwasawa cohomology complexes

(4.2)

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\,:=\mathbf{R\Gamma}(G_{\mathbb{Q},S},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda^{\iota})\,,\qquad\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\,:=\,\mathbf{R\Gamma}(G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda^{\iota})

as objects of the derived category
\mathscr{D}(\Lambda^{(n)})
of
\Lambda^{(n)}
-modules.
One then has

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\Lambda\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},S}(T_{f}(j))\,,\qquad\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\Lambda\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j)).

Since the action of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
(respectively
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
) on
V_{k}
factors through a quotient containing a pro-
p
-group of finite index,
the following properties follow from the general results of Flach
[
Fla00
]

(see especially Propositions 4.1, 4.2, 5.2 and Remark 3.1 in op. cit.):

\bullet

The complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))

and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))
(
\ell\in S
) are perfect objects
in the derived category
\mathscr{D}(\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)})
of
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
-modules.
The complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))
and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))
are perfect objects
in the derived category
\mathscr{D}(\Lambda^{(n)})
of
\Lambda^{(n)}
-modules.

\bullet

For
m\geqslant n,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\Lambda^{(m)}\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\Lambda^{(m)}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\Lambda^{(m)}\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\Lambda^{(m)}).

Set
D_{k}:={\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}\left(\mathbf{D}_{k}\right).
Recall that we denote by
N_{\alpha}
the
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice
of
D^{(\alpha)}:={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{f}^{(\alpha)})
generated by
\eta_{f}^{\alpha}
. Let us fix a free
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
-submodule

N_{k}^{(n)}\subset D_{k}
of rank one
such that

N_{k}^{(n)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}=N_{\alpha}.

For each non-archimedean
place
\ell\neq p
, we consider the usual unramified local condition at
\ell
:

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\text{\rm Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)):=\,\left[(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))^{I_{\ell}}\otimes\Lambda^{\iota}\xrightarrow{1-f_{\ell}}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))^{I_{\ell}}\otimes\Lambda^{\iota}\right],

where
I_{\ell}
is the inertia subgroup at
\ell
and
f_{\ell}
is the geometric Frobenius.

At
\ell=p
, we shall consider the following Perrin-Riou–style local conditions, see
[
Ben14
]
for a further discussion.
Let
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{E}
denote the functor which associates to a

p
-adic
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation its overconvergent
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
[
CC98
]
. There exists a quasi-isomorphism

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\simeq\left[\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{E}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\xrightarrow{\psi-1}\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{E}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\right],

in the derived category of
\Lambda^{(n)}
-modules, cf.
[
Ben14
, Proposition A5]
.
Since
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
is flat over
\Lambda,
we have

(4.3)

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{L}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\\
\simeq\left[\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{E}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\xrightarrow{\psi-1}\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{E}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\right].

Moreover, as
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{E}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\simeq\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{k}(j))

(cf.
[
Pot12
]
), we have the following isomorphism in the derived category
\mathscr{D}(\mathscr{H}(\Gamma))

of
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
-modules:

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\simeq\left[\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{k}(j))\xrightarrow{\psi-1}\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{k}(j))\right].

Let us set

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}):=({N_{k}^{(n)}}[j]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Lambda)[-1].

We will denote the Perrin-Riou exponential map

\text{\rm Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(j),j}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{k}[j])\longrightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V_{k}(j))^{\psi=1}

simply by
\text{\rm Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(j)}.
The map
\text{\rm Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(j)}
induces a morphism

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma),

which we shall take as the local condition at
p.

Consider the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}
\textstyle{\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]})\right)\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\,\,.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Note that the objects of this diagram depend on
V_{k}
and
D_{k}
rather than
T_{k}^{(n)}
and
N_{k}^{(n)}
(since
p
is inverted), and we denote by
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)
the Selmer complex associated to this diagram.
By definition, the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)
(to which we henceforth refer as the
thick
Selmer complex) sits in the following distinguished triangle:

(4.4)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\longrightarrow&\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\bigoplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right)\right)\right)\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\longrightarrow\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\right)\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\xrightarrow{[+1]}\,.\end{aligned}

Let us define the fundamental line
\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}}[j]\right)
on setting

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}

\displaystyle\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right):=

\displaystyle{\det}^{-1}_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw},S}(T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\bigoplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right)\right)\right)\otimes{\det}_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\right)\right).

It follows from the exact triangle (
4.4
) that we have a natural injection

(4.5)

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),{N_{k}^{(n)}[j]}\right)\longrightarrow{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\,.

Suppose
m\geqslant n
are natural numbers both verifying the conditions (
L1)
) and (
L2)
). We then have an isomorphism

(4.6)

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)}\simeq\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(m)},N_{k}^{(m)}[j]\right),

which is compatible with the morphisms (
4.5
)
in the obvious sense. Set

(4.7)

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right):=\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(\infty)}}.

Since the rings
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
and
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(\infty)}
are local,

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right)

are free modules of rank one over
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}
and
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(\infty)}
, respectively. It follows from (
4.6
) and Lemma
4.1
(ii) that the module
\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right)
(to which we henceforth refer to the
thick
fundamental line) does not, up to canonical isomorphism, depend on the choice of
n
and the lift
T_{k}^{(n)}
of
T_{f}
. By extension of scalars, the map (
4.5
) induces a map

(4.8)

\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\,:\,\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right)\longrightarrow{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\,.

Recall that

N_{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}E=D^{(\alpha)}.

Consider the Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{f}(j),\alpha\right)

of
\mathscr{H}_{E}(\Gamma)
-modules associated to the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{{\mathrm{Iw}},S}(T_{f}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j))\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}
\textstyle{\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{f}(j),\alpha)\right)\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)\,\,,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which was introduced in Section
3.3.1
.

The tautological exact sequence (
4.1
)
induces a distinguished triangle

(4.9)

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{f}(j),\alpha\right)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{f}(j),\alpha\right)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{f}(j),\alpha\right)[1]\,.

4.1.3.
Trivialization of the thick fundamental line

Our goal in §
4.1.3
is to establish an auxiliary statement (Proposition
4.3
below) that we will use to trivialize the thick fundamental line
\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j])
.

Let
R
be a Bézout ring, which is simultaneously an
E
-algebra. Set
\widetilde{R}:=R\otimes_{E}\widetilde{E}
. Then
\widetilde{R}/X\widetilde{R}=R.
If
\widetilde{M}

is a
\widetilde{R}
-module, we can and we will consider
M:=\widetilde{M}/X\widetilde{M}
as an
R
-module. Note that
X^{2}\widetilde{M}=0.
We shall consider
\widetilde{R}
-modules satisfying the following additional
condition:

(M)

\ker({\widetilde{M}}\xrightarrow{X}{\widetilde{M}})=X{\widetilde{M}}\,.

Note that each free
\widetilde{R}
-module verifies
(M)
.

Lemma 4.2
.

The following two conditions are equivalent:

\widetilde{M}
satisfies
(M)
.

The sequence

{0\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow\widetilde{M}\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0,}

where the map
M:=\widetilde{M}/X\widetilde{M}\to\widetilde{M}
is induced by multiplication
by
X
, is exact.

Proof.

This is clear and the proof is omitted.
∎

Proposition 4.3
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{M}
is a finitely generated
\widetilde{R}
-module
satisfying
(M)
. If
M
is finitely presented over
R
, then
\widetilde{M}
admits a free resolution of length
1
.

Proof.

Since
R
is a Bézout ring and
M
is finitely presented, it has a free resolution

0\longrightarrow P_{1}\longrightarrow P_{0}\longrightarrow M\longrightarrow 0

of length 1 (cf. §
3.3.2
).
We will construct a diagram of the form

(4.10)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{P}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{r_{1}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{P}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{r_{0}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{P_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{P_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

where
\widetilde{P}_{i}
(
i=0,1
) are free
\widetilde{R}
-modules. Fix a basis
\{e_{0,i}\}_{i}
of
P_{0}.
Let
\widetilde{P}_{0}=\underset{i}{\oplus}\widetilde{R}\,\widetilde{e}_{0,i}
be an arbitrary free
\widetilde{R}
-module of rank
{\rm rank}_{R}\,P_{i}
, generated by some set
\{\widetilde{e}_{0,i}\}_{i}.

Let us denote by
{r_{0}\,:\,\widetilde{P}_{0}\rightarrow P_{0}}
the map given by

\sum_{i}\widetilde{a}_{i}\widetilde{e}_{0,i}\longmapsto\sum_{i}a_{i}e_{0,i},

where
a_{i}
stands for the image of
\widetilde{a}_{i}
under the projection
\widetilde{R}\twoheadrightarrow R
.

Let
\widetilde{m}_{i}\in\widetilde{M}
be any lift of
m_{i}:=f_{0}(e_{0,i})\in M
. We define
\widetilde{f}_{0}
as the unique
\widetilde{R}
-linear map such that

\widetilde{f}_{0}(\widetilde{e}_{0,i})=\widetilde{m}_{i}
for all
i.
It is easy to see by Nakayama’s lemma that
\widetilde{f}_{0}
is surjective and the square on the right in diagram (
4.10
) commutes.

Similarly, fix a basis
\{e_{1,j}\}_{j}
of
P_{1}
and set
\widetilde{P}_{1}=\underset{j}{\oplus}\widetilde{A}\widetilde{e}_{1,j}.
We define the map
r_{1}\,:\,\widetilde{P}_{1}\rightarrow P_{1}
as before, by setting
r_{1}(\widetilde{e}_{1,j})=e_{1,j}.
Take any lifts
\widetilde{n}_{j}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}

of the elements
n_{j}:=f_{1}(e_{1,j})
and define the
\widetilde{R}
-linear map
g\,:\,\widetilde{P}_{1}\rightarrow P_{0}
setting
g(\widetilde{e}_{1,j})=\widetilde{n}_{j}.
If we take
\widetilde{f}_{1}=g,

then the square on the left in diagram (
4.10
) commutes, but the upper row is not necessarily
exact, and we therefore need to modify
g
.

For any
j
, it follows from the constructions above that

\widetilde{f}_{0}\circ g(\widetilde{e}_{1,j})\in X\widetilde{M}.
Hence
\widetilde{f}_{0}\circ g(\widetilde{e}_{1,j})=Xx_{j}
for some
x_{j}\in\widetilde{M}.
Take
y_{j}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
such that

\widetilde{f}_{0}(y_{j})=x_{j}.
Define the map
\widetilde{f}_{1}
setting

\widetilde{f}_{1}(\widetilde{e}_{1,j})=g(\widetilde{e}_{1,j})-Xy_{j},\qquad\forall j\,.

Then
\widetilde{f}_{0}\circ\widetilde{f}_{1}=0.
In addition, since
\widetilde{f}_{1}\equiv g\pmod{X},

the diagram (
4.10
) is commutative.

We only need to check that the upper row of diagram (
4.10
) is exact. To that end, let us first assume that
x\in\ker(\widetilde{f}_{1})
. Write
x=\sum_{j}\widetilde{a}_{j}\widetilde{e}_{1,j}.

Then
r_{1}(x)\in\ker(f_{1}),
and therefore
\sum_{j}a_{j}e_{1,j}=0,

where
a_{j}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
denotes the image of
\widetilde{a}_{j}
. We therefore infer that
a_{j}=0
and hence
\widetilde{a}_{j}\in X\widetilde{R}
for all
j.
Writing
\widetilde{a}_{j}=X\widetilde{b}_{j}
, we see that
x=Xy
for
y=\sum_{j}\widetilde{b}_{j}\widetilde{e}_{1,j}\in\widetilde{P}_{1},
and
X\widetilde{f}_{1}(y)=0
in
\widetilde{P}_{0}.

Since
\widetilde{P}_{0}
satisfies the condition
(M)
, this implies that

\widetilde{f}_{1}(y)\in X\widetilde{P_{0}},
and therefore once again that
\widetilde{b}_{j}\in X\widetilde{R}.

This shows that
\widetilde{a}_{j}=0
for all
j
, and hence
x=0
.

We next check the exactness of the upper row at
\widetilde{P}_{0}
. Assume that
x\in\widetilde{P}_{0}

is such that
\widetilde{f}_{0}(x)=0
. It follows from the commutativity of (
4.10
)
and the exactness of the bottom row that there exists
y\in\widetilde{P}_{1}

such that
\widetilde{f}_{1}(y)\equiv x\pmod{X\widetilde{P}_{0}}
. We may therefore assume without loss of generality that
x\in X\widetilde{P}_{0}.
Write
x=Xx^{\prime}
with
x^{\prime}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}.
Then

X\widetilde{f}_{0}(x^{\prime})=0
in
\widetilde{M}.
Since
\widetilde{M}
satisfies
(M)
,
this implies that
\widetilde{f}_{0}(x^{\prime})\in X\widetilde{M}.
Hence

f_{0}\circ r_{0}(x^{\prime})=0
and it follows from the commutativity of (
4.10
) that there exists
z\in\widetilde{P}_{1}
such that
\widetilde{f}_{1}(z)\equiv x^{\prime}\pmod{X}.

Therefore
\widetilde{f}_{1}(Xz)=Xx^{\prime}=x,
and the exactness is checked.
∎

Suppose that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f})\neq 0
. By (
3.53
),
\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)=0
for all
i\neq 2
,
and it follows from the global Euler–Poincaré characteristic formula that
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)
is torsion. The distinguished triangle (
4.9
) then reduces to an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\xrightarrow{[X]}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow 0.

We infer using Lemma
4.2
that
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)
verifies the condition
(M)
.
By Proposition
3.36
, for each
\eta\in X(\Delta),
the
\eta
-isotypic component
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)^{(\eta)}
is a finitely presented torsion module over
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)_{\eta}
.
Therefore, for every integer
j
, we have a canonical trivialization
(
3.50
)

(4.11)

i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\,:\,{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right)\xhookrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\,.

These maps are compatible with one another under twisting.

Paralleling the discussion in §
3.3
, the map (
4.5
) allows us to consider

\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right)
as a
{\Lambda}^{(\infty)}
-submodule of

{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}^{-1}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(V_{k}(j),\alpha\right).

Definition 4.4
.

We define the free
\Lambda^{(\infty)}
-module of rank one

(4.12)

{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right):=i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\left(\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[j]\right)\right)

and call it the infinitesimal thickening of the module of algebraic
p
-adic
L
-functions associated with
T_{k}^{(\infty)}(j)
.

As we have remarked at the start of Chapter
3
, this terminology is inspired by that of Perrin-Riou
[
PR95
, §2]
. The nomenclature can be justified by the Iwasawa main conjecture

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

below, which predicts that this module is indeed generated by the infinitesimal thickening of the critical
p
-adic
L
-function. Its subtle relation with the modules of “punctual”
p
-adic
L
-functions (that are given by Definition
3.30
) is the subject of Theorem
4.14
.

4.2.
Main Conjectures for the infinitesimal thickening of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions

4.2.1.

We are now ready to formulate the infinitesimal thickening of the Iwasawa Main Conjecture for
\theta
-critical forms

MC

(
f
,
α
)

.
Recall that we denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}
the image of
\mathcal{E}_{N}=\underset{\ell\mid N}{\prod}(1-a_{\ell}(x)\sigma_{\ell}^{-1})
in
\Lambda^{(\infty)}.

Conjecture 4.5
.

We have
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\rm cr}(V_{f})\neq 0
and

\widetilde{\mathbf{MC}}(f)

{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(k),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[k]\right)^{\pm}=\left(\lambda^{\pm}(f^{*})\cdot\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right)\,,

where
\left(\lambda^{\pm}(f^{*})\cdot\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right)
denotes the
\Lambda^{(\infty),\pm}
-module generated by

\lambda^{\pm}(f^{*})\cdot\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{S},\alpha^{*}}^{\pm}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}.

Using Proposition
2.32
, this conjecture can be formulated in terms of Kato’s

p
-adic
L
-function:

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}^{\iota}\cdot{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(k),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[k]\right)=\left(\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota}\right).

4.2.2.

In what follows, we shall explore various consequences of Conjecture
4.5
.

Proposition 4.6
.

Conjecture

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

implies

MC

(
f
,
α
)

.

Proof.

This follows from constructions of Section
3.4.2
and the following commutative diagram, where the vertical morphisms are induced by
\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
:

\textstyle{\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{k}^{(\infty)}(k),N_{k}^{(\infty)}[k])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(k)}^{(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(k),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(k)}^{(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{f}(k),N_{\alpha}[k])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(k)}^{(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{{\det}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(k),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{f}(k)}^{(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma).}

∎

4.2.3.
Descent formalism over
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)

Our main aim in the remainder of this chapter is to prove that Conjecture

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

is consistent with the slope-zero Main Conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

and the interpolation
properties of the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function
L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
. We remark that the thick Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)
is not semi-simple, namely the natural map

\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)_{\Gamma}

is not an isomorphism.

Our goal in §
4.2.3
is to prove an analog of the descent formula (
3.60
) for
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)
-modules.

To simplify notation, let us put
z:=\gamma_{1}-1.
Let
\widetilde{M}
and

\widetilde{N}
be free
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
-modules
of finite rank
h
and let

\phi\,:\,\widetilde{M}\longrightarrow\widetilde{N}

be an injective morphism. Then
\phi
induces a map

\det(\phi)\,:\,{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}(\widetilde{M})\otimes{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}(\widetilde{N})\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1}).

Let
\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}}:=\widetilde{M}\otimes_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}\widetilde{E}
and

\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}}:=\widetilde{N}\otimes_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}\widetilde{E}.
We have
an induced morphism of free
\widetilde{E}
-modules

\phi_{\Gamma}\,:\,\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}}.

Consider the tautological exact sequence

(4.13)

0\longrightarrow\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})\longrightarrow\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\Gamma}}\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma})\longrightarrow 0.

of
\widetilde{E}
-modules. We assume that the following condition holds true:

D1)

\dim_{E}\left(\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})\right)=1.

It follows from this condition that
\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})\subset X\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}}

and
X\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}}\subset\mathrm{im}(\phi_{\Gamma}).

Let
m\in\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})
be any element. Let us write
m=Xm^{\prime}
for some
m^{\prime}\in\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}}

and take a lift
\widehat{m}^{\prime}\in M
of
m^{\prime}
under the canonical projection

\widetilde{M}\rightarrow\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}}
. Then

\phi(X\widehat{m}^{\prime})\in X\widetilde{N}\cap z\widetilde{N}=zX\widetilde{N}.

Write
\phi(X\widehat{m}^{\prime})=Xz\widehat{n}
, with
\widehat{n}\in\widetilde{N}
. Let
B(\phi)(m)
denote the image of
\widehat{n}
in
\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma})=\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}}/\mathrm{im}(\phi_{\Gamma})
. It is easy to see that
B(\phi)(m)
does not depend on the choice of
\widehat{n}
, and we have a well defined map

B(\phi)\,:\,\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})\longrightarrow\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma})\,,

which we view as a variant of the Bockstein morphism. We assume in addition that

D2)

The map
B(\phi)
is an isomorphism.

Then the exact sequence (
4.13
) induces a trivialization

(4.14)

i_{\phi}\,:\,{\det}_{E}(\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}})\otimes{\det}^{-1}_{E}(\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}})\simeq{\det}_{E}(\ker(\phi_{\Gamma}))\otimes{\det}_{E}^{-1}(\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma}))\xrightarrow{B(\phi)}E\,.

Example 4.7
.

In this paragraph, let us consider the special case when
\widetilde{M}=\widetilde{N}=\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1}).
Any morphism

\phi\,:\,\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
can be written in the form

\phi(\widetilde{m})=(a(z)+Xb(z)){\widetilde{m}},\qquad\widetilde{m}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1}),

for some
a(z),b(z)\in{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1}).
We have
\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}}=\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}}=\widetilde{E},

and
\phi_{\Gamma}\,:\,\widetilde{E}\rightarrow\widetilde{E}
is the multiplication by
Xb(0)
map. Therefore, condition (
D1)
) holds if and only if
b(0)\neq 0.

The map
B(\phi)\,:\,X\widetilde{E}\rightarrow\widetilde{E}/X\widetilde{E}
sends
X
to
a^{*}(0):=\left.z^{-1}a(z)\right|_{z=0},
and therefore (
D2)
) holds if and only if
a^{*}(0)\neq 0.

We refer the reader to Lemma
4.9
where we present criteria to check the properties (
D1)
) and (
D2)
). We employ these criteria in Theorem
4.13
in the context of eigencurve, to check their validity (under suitable assumptions) for the module
\widetilde{M}=\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)
. This is key to the Iwasawa decent formalism for thick Selmer complexes that we develop in §
4.2.5
.

For any
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
-bases
\{m_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
and
\{n_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}

of
\widetilde{M}
and
\widetilde{N}
, let us set

\widetilde{\mathbf{m}}=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{h}m_{i}\in{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}\widetilde{M},\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{n}}=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{h}n_{i}\in{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}\widetilde{N}.

Consider the families
\{m_{i},Xm_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
and
\{n_{i},Xn_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}

as
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
-bases of
\widetilde{M}
and
\widetilde{N}
and let us set

\mathbf{m}=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{h}(m_{i}\wedge Xm_{i})\in{\det}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}(\widetilde{M}),\qquad\mathbf{n}=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{h}(n_{i}\wedge Xn_{i})\in{\det}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})}(\widetilde{N}).

Let
\mathbf{m}_{\Gamma}\in\det_{E}(\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}})
and
\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}\in\det_{E}(\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}})
denote the images of
\mathbf{m}
and
\mathbf{n}
.

The following statement is the analogue of the descent formula (
3.60
) in this setting:

Proposition 4.8
.

Assume that the map
\phi
satisfies conditions (
D1)
) and (
D2)
). Retaining the notation of §
4.2.3
and §
4.2.3
, let us write

\det(\phi)(\widetilde{\mathbf{m}}\otimes{\widetilde{\mathbf{n}}}^{-1})=a(z)+b(z)X.

Then
a(z)
has a zero of order one at
0
and

i_{\phi}(\mathbf{m}_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbf{n}^{-1}_{\Gamma})=a^{*}(0)\cdot b(0),

where
a^{*}(0)=\left.z^{-1}a(z)\right|_{z=0}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{H}(\Gamma_{1})
is a Bézout ring, it is easy to see that we can choose an
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
-basis
\{m_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
of
M
such that
X\overline{m}_{1}
is an
E
-basis of
\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})
(where
\overline{m}
denotes the image of
m\in M
under the projection
M\to M_{\Gamma}
). Fix an arbitrary
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
-basis
\{n_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
of
N
. Then

\phi(m_{i})=\underset{j=1}{\overset{h}{\sum}}(a_{ij}(z)+Xb_{ij}(z))n_{j},

where
a_{1j}(0)=0
for all
1\leqslant j\leqslant h.

Set
a_{1j}^{*}(0):=\left.z^{-1}a_{1j}(z)\right|_{z=0}.

Using the fact that
a_{1j}(0)=0
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant h,

it is easy to see that

(4.15)

a^{*}(0)\cdot b(0)=\det(A)\cdot\det(B)

where

A=\left(\begin{matrix}a_{11}^{*}(0)&a_{12}^{*}(0)&\ldots&a_{1h}^{*}(0)\\
a_{11}(0)&a_{12}(0)&\ldots&a_{1h}(0)\\
\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\ddots\\
a_{h1}(0)&a_{h2}(0)&\cdots&a_{hh}(0)\end{matrix}\right),\qquad B=\left(\begin{matrix}b_{11}(0)&b_{12}(0)&\ldots&b_{1h}(0)\\
a_{11}(0)&a_{12}(0)&\ldots&a_{1h}(0)\\
\cdots&\cdots&\cdots&\ddots\\
a_{h1}(0)&a_{h2}(0)&\cdots&a_{hh}(0)\end{matrix}\right).

Then

B(\phi)(Xm_{1})=\underset{j=1}{\overset{h}{\sum}}a_{ij}^{*}(0)n_{j}\mod{\mathrm{im}(\phi_{\Gamma})}\,,

and
i_{\phi}(\mathbf{m}_{\Gamma}\otimes\mathbf{n}_{\Gamma}^{-1})
is equal to the determinant

\det\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}cc|cc|cc|cc@{}}0&b_{11}(0)&0&b_{12}(0)&\cdots&\cdots&0&b_{1h}(0)\\
a_{11}^{*}(0)&0&a_{12}^{*}(0)&0&\cdots&\cdots&a_{1h}^{*}(0)&0\\
\hline\cr a_{21}(0)&b_{21}(0)&a_{22}(0)&b_{22}(0)&\cdots&\cdots&a_{2h}(0)&b_{2h}(0)\\
0&a_{21}(0)&0&a_{22}(0)&\cdots&\cdots&0&a_{2h}(0)\\
\hline\cr\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&&\vdots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\hline\cr a_{21}(0)&b_{21}(0)&a_{22}(0)&b_{22}(0)&\cdots&\cdots&a_{hh}(0)&b_{hh}(0)\\
0&a_{h1}(0)&0&a_{h2}(0)&\cdots&\cdots&0&a_{hh}(0)\end{array}\right)

Using elementary row and column operations, it is easy to see that this determinant is equal to a determinant of the form

-\det\left(\begin{array}[]{@{}c|c@{}}*&A\\
\hline\cr B&0\end{array}\right)=\det(A)\det(B)\,.

Comparing this computation with (
4.15
), we obtain the lemma
for our particular choice of
\{m_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
.

The general case can be reduced to this particular case by a change of basis.
∎

Let
\widetilde{M}
be a
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
-module satisfying the condition of Proposition
4.3
. Recall that
\widetilde{M}
has a free resolution

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{P}_{1}\xrightarrow{\,\,\phi\,\,}\widetilde{P}_{0}\longrightarrow\widetilde{M}\longrightarrow 0.

Then
{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}(\widetilde{M})\simeq{\det}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}(\widetilde{P}_{0})\otimes{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}(\widetilde{P}_{1})
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Assume in addition that
\dim_{E}({\widetilde{M}}^{\,\Gamma_{1}})=1
. Then:

i)
\widetilde{M}^{\Gamma_{1}}\subset X\widetilde{M}
.

ii) The map

\displaystyle B\,:\,{\widetilde{M}}^{\,\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}},

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle\longmapsto y\mod{z\widetilde{M}},\qquad\textrm{where $B(m)=Xy$}

is well-defined (namely, it does not depend on the choice of
y
).

iii) The map
\phi\,:\,\widetilde{P}_{1}\rightarrow\widetilde{P}_{0}
satisfies the condition (
D1)
) and we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\widetilde{M}}^{\,\Gamma_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B(\phi)}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{M}}_{\Gamma_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma}).}

In particular,
\phi
satisfies the condition (
D2)
) if and only if
B
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward and is omitted.
∎

Let
\widetilde{C}^{\bullet}
be a complex of
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
-modules concentrated in a single
even
degree
i

and such that
H^{i}(\widetilde{C}^{\bullet})
satisfies the conditions
of Proposition
4.3
. Let

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{P}_{1}\xrightarrow{\,\,\phi\,\,}\widetilde{P}_{0}\longrightarrow H^{i}(\widetilde{C}^{\bullet})\longrightarrow 0

be a free resolution of
H^{i}(\widetilde{C}^{\bullet}).
There exists a unique, up to a homotopy, lift of the resolution above to a quasi-isomorphism

\textstyle{\widetilde{P}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\widetilde{P}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{C}^{i-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{C}^{i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{C}^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{C}^{i+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdots.}

Set
\widetilde{C}^{\bullet}_{0}=\widetilde{C}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})}^{\mathbb{L}}E.

The diagram above provides us with a trivialization

(4.16)

{\eta}_{0}\,:\,{\det}_{E}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}^{\bullet}_{0})\simeq{\det}_{E}^{-1}((\widetilde{P}_{1})_{\Gamma_{1}}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{P}_{0})_{\Gamma_{1}})\xrightarrow{i_{\phi}}E,

where
i_{\phi}
is the map (
4.14
).
If
\widetilde{P}_{\bullet}^{\prime}
is another free resolution of
H^{i}(\widetilde{C}^{\bullet}),

then there exists a unique, up to a homotopy, quasi-isomorphism
\widetilde{P}_{\bullet}^{\prime}\simeq\widetilde{P}_{\bullet}.
This implies that
{\eta}_{0}
does not depend on the choice of
\widetilde{P}_{\bullet}.

The map
{\eta}_{0}
should be seen as an analogue of the trivialization map (
3.57
) in classical Iwasawa theory.

Remark 4.10
.

Let us consider the special case when
h=2
and consider the map

\phi\,:\,\widetilde{M}\rightarrow\widetilde{N}

between two free
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma_{1})
-modules of rank
2

given by the diagonal matrix of the form

\left(\begin{matrix}a_{1}(z)+Xb_{1}(z)&0\\
0&a_{2}(z)+Xb_{2}(z)\end{matrix}\right)

with
a_{1}(0)=a_{2}(0)=0
and
b_{1}(0),b_{2}(0)\neq 0.

Then
\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})=X\widetilde{M},
and in particular,

\dim_{E}(\ker(\phi_{\Gamma}))=\dim_{E}(\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma}))=2.
In this situation, we still can
define the isomorphisms
B(\phi)\,:\,\ker(\phi_{\Gamma})\rightarrow\mathrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma})
and

i_{\phi}\,:\,{\det}_{E}(\widetilde{M}_{\Gamma_{1}})\otimes{\det}^{-1}_{E}(\widetilde{N}_{\Gamma_{1}})\rightarrow E
via the same formulae. An easy computation gives

i_{\phi}(m_{\Gamma}\otimes n_{\Gamma}^{-1})=a_{1}^{*}(0)a_{2}^{*}(0)b_{1}(0)b_{2}(0).

On the other hand,
\det(\phi)(\widetilde{m}\otimes\widetilde{n}^{-1})=a_{1}(z)a_{2}(z)+X(a_{1}(z)b_{2}(z)+b_{1}(z)a_{2}(z)).
This shows that Proposition
4.8

can not be directly generalized to the case when
\dim_{E}(\ker(\phi_{\Gamma}))>1.

4.2.4.
The Bockstein map for thick Selmer complexes

In the present subsection, we study the Bockstein morphism
B(\phi)
in the context of thick Selmer complexes. The main results of §
4.2.4
, which are recorded as Theorem
4.13
(where we explicitly describe the Bockstein morphism for thick Selmer complexes and its key properties), will be used in §
4.2.5
to prove various leading term formulae (cf. Theorems
4.14
and
4.15
) employing our Iwasawa descent formalism for thick Selmer complexes.

The reader familiar with
[
Nek06
]
will realize that the overall structure of this portion of our work is akin to §11.6 and §11.7 in op. cit.: Nekovář studies Bockstein morphisms and their basic properties in
[
Nek06
, §11.6]
to establish a descent formalism, and applies this with his Selmer complexes in
[
Nek06
, §11.7]
to prove what he calls “formulas of the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer type”. Note however that his formalism does not apply in the setting we have placed ourselves.

We start with several
auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
be an integer. Assume that one of the following conditions holds:

j\neq\frac{k}{2}
.

j=\frac{k}{2}
and
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))=\{0\}
.

Then,

\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))^{\Gamma}=\{0\}=\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))_{\Gamma}\,.

Proof.

The commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{k}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S}{\oplus}H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

induces the exact sequence

\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{f}(j))\,.

Let
A
(resp.
B\subset\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{f}(j))
) denote the coimage (resp. the image) of the first (resp. the second) map. Consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow A\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))\longrightarrow B\longrightarrow 0\,.

By the snake lemma, we have an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow A^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow B^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow A_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow B_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow 0\,.

Recall that that if
M
is a finitely generated torsion
\Lambda_{E}
-module,
then
M
is of finite dimension over
E
, and

(4.17)

M^{\Gamma}=\{0\}\,\iff\,M_{\Gamma}=\{0\},

cf. (
3.58
).
Since
\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{f}(k/2))_{\Gamma}\simeq H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))^{*}
, we have

\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))^{\Gamma}=\{0\}=\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}
in both cases (a) and (b). This implies that the modules
A^{\Gamma}
,
B_{\Gamma}
, and therefore
A_{\Gamma}
and
B^{\Gamma}
, vanish. Thus

\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))^{\Gamma}=\{0\}=\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))_{\Gamma},

as required.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
be an integer. Assume that one of the following conditions holds:

j\neq\frac{k}{2}
.

j=\frac{k}{2}
and
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(k/2))=\{0\}=H^{1}_{0}(V_{f^{*}}(k/2))
.

Then the rows of the following commutative diagram are exact and the vertical morphisms are injective:

(4.18)

29.5pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 104.70985pt\raise-37.62196pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.9764pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{k}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 123.44138pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 123.44138pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 217.11884pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 217.11884pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 311.06021pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 311.06021pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

In particular, for every such
j
we have

\textup{res}_{p}\circ\iota_{k}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\right)=\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))\right)\cap\iota_{k}\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\right)=\iota_{k}\circ\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\right)\,.

Proof.

Since
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))=\{0\}=H^{0}(V_{f}(j))
, the rows of the diagram are exact except possibly in the right-most surjection on the top.

We have an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))

of
\Lambda_{E}
-modules, where
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
is free of rank one and
H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))
is a
\Lambda_{E}
-torsion module (cf.
[
Kat04
]
, Theorem 13.4). Therefore,
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))
is free of rank
2
over
\Lambda_{E}
.
It follows from Lemma
3.42
and Lemma
4.11
that

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{f}(j))_{\Gamma}\quad\hbox{ and }\quad H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))=H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))_{\Gamma}\,.

Therefore,
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))=1
and
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))=2.

This shows that the map
H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))\rightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))
is surjective, and we have proved that the rows of the diagram are exact.

It follows from the vanishing
H^{1}_{0}(V_{f}(j))=\{0\}
that the left-most and the right-most vertical maps are injective. Therefore, the vertical map in the middle is injective as well. The final assertion can be proved by a diagram chase.

∎

We now prove the main result of this section.
Let
g
denote the map

(4.19)

\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\right)\oplus\left(D_{k}[j]\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\right)\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}\,+\,\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(\chi^{j})}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\,,

where we use the notation from Section
2.1.2
.
If
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f})\neq 0,
the map
g
is injective,
and we regard (
4.19
) as a resolution of

\mathrm{coker}(g)
. Since
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\zeta_{p^{\infty}}),V_{k}^{*}(1-j))=\{0\},

we also have
H^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))=\{0\},
and the sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Since
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))
is free over
\Lambda_{E}
, this implies that
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))
is
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}
-free,
and therefore
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)
is
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)
-free.

Let us denote by
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{(\mathds{1})}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)
the isotypic component corresponding to the trivial character of
\Delta=\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})/\mathbb{Q})
. Note that the
\Gamma_{1}
-invariants (resp.
\Gamma_{1}
-coinvariants) of this component coincide with the
\Gamma
-invariants (resp.
\Gamma
-coinvariants) of
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)
.

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
be an integer such that either
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
, or else
j=\frac{k}{2}
and
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0
.

We have

\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)^{\Gamma}\simeq\mathrm{coker}(g)^{\Gamma}\,,\qquad\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)_{\Gamma}\simeq\mathrm{coker}(g)_{\Gamma}\,.

Moreover:

Assume that the condition (
GP)
) holds. There exists a commutative diagram

(4.20)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 42.31635pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-42.31635pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 77.15768pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.24902pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 143.64532pt\raise-24.13611pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-42.31635pt\raise-32.7206pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
