48.19135pt\raise-28.37631pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.31635pt\raise-32.7206pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.31635pt\raise-32.7206pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\dfrac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}{\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j)))},}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

where the vertical map on the right
\widetilde{\delta}
is given by

\widetilde{\delta}(Xd)=-(j-1)!\left(\frac{1-p^{j-1}\alpha^{-1}}{1-p^{-j}\alpha}\right)\left(\exp^{*}_{V^{(\alpha)}_{f}(j)}\right)^{-1}({d}),\qquad d\in D_{k}[j].

The following conditions are equivalent.

1)

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\neq 0
.

2)

The condition (
GP)
) holds,
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)^{\Gamma}
is a one dimensional
E
-vector space
and the map

B\,:\,\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)_{\Gamma}

defined as in Lemma
4.9
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

In all cases we consider,
H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f}(\frac{k}{2})=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(V_{f^{*}}(\frac{k}{2}))=0.
Therefore we can
apply Lemmas
4.11
and
4.12
. By the definition of the Selmer complex, we have an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathrm{coker}(g)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\longrightarrow 0\,.

Applying the snake lemma to the diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\gamma-1]}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{2}(V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

obtained from this exact sequence and Lemma
4.11
, we deduce that

(4.21)

\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)^{\Gamma}\simeq\mathrm{coker}(g)^{\Gamma}\quad\hbox{ and }\quad\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)_{\Gamma}\simeq\mathrm{coker}(g)_{\Gamma}.

This proves the very first assertion in our theorem.

Since

\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\right)^{\Gamma}=\{0\}\,,

it follows that

\textrm{coker}(g)^{\Gamma}=\ker(g_{\Gamma}):=\ker\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))_{\Gamma}\oplus D_{k}[j]\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}\,+\,\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(\chi^{j}),0}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\right).

Moreover, by Proposition
2.4
and Proposition
2.6
, we have

\ker\left(\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(\chi^{j}),0}\right)=XD_{k}[j]\subset D_{k}[j]\quad,\qquad\textrm{im}\left(\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(j),0}\right)=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},XV^{(\beta)}_{k}(j)).

Note that
XD_{k}[j]\simeq D^{(\alpha)}[j]
and
(XV_{k})^{(\beta)}(j)\simeq V^{(\beta)}_{f}(j).

Since

\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\right)\cap H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V^{(\beta)}_{f}(j))=H^{1}_{\beta}(V_{f}(j))=\{0\}

and
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))_{\Gamma}\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))
, it follows from Lemma
4.12
that

\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))_{\Gamma}\right)\cap\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(j),0}(D_{k}[j])=\{0\}\,.

This yields a canonical isomorphism

\textrm{coker}(g)^{\Gamma}\simeq\ker\left(\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{k}(\chi^{j}),0}\right)=XD_{k}[j]\simeq D^{(\alpha)}[j]\,.

On the other hand, it follows from Lemma
4.12
that

\dim_{E}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))\right)=2\quad\hbox{ and }\quad\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))\right)\cap\iota_{k}\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j))\right)=\{0\}\,.

Therefore,

\textrm{coker}(g)_{\Gamma}\simeq\textrm{coker}(g_{\Gamma}):=\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))}{\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{k}(j))\right)+\iota_{k}\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}^{(\beta)}(j)\right)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}{\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j))\right)}\,,

where the last isomorphism is induced by the projection
V_{k}(j)\rightarrow V_{f}(j)
. In particular,

\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)_{\Gamma}=\dim_{E}\left(\textrm{coker}(g)_{\Gamma}\right)=1.

Let
0\rightarrow\widetilde{P}_{1}\xrightarrow{\phi}\widetilde{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\to 0
be a free resolution. Since
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)
is free,
there exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j))\oplus D_{k}[j]\right)\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{k}(j))\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{coker}(g)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{P}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\widetilde{P}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0}

with exact rows and columns. Applying the snake lemma, we obtain the following cartesian squares, where all maps are

Therefore, we have a commutative diagram

where the vertical map on the right is given by

\widetilde{\delta}(Xd)=\mathrm{pr}_{0}(y),\quad\textrm{where $d\in D_{k}[j]$ and $\mathrm{Exp}_{V^{(\alpha)}_{x_{0}}(j),j}(d)=(\gamma-1)y.$}

The identity (
3.47
) allows us to write

y=\ell(V_{f}(j))\cdot\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(x),

where
\ell(V_{f}(j))=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1-k+j\leqslant i\leqslant j-1\\
i\neq 0\end{subarray}}{\prod}\ell_{i}.

Therefore, using (
1.23
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}(Xd)=\left(\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant i\leqslant k-1\\
i\neq k-j\end{subarray}}{\prod}(i+j-k)\right)\mathrm{pr}_{0}\circ\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,j}(x)=-(j-1)!\left(\frac{1-p^{j-1}\alpha^{-1}}{1-p^{-j}\alpha}\right)\left(\exp^{*}_{V^{(\alpha)}_{f}(j)}\right)^{-1}(d).

Now the proof of (ii) follows from (
4.22
)
and (
4.23
).

The implication

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\neq 0\quad\implies{\quad\eqref{item_C4}}

is explained in Remark
0.4.3
(ii). We now use (
3.14
) to see that the map
\widetilde{\delta}
is an isomorphism if and only if
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(j))\neq 0
. This concludes the proof of (ii).
∎

4.2.5.
Iwasawa descent

The main results in this subsection are Theorems
4.14
and
4.15
, where we prove various leading term formulae, employing Iwasawa descent for thick Selmer complexes which we established in the earlier portions (§
4.2.3
and §
4.2.4
) of the present chapter.

Let us set

\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\widetilde{E}.

Note that

\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}E

as complexes of
E
-vector spaces. For an integer
n
that verifies (
L1)
) and (
L2)
), let us put

\mathbf{R\Gamma}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right).

More explicitly,

\mathbf{R\Gamma}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)=\begin{cases}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\right)&\textrm{if $\ell\neq p$}\\
N_{k}[j][-1]&\textrm{if $\ell=p$}.\end{cases}

We will consider the fundamental line

\displaystyle\Delta_{\widetilde{E}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right):=

\displaystyle\,\,{\det}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{S}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\right)\oplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\right)\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}{\det}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}}\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\right).

over
\mathcal{O}^{(n)}_{E}
. Note that

\Delta_{\widetilde{E}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\simeq\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\mathcal{O}_{E}^{(n)}\,.

We will also consider a fundamental line over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, which we define on setting

(4.24)

\Delta_{E}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right):=\\
{\det}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\left(\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{S}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j)\right)\bigoplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\right)\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}{\det}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\left(\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},T_{k}^{(n)}(j))\right).

We remark that

\Delta_{E}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\simeq{\det}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Delta_{\widetilde{E}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right).

By the functoriality of
\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\Lambda^{(n)}}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{E},

there exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{(\alpha)}_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}(j)}}
\scriptstyle{\otimes_{\Lambda^{(n)}}^{\mathbf{L}}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{E}}
\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathrm{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}}\widetilde{E}}
\textstyle{\Delta_{\widetilde{E}}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\Theta}_{V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{E}}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha).}

Applying the functor
{\det}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}(-)
on the bottom row of this diagram,
we obtain a map

\Theta_{V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\,:\,\Delta_{E}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(j),N_{k}^{(n)}[j]\right)\longrightarrow{\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\,.

Let

i_{V_{k}(j)}\,:\,{\det}_{E}^{-1}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\rightarrow E,

denote the trivialization
map (
4.16
)
for the complex
\widetilde{C}^{\bullet}=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha).

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
be an integer such that either
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
, or else
j=\frac{k}{2}
and
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0
. Assume that

.
Then there exists a canonical isomorphism

with the following properties:

ii)

The map
\mu

Here
\widetilde{\mathrm{Exp}}_{\beta,j,0}

),
and

is placed in degree one. This diagram gives rise to an exact triangle

XD_{k}[j][-1]\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}(XV_{k}(j),D_{k}[j])\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\,.

Let
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{0}(V_{k}/XV_{k}(j),\alpha)

\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{S}(V_{k}/XV_{k}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{k}/XV_{k}(j))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

with strict local conditions at

. Note that we canonically have

. The exact sequence of local conditions

0\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},XV_{k}(j),D_{k}[j]))\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{k}(j),D_{k}[j])\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\bigoplus}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},V_{k}/XV_{k}(j))\longrightarrow 0

provides us with an exact triangle

\mathbf{R\Gamma}(XV_{k}(j),D_{k}[j])\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{0}(V_{k}/XV_{k}(j),\alpha)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}(XV_{k}(j),D_{k}[j])[1]\,.

Combined with (

), we obtain canonical isomorphisms

(4.29)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha)&\simeq{\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{0}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\otimes{\det}^{-1}_{E}XD_{k}[j]\otimes{\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\\
&\simeq{\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\otimes{\det}^{-1}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\beta)\,,\end{aligned}

where we use the isomorphism

to write

We define
\mu
as the composition of the maps in (

).
Let

be a free resolution of
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(j),\alpha).

By Theorem
4.13
and Proposition

, we have the following

\textstyle{\ker(f_{\Gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B(\phi)}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{XD_{k}[j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\delta}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{D^{(\alpha)}[j]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\textrm{coker}(\phi_{\Gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V_{f}(j))}{\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(V_{f}(j)))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)\quad.}

Since the Bockstein map
\mathrm{Bock}_{2}
trivializes
{\det}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{f}(j),\alpha)

and
B(\phi)
trivializes
{\det}_{E}\mathbf{R\Gamma}(V_{k}(j),\alpha),
it is immediate from the construction of

that the diagram
(
4.25
) commutes.
Using the diagram (

), it is now easy to check that the map
\mu
sends

\Theta_{V_{k}(j)}^{(\alpha)}(\Delta_{E}(T^{(n)}_{k}(j)),N_{k}^{(n)}[j])

onto

a\cdot\Theta_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\left(\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\Theta_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}\left(\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\right)\,,

where

is such that

which is precisely Part (ii) in the statement of our theorem.

Finally, (iii) follows from (i), (ii) and Lemma
3.38
,
which tells us that

\displaystyle i_{V_{x_{0}}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\circ\Theta_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\alpha)}\left(\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\alpha)\right)=\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[j],\mathds{1},0),

\displaystyle i_{V_{x_{0}}(j)}^{(\beta)}\circ\Theta_{V_{f}(j)}^{(\beta)}\left(\Delta_{E}(T_{f}(j),\beta)\right)=\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0).

This completes the proof of our theorem.
∎

The following theorem is evidence for the infinitesimal thickening

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

of the
\theta
-critical Main Conjecture, as it shows (among other things) that it is concurrent with the conclusion of Theorem
2.23
and slope-zero Main Conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

.

Theorem 4.15
.

Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
be an integer such that either
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
, or else
j=\frac{k}{2}
and
L(f^{*},\frac{k}{2})\neq 0.

Assume that

\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cr}}(V_{f}(k-j))\neq 0
,

the infinitesimal thickening

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

of the
\theta
-critical Main Conjecture is valid.

Then,

(4.30)

L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})^{\pm}\sim_{p}C_{\mathrm{K}}\cdot{\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*};\chi^{j})\cdot\frac{b(\mathds{1},k-j)}{a(\mathds{1},k-j)}}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\beta}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)^{\pm}\,.

Proof.

The Infinitesimal Main Conjecture reads

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}^{\iota}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(k),N_{k}[k]\right)=\Lambda^{(n)}\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})^{\iota},\qquad\textrm{for $n\gg 0.$}

Twisting both sides, we deduce that

\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{j}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}^{\iota}\right)\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(k-j),N_{k}[k-j]\right)=\Lambda^{(n)}\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{-j}\circ\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\right)^{\iota},\qquad\textrm{for $n\gg 0.$}

We can rewrite the final equality in the form

\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{j}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{N}^{\iota}\right)\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}\left(T_{k}^{(n)}(k-j),N_{k}[k-j]\right)=\left(a+\frac{X}{\varpi_{E}^{n}}b\right)\left(\mathrm{Tw}_{-j}\circ\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\right)^{\iota},

where
a+\frac{X}{\varpi_{E}^{n}}b\in\Lambda^{(n)}
is a unit; this is equivalent to saying that
a\in\Lambda^{\times}
and
b\in\Lambda.

By Theorem
4.13
,
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(k-j),\alpha)^{\Gamma}

is one-dimensional, and we can apply our descent formalism with the lattice

\Theta_{\mathrm{Iw},V_{k}({k-}j)}^{(\alpha)}(\Delta_{\mathrm{Iw}}(T_{k}^{(n)}(k-j),N_{k}[k-j])\subset{\det}^{-1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)}\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(V_{k}(k-j),\alpha).

Recall that

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})=L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})+XL_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})\,.

The function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
has a simple zero at all characters
\chi^{j}
(where
j
is as in the theorem), and we denote by
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],*}(f^{*},\xi^{*},\chi^{j})
its special value at
\chi^{j}.

Proposition
4.8
implies that
the map
i_{V_{k}({k-}j)}
sends

{\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*};\chi^{j})^{2}\cdot}\Theta^{(\alpha)}_{V_{k}({k-}j)}\left(\Delta_{E}(T_{k}^{(n)}(k-j),N_{k}^{(n)}[k-j])\right)

onto

(4.31)

a(0)\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],*}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})\left(a\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})+{\frac{b}{\varpi_{E}^{n}}}\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0]}(f^{*},\xi^{*},\chi^{j})\right)\\
={a(0)^{2}\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],*}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j}).}

On the other hand, Theorem
4.14
yields

i_{V_{k}(k-j)}\circ\Theta_{V_{k}({k-}j)}^{(\alpha)}\left(\Delta_{E}(T^{(n)}_{k}({k-}j),N_{k}^{(n)}[{k-}j])\right)=\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{f}(j),N_{\alpha}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\beta}[k-j],\mathds{1},0).

Therefore,

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],*}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})

\displaystyle\,\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})

\displaystyle\sim_{p}C_{\mathrm{K}}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*};\chi^{j})^{2}\cdot\frac{b(\mathds{1},k-j)}{a(\mathds{1},k-j)}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\alpha}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(j),N_{\beta}[j],\mathds{1},0).

Note that Conjecture

MC

(
f
,
α
)

(which follows from

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

by Proposition
4.6
) implies that

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[0],*}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})\sim_{p}\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*};\chi^{j})\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\alpha}^{*}(T_{x_{0}}(k-j),N_{\alpha}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)\,.

Hence,

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}^{[1]}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})\sim_{p}C_{\mathrm{K}}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{N}({f^{*};\chi^{j}})\cdot{\frac{b(\mathds{1},k-j)}{a(\mathds{1},k-j)}}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\beta}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)\,,

and the proof of our theorem is complete.
∎

Corollary 4.16
.

The formula (
4.30
) can be rewritten in the form

L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})^{\pm}\sim_{p}C_{\mathrm{K}}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*};\chi^{j})\cdot\frac{e_{p,\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\mathds{1},j)}{e_{p,\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\mathds{1},j)}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\beta}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)^{\pm}\,.

Proof.

This follows from the equalities

\frac{b(\mathds{1},k-j)}{a(\mathds{1},k-j)}=\frac{e_{p,\alpha}(f,\mathds{1},k-j)}{e_{p,\beta}(f\mathds{1},k-j)}=\frac{e_{p,\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\mathds{1},j)}{e_{p,\beta^{*}}(f^{*}\mathds{1},j)}\,.

∎

Remark 4.17
.

It follows from Theorem
2.23
(see especially Equations (
2.45
) and (
2.48
)) that

L^{[1]}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})^{\pm}=C_{K}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{N}(f^{*};\chi^{j})\cdot\frac{e_{p,\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\mathds{1},j)}{e_{p,\beta^{*}}(f^{*}\mathds{1},j)}\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})^{\pm}.

Combining this formula with Corollary
4.16
, we deduce that

L_{\mathrm{K},\beta^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*};\chi^{j})^{\pm}\sim_{p}\cdot\mathbf{L}_{\mathrm{Iw},\beta}(T_{f}(k-j),N_{\beta}[k-j],\mathds{1},0)^{\pm}\,.

This also follows from the slope-zero Main Conjecture
3.32
via Iwasawa theoretic descent.
This shows that the conjecture
\widetilde{\mathbf{MC}}(f)

is compatible with the slope-zero Main Conjecture
\mathbf{MC}(f,\beta)
.

Chapter 5
Thick Selmer groups and derivatives of critical
p
-adic
L
-functions

Throughout §
5
, we shall work in the setting of §
2.4.2
. In particular, we assume throughout that the hypothesis (
GP)
) holds true.

5.1.
Recap and motivation

Before we describe our results in the present chapter, we summarize our results in the earlier portions and explain how our results in §
5
complement those.

Recall that in §
2.4.4
, we have introduced a two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)
. We proved that we can write it in the form

(5.1)

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)=\ell(V_{f}(j))\,L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp},\pm}(f,\xi)+X\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi)+\ldots\,,

where
\ell(V_{f}(j))=\left(\underset{i=1-k}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}\ell^{\iota}_{i}\right)

(cf. Theorem
2.23
(i)).

The interpolation properties of
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1]}(f,\xi)
in terms of the critical values of the Hecke
L
-function of
f
(cf. Theorem
2.23
(ii)) suggest that the
thick
p
-adic
L
-function

\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi):=L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\pmod{X^{2}}
also carries arithmetic information, that is not readily visible in
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi):=L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\pmod{X}
.

Based on this insight, we developed a theory of thick Selmer complexes in Chapter
4
and introduced what we call the thick fundamental line. We formulated the Main Conjecture

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

in terms of these to reflect the arithmetic properties of the thick
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\pmod{X^{2}}
.

5.1.1.
Special values

Let
j
be an integer and
r
a natural number. In what follows, we consider the function
{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi,\chi^{s})
,
which is analytic on
j+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and we denote by

\left.\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial s^{r}}{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi,\chi^{s})\right|_{s=j}

its derivative at
s=j
.

The non-central critical
j

Let us now fix an integer
j\neq\frac{k}{2}
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
. In view of (
5.1
), we have the following linearization
of
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi,\chi^{s})
about
X=0
,
s=j
:

(5.2)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi,\chi^{s})\equiv\,(s-j)\cdot\ell^{*}(V_{f}(j))\cdot L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp},\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{j})+X\cdot&L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{j})\\
&\mod(X^{2},X(s-j),(s-j)^{2})\,.\end{aligned}

Here,
\ell^{*}(V_{f}(j)):=\underset{{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1-k\\
i\neq-j\end{subarray}}}{\overset{-1}{\prod}}(i+j)
.

Theorem
2.23
and Proposition
2.27
allows us to compute the linearization
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})
of the thick
p
-adic
L
-function about
s=j
in terms of the Hecke
L
-value
L(f,j)
, assuming that the
\mathcal{L}
-invariant
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}(V_{f}(j))
is nonzero.

Non-critical
j

We may assume without loss of generality that
j\geq k
. Granted the validity of the
\theta
-critical main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
α
)

(which is equivalent, assuming that
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\rm Iw}
is nonzero, to the slope-zero main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

, cf. Proposition
3.34
), we have

L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{j})\neq 0\,.

This value can be explained (up to a
p
-adic unit) in terms of fundamental arithmetic invariants; see Theorem
3.45
.

In summary, assuming that
\mathscr{L}^{\rm cr}_{\rm Iw}(V_{f})\neq 0
and the slope-zero main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

holds true, the leading term of the thick
p
-adic
L
-function
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})
about
s=j
equals
L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{j})
, whose arithmetic meaning is explained by Theorem
3.45
(i).

Special value at the central critical point

Let us assume that
k
is an even integer. In view of our discussion in Sections
5.1.1
–
5.1.1
, the case that remains to understand the arithmetic meaning of
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi)
concerns the central critical point
j=\frac{k}{2}
.

Suppose first that
L(f,\frac{k}{2})\neq 0
. Then the treatment of
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})
about
s=\frac{k}{2}
is identical to the treatment of other critical values (cf. §
5.1.1
and Theorem
4.15
). We will therefore assume until the end of §
5.1.1
that
L(f,\frac{k}{2})=0
. Let us put
r:={\mathrm{ord}}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f,s)
.

Let us assume that the conditions (
BK)
), (
ht)
) and (
h-PR)
) as well as the slope-zero main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

hold true. It follows from (
5.1
) combined with Theorem
2.23
, Proposition
2.27
, Proposition
3.25
(iii), and the proof of Proposition
3.23
that

(5.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi,\chi^{s})\equiv&\left(s-\frac{k}{2}\right)^{r}\cdot\dfrac{\ell^{*}(V_{f}(j))}{(r-1)!}\cdot\frac{\partial^{r-1}}{\partial s^{r-1}}\left.L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp},\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})\right|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}\\
&\,\,+X\cdot\left(s-\frac{k}{2}\right)\cdot\frac{d}{ds}\left.L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})\right|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}\qquad\mod\left(X\left(s-\frac{k}{2}\right)^{2},\left(s-\frac{k}{2}\right)^{r+1}\right).\end{aligned}

Moreover, since the
\theta
-critical main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
α
)

holds true in this scenario, Theorem
3.47
(ii) provides us with an interpretation of the partial derivative

\frac{\partial^{r-1}}{\partial s^{r-1}}\left.L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[0],\mathrm{imp},\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})\right|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}

in terms of the fundamental arithmetic invariants (among which is the
p
-adic regulator
R_{\alpha}(T_{f}(k/2))
).

As the next task at hand, we need to compute the derivative

\frac{d}{ds}L_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{[1],\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})_{|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}}

in terms of
p
-adic heights. A strategy paralleling the discussion in the previous paragraph would require

•

the truth of the thick Main Conjecture

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

,

•

a descent formalism for the thick fundamental line, extending Theorem
4.15
(ii) to cover the case when
j=\frac{k}{2}
and
r>0
.

This doesn’t seem tractable; see Remark
5.16
.

We will instead use a different method to compute
\frac{d}{ds}\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi,\chi^{s})_{|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}}
when
r=1
in terms of
p
-adic heights on the thick Selmer complexes (cf. Theorem
5.29
). In this regard, our results in the present chapter are supplementary to those we have established in Chapters
3
and
4
.

Remark 5.1
.

Proposition
3.19
and Corollary
3.20
shows that when
r=1
, (
5.3
) is valid under a weaker set of assumptions:

•

The hypothesis (
PR)
) holds.

•

The height pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\beta}
is nonzero.

•

Either (
SZ1)
) or (
SZ2)
) is valid, to guarantee that the slope-zero main conjecture

MC

(
f
,
β
)

holds true; cf.
[
SU14
,
Wan15
]
,
[
Ski16
, Theorem 2.5.2]
,
[
Kat04
, Theorem 15.2]
.

As we have explained in the proof of Corollary
3.21
, all these three conditions hold true when
f=f_{A}
is the normalized newform attached to an elliptic curve
A/\mathbb{Q}
.

5.1.2.
This chapter

Our main objective in §
5
is twofold.

Our first goal is to illustrate the relation of thick Selmer groups and the Bloch–Kato Selmer groups in an explicit manner; see in particular Corollary
5.14
.

Our second goal is to compute, when
r=1
, the height pairings we have introduced in §
1.3.1
on thick Selmer groups; cf. Theorem
5.29
.

5.2.
Selmer complexes revisited

5.2.1.
Notation

Throughout §
5.2
, we work in the setting of Section
2.1
.
We study the arithmetic invariants associated to the following objects:

•

A free
\widetilde{E}
-module
\widetilde{V}
of rank
2

equipped with a continuous action of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}.

•

We set
V=\ker(\widetilde{V}\xrightarrow{X}\widetilde{V})
and assume that the
restriction of
V
on the decomposition group at
p
splits as

V=V^{(\alpha)}\oplus V^{(\beta)},

where
V^{(\alpha)}
and
V^{(\beta)}
are one-dimensional crystalline representations with Hodge–Tate weights
1-k
and
0
respectively, with
\alpha
and
\beta
the
\varphi
-eigenvalues on
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V^{(\alpha)})
and
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V^{(\beta)}).
We assume that
\alpha\neq p^{1-k}
. Set
\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}:=\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V^{(\alpha)})
and

(5.4)

\mathbf{D}:=t^{k-1}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}.

•

A non-saturated crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\subset\mathbf{D}_{\mathrm{rig},\widetilde{E}}^{\dagger}(\widetilde{V})
of rank one over the relative Robba ring
\mathcal{R}_{\widetilde{E}}
such that

\ker(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\xrightarrow{X}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\simeq\mathbf{D}.

In this section, we assume that
k
is even. We are interested
in the central twist of the representations
V
and
\widetilde{V}
and set

\displaystyle{}^{\rm c}V:=V(\chi^{\frac{k}{2}}),

\displaystyle{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}:=V(\chi^{\frac{k}{2}})

\displaystyle{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}:=\mathbf{D}(\chi^{\frac{k}{2}}),

\displaystyle{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{\frac{k}{2}}).

The
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation
{}^{\rm c}V
is split in our setting:

(5.5)

\left.{}^{\rm c}V\right|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}={}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)}\oplus{}^{\rm c}V^{(\beta)}\,.

The associated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules can be explicitly described:

\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}({}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)})=\mathscr{R}_{E}(\chi^{1-\frac{k}{2}}\nu_{\alpha})\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}({}^{\rm c}V^{(\beta)})=\mathscr{R}_{E}(\chi^{\frac{k}{2}}\nu_{\beta})\,,

where
\nu_{\alpha}
and
\nu_{\beta}
are unramified characters such that
\nu_{\alpha}(\mathrm{Fr}_{p})=\alpha/p^{k-1}
and
\nu_{\beta}(\mathrm{Fr}_{p})=\beta.

5.2.2.
Punctual Selmer complexes

Our main objective in §
5.2.2
is to
examine the Selmer complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})
and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
associated to the data
({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})
and
({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
as in Section
1.2.2
. In particular, we illustrate the degenerate properties of
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
. This will serve as an added justification for our definition and extensive discussion in Section
5.2.4
of the thick Selmer complexes. We first examine the Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}).

Proposition 5.2
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(5.6)

\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}({}^{\rm c}V,\alpha)

in the derived category. Here
\mathbf{R\Gamma}^{\mathrm{imp}}({}^{\rm c}V,\alpha)
is the Selmer complex of Perrin-Riou given as in §
3.5.2
, where we take
V
in place of
V_{f}
.

Proof.

We have the following commutative diagram of local conditions at
p
:

\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)})[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Exp}_{\alpha,k/2,0}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V).}

where the left vertical map
\mathbf{Exp}_{\alpha,k/2,0}
is induced by the
map
\mathbf{Exp}_{\alpha,k/2}\,:\,\mathfrak{D}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})[-1]\rightarrow\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}).
In degree one, this map coincides with the exponential map

\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,k/2,0}\,:\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})\,.

Equation (
1.23
) reads:

\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,k/2,0}(x)=\frac{(-1)^{k/2}}{(k/2-1)!}(\exp^{*}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}})^{-1}\left((1-\alpha^{-1}p^{k/2-1})(1-\alpha p^{-k/2})^{-1}(x)\right).

Since
v_{p}(\alpha)=k-1
and
\exp^{*}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}}\,:\,H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})
is an isomorphism, the map
\mathrm{Exp}_{\alpha,k/2,0}

is an isomorphism. Moreover
H^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})=0
for
i\neq 1.
Thus

\mathbf{Exp}_{\alpha,k/2,0}
is a quasi-isomorphism, and therefore the corresponding Selmer complexes are quasi-isomorphic.
∎

We turn our attention to the cohomology of
\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}).

Lemma 5.3
.

We have
H^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})=0
(for
i\neq 1
) and
\dim_{E}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})=1.

Moreover, the natural map
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)})

induced by the inclusion
{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}\hookrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}
, is the zero map.

Proof.

The computation of
H^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
follows easily from the Poincaré
duality and the Euler characteristic formula for the cohomology of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules.

Let us consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathscr{D}_{\textrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)})}

The Hodge–Tate weights of
{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}
are positive, and therefore
the left vertical map is an isomorphism. The right vertical map is the zero map, since the Hodge–Tate weight of the
1
-dimensional
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation
{}^{\rm c}V_{f}^{(\alpha)}
is negative. Therefore the bottom row is the zero map.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Let us consider the tautological exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}\longrightarrow\,^{c}\mathbb{D}\longrightarrow 0\,,

where
{}^{c}\mathbb{D}:={}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}/{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}.

It follows from Lemma
5.3
that the map
\partial_{0}\,:\,H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
is an isomorphism. It can be described explicitly as follows.
Let
x\in H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D})
and let
d\in{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}
denote any lift of
x
. Set
y=((\varphi-1)d,(\gamma-1)d).
Then
y\in C^{1}_{\varphi,\gamma}({}^{c}\mathbf{D})
and

\partial_{0}(x)=y.

Proposition 5.5
.

The Selmer group
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
splits canonically into the direct sum

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}),

\displaystyle[(z,(z_{\ell}^{+}),(\lambda_{\ell}))]

\displaystyle\longmapsto\quad\quad\left(\,[z]\,\,,\,\,[z_{p}^{+}]\,\right).

Proof.

This is proved in a manner identical to
[
Ben20
, Proposition 7.1.2]
. The exact sequence (
1.31
) combined with the fact that

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V)=\{0\}

shows that

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})=\ker\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},{}^{\rm c}V)\oplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\bigoplus}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\right)\xrightarrow{f_{S}-g_{S}}\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)\right).

Since
H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)\xrightarrow{f_{\ell}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)
is the natural inclusion for
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}
and
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\xrightarrow{g_{p}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V)
is the zero-map by Lemma
5.3
,
we have a natural identification

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\,\,\,

\displaystyle=\,\ker\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},{}^{\rm c}V)\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\bigoplus}\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)}{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)}\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V)\right)\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})

\displaystyle\simeq H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\,,

\displaystyle[(z,(z_{\ell}^{+}),(\lambda_{\ell}))]

\displaystyle\longmapsto\quad\quad\left(\,[z]\,\,,\,\,[z_{p}^{+}]\,\right)

where the second equality follows from the definition of the fine Selmer group
H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)
, cf. §
1.2.3
. This completes the proof of our proposition.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

Let
f
be an eigenform with the critical stabilisation
f^{\alpha},

and let
V:=V_{f}
,
\mathbf{D}:=t^{k-1}\mathbf{D}^{(\alpha)}.
Proposition
5.5
tells us that
R^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
extends the fine Selmer group
H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)
, rather than the Bloch–Kato Selmer group, by the local cohomology group
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
. This is an indication as to why the Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})
may not capture some of the arithmetic invariants of
f
(that the Bloch–Kato Selmer group encodes).

We continue to work in the setting of §
5.2.2
. Assume that we have a
Galois equivariant non-degenerate bilinear pairing

(5.8)

V^{\prime}\otimes V\longrightarrow E(1-k).

It follows from the non-degeneracy of this pairing that the
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-representation
V^{\prime}
also splits into the direct sum

V^{\prime}=V^{\prime(\alpha)}\oplus V^{\prime(\beta)},

where
V^{\prime(\alpha)}
(respectively
V^{\prime(\beta)}
) is the orthogonal complement of

V^{(\beta)}
(respectively
V^{(\alpha)}
). Set
\mathbf{D}^{\prime(\alpha)}:=\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}(V^{\prime(\alpha)})
and
\mathbf{D}^{\prime}:=t^{k-1}\mathbf{D}^{\prime(\alpha)}.

The pairing (
5.8
) induces

{}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}\otimes{}^{\rm c}V\longrightarrow E(1)\,,

which gives rise to the
p
-adic height pairing

\left<\,,\,\right>_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}=\left<\,,\,\right>^{\textup{cyc}}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}\,:\,\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\longrightarrow E[-2]\,.

Proposition 5.7
.

We have a commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where the left vertical map is induced by the decomposition (
5.7
), and the
p
-adic height pairing
\left<\,\,,\,\,\right>_{\alpha}
is induced by the composition

H_{0}^{1}({}^{\rm c}V)\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}V)^{\Gamma}\longrightarrow\Sha_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}V)_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow H_{0}^{1}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime})^{*}\,,

(cf. §
3.5.3
).

Proof.

Employing Part (iii) of Proposition
1.9
with
\mathbf{D}_{1}^{\prime}=0
and taking
\mathbf{D}_{2}^{\prime}=\mathbf{D}^{\prime}
to be the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module associated to any splitting module, we infer that the restriction of any
p
-adic height pairing (associated to any splitting module) to
H^{1}_{0}
is independent of the choice of the said splitting module, and therefore coincides with
\left<\,\,,\,\,\right>_{\alpha}.

Since the
p
-adic height pairing factors through the Bockstein morphisms by its definition (cf. Equation (
1.38
)),
the proof follows from the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the horizontal maps are the Bockstein maps, together with the fact that
H^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}})=\{0\}
, which we have checked in Lemma
5.3
.
∎

Corollary 5.8
.

In the setting of Theorem
5.7
, assume that
H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)=\{0\}
. Then the height pairing

\left<\,\,,\,\,\right>_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}\,\,:\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V,{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})\longrightarrow E

on the extended Selmer groups vanishes.

Remark 5.9
.

Corollary
5.8
is further evidence as to why the Selmer complex attached to a
\theta
-critical modular form
f_{\alpha}
does not capture all the arithmetic invariants associated with
f
. Together with Remark
5.6
, they serve as a justification for the study of what we call thick Selmer complexes in §
5.2.4
below.

5.2.3.
Thick Selmer groups

In this section, we study various Selmer groups naturally attached to the representation
{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}=\widetilde{V}(\chi^{k/2}).
The relevant Selmer
complex will be introduced in the next section.

Definition 5.10
.

The thick Greenberg Selmer group is defined by setting

\mathrm{Sel}_{\alpha}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}):=\ker\left({H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}\longrightarrow\underset{\ell\neq p}{\bigoplus}\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}\bigoplus\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X\cdot{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}\right).

The sequence (
4.1
) induces the exact sequence

(5.9)

0\longrightarrow{H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V)}\xrightarrow{[X]}{H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}})\longrightarrow{H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V)}

where the injection on the left is because
{H^{0}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V)}=\{0\}
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Assume that
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)=0
for all
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}.

Then the sequence (
5.9
) gives rise to an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V)}\xrightarrow{[X]}\mathrm{Sel}_{\alpha}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\rightarrow H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)\,.

Proof.

Let
\ell\neq p
be a prime in
S
. Since
\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)=\dim_{E}H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V),
we have that

H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)=0,\qquad\forall\ell\neq p.

It then follows from the short exact sequence

H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)

that
H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})=0
. For
\ell=p,
the tautological exact sequence

0\longrightarrow X{}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)}\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}/X{}^{\rm c}V^{(\beta)}\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}V\longrightarrow 0

induces the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}/X{}^{\rm c}V^{(\beta)})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V),

where
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)})\simeq H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}V)/H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}V).
Let us set

\displaystyle U_{\mathrm{f}}:=\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\oplus}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}V^{(\alpha)}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}:=\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\oplus}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}/X{}^{\rm c}V^{(\beta)}),

\displaystyle U_{0}:=\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\oplus}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)\oplus H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V).

We then have a commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{H_{S}^{1}({}^{\rm c}V)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{\mathrm{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{U_{0}\,.}

The exact sequence of the kernels of vertical maps reads

0\longrightarrow{H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V)}\xrightarrow{[X]}\mathrm{Sel}_{\alpha}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\xrightarrow{\,\pi\,}H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)\,,

and the proposition is proved.

∎

5.2.4.
Thick Selmer complexes

In this Section, we are interested in Selmer complexes attached to the representation

{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}:=\,\widetilde{V}(\chi^{k/2}).

The complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
(defined following the formalism of §
1.2.2
) will be called the
thick Selmer complex
and its cohomology groups the
thick (extended) Selmer groups
.

Our primary objective in §
5.2.4
is to compute the thick Selmer group
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
explicitly and show that it is indeed the extension of the true (Bloch–Kato) Selmer group
H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V)
in an important case of interest (e.g. when the Hecke
L
-function of the dual form
f^{*}
has at most a simple zero at
\frac{k}{2}
). This will be the first indication that the thick Selmer complexes are the correct algebraic objects that capture arithmetic properties of the refinement
(f,\alpha)
of
f
(in the sense of Bellaïche), including their extremal exceptional zeros as well as those encoded in the Bloch–Kato Selmer group (which conjecturally explain the leading terms of complex
L
-functions).

We continue to work in the setting of §
5.2.2
. In particular, we assume that the conditions (
C1)
)–(
C3)
) and (
GP)
) hold true.

Proposition 5.12
.

The following assertions are valid:

H^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})=0
for
i\neq 1
, and
the exponential map induces an isomorphism

\exp\,:\,{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}).

We have a quasi-isomorphism

\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\simeq\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\,,

where the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
is as in §
4.2.5
, where we replace
V_{k}
with
\widetilde{V}.

Proof.

On taking the cohomology of the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}\xrightarrow{[X]}{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}\longrightarrow 0

and taking into account that
H^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D})=0
for
i\neq 1,
we
obtain that
H^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})=0
for
i\neq 1.
Moreover,
{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}
is crystalline of weight
k/2\geqslant 1
and
\alpha\neq p^{k/2-1}.
Therefore, the exponential map is an isomorphism, as claimed.

The proof is entirely analogous to the proof of Proposition
5.2
. We have the following commutative diagram of local conditions at
p
:

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Exp}_{{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}},0}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{\alpha,k/2}^{(\mathds{1})}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R\Gamma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\,.}

Here, the horizontal maps correspond to the local conditions at
p
for the complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
, respectively. Recall that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}
is not saturated in
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},E}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})
and has Hodge–Tate weights
(k/2,k/2).
Therefore, the vertical map on the left is an isomorphism in degree one.
Moreover
\mathbf{R}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{i}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})=0

for
i\neq 1.
Thence, the vertical map on the left is a quasi-isomorphism.
This concludes the proof of this portion.

∎

We will compute
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
(in turn also
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
), assuming that

H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)=0.

In this respect, the present section
can be thought of as a supplement to §
4
.
Set
\widetilde{D}={\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
and let us put

{}^{c}\widetilde{D}:=\widetilde{D}[k/2]
.

Proposition 5.13
.

Assume that the hypothesis (
GP)
) holds true and that
H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)=0
. Then:

We have the following natural short exact sequences:

(5.10)

0\longrightarrow X{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\alpha}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow 0\,.

If in addition the condition (

X

)
) holds for
V
, i.e. if the map

H^{1}_{\rm f}(V)\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},V)

is nonzero, then there exists an exact sequence

(5.11)

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\longrightarrow{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\longrightarrow 0\,.

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha):=\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\otimes^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)}E,
we have

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)=\ker\left({H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}\oplus\left(\underset{\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}}{\bigoplus}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\oplus{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\right)\xrightarrow{f_{S}-g_{S}}\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right),

where
g_{p}=\widetilde{\mathrm{E}}_{\alpha,k/2}^{(\mathds{1})}
(cf. the proof of
Proposition
5.12
).
Thanks to this description of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
and since
g_{\ell}
is injective for
\ell\neq p
, the module
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
fits in an exact sequence

(5.12)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0\longrightarrow\ker&\left({}^{c}\widetilde{D}\xrightarrow{g_{p}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\longrightarrow\ker\left({H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{S}}\frac{\underset{\ell\in S}{\bigoplus}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}{{\rm im}(g_{S})}\right)\longrightarrow 0\,.\end{aligned}

We note that

(5.13)

{\rm im}(g_{\ell})=\begin{cases}H^{1}_{{\rm f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})&\hbox{ if }\ell\neq p\,,\\
H^{1}_{{\rm f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X\cdot{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})&\hbox{ if }\ell=p\,,\end{cases}

where this follows from definitions if
\ell\neq p
(cf. §
1.2.1
), whereas in the case
\ell=p
, we refer to Corollary
2.8
. Therefore, the rightmost term in (
5.12
) equals
\mathrm{Sel}_{\alpha}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})
.

Using (
2.6
) and Proposition
2.6

we observe that

\ker\left({}^{c}\widetilde{D}\xrightarrow{\,g_{p}\,}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)=X{}^{c}\widetilde{D}={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{c}V^{(\alpha)})\,.

This combined with the discussion in the previous paragraph completes the proof of the exactness of the first sequence.

It follows from definitions that
H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\subset\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha).

Set
H^{1}_{/0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}):=H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})/H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}).
We then have an exact sequence

(5.14)

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\longrightarrow\ker\left(H^{1}_{/0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\oplus{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)\longrightarrow 0\,.

The localisation map
H^{1}_{/0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})
is injective, and it follows from the exact sequence (
5.9
) and the condition (

X

)
) that

\mathrm{im}\left(H^{1}_{/0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)=H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\,.

On the other hand, it follows from (
GP)
) that

\mathrm{im}\left({}^{c}\widetilde{D}\xrightarrow{\,g_{p}\,}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)=H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}).

Therefore, the map

\ker\left(H^{1}_{/0}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\oplus{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)\longrightarrow{}^{c}\widetilde{D},\qquad(x,y)\mapsto y

is an isomorphism. Using this isomorphism, we can rewrite the exact sequence (
5.14
) as
(
5.11
). This concludes the proof of our proposition.
∎

Using the isomorphisms
X{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\simeq{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\overset{\exp}{\simeq}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
and

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\simeq\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
,
we can rewrite the exact sequence (
5.10
) in the following form:

0\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sel}_{\alpha}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow 0\,.

We record the following consequence of this fact:

Corollary 5.14
.

Assume that
H^{1}_{0}(V)=0
and the condition (
GP)
)
holds. Then there exist an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\,\Delta\,}H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V)\longrightarrow 0\,.

If, in addition, the condition (

X

)
) holds, then
we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}{}^{c}\widetilde{D},

\displaystyle[x^{\mathrm{sel}}]:=[(x,(x^{+}_{\ell})_{\ell\in S},(\lambda_{\ell})_{\ell\in S})]\longmapsto\log_{{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}}(x^{+}_{p})\,,

and the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\log}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\log}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\log}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}^{c}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\,.}

The following proposition computes the Bockstein map on the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha).

Proposition 5.15
.

Suppose that
H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)=0
and
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V)=0
for all
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}.

Assume that the hypotheses (

X

)
) and (
GP)
) hold true. Then:

There exist natural isomorphisms

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\simeq{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\,\,,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\simeq\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}{\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)}\,.

In particular,
\mathbf{R}^{i}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
is a free
\widetilde{E}
-module of rank one
(i=1,2)
.

We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Bock_{2}}}
\textstyle{{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\frac{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})}{\textup{res}_{p}\left(H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\right)}\,,}

where the right vertical map
\vartheta
is given by the following rule: Let

d\in{}^{c}\widetilde{D}
and let
\widehat{d}\in{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\otimes\Lambda
be any lift of

d.
Then there exist
x\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
and

y\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\otimes_{\Lambda_{E}}\mathscr{H}(\Gamma)
such that

\mathrm{Exp}_{{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}}(\widehat{d})=\textup{res}_{p}(x)+(\gamma-1)y\,.

Then
\vartheta(d):=\mathrm{pr}_{0}(y)\mod H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})
.
In particular,
\mathrm{im}(\vartheta)\subset H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})
.

Proof.

Since
H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V)=0,

it follows from Lemma
4.11

that

\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})^{\Gamma}=\{0\}=\Sha^{2}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})_{\Gamma}
, and

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)=\mathrm{coker}(g)^{\Gamma}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)=\mathrm{coker}(g)_{\Gamma}\,,

where
g
is the map (
4.19
) with
j=\frac{k}{2}
:

\left(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\right)\oplus\left({}^{c}\widetilde{D}\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\right)\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}\,+\,\mathrm{Exp}_{{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\otimes_{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{E}}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}(\Gamma)\,.

Therefore, as in the previous chapter, we infer that
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
and
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
can be naturally identified with the kernel and the cokernel, respectively, of the map

g_{\Gamma}\,:\,H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})_{\Gamma}\oplus{}^{c}\widetilde{D}\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}\,+\,\mathrm{Exp}_{{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}},0}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\,.

Recall that in Corollary
5.14
(see also the proof of Proposition
5.13
),
we already proved that

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)\simeq\ker(g_{\Gamma})\simeq{}^{c}\widetilde{D}.

This can be deduced also using the arguments below, which utilize to compute

\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha).

Recall that
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})_{\Gamma}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
. In what follows, we shall use the indicated isomorphism to identify these spaces. It follows from Lemma
4.12

that
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})_{\Gamma}=H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})
sits in the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}(X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})_{\Gamma}\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}V)\longrightarrow 0\,,

which can be extended to the following commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{res}_{p}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{res}_{p}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}V)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{res}_{p}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\{p\}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V)}

where the vertical arrows are injections.
Since the restriction map

H^{1}_{\{p\}}(X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})\xrightarrow{\textup{res}_{p}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})

is an isomorphism, and

\mathrm{im}\left(\mathrm{Exp}_{{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}},0}\right)=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}),

we have

(5.15)

\mathrm{im}\left(\mathrm{Exp}_{{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}},0}\right)=H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})=\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\{p\}}(X{}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})).

As a result, we have
{\rm im}(g_{\Gamma})=\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V})_{\Gamma})=\textup{res}_{p}(H^{1}_{\{p\}}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V}))\,,

and the second isomorphism in (i) follows.

The proof that
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
is a free
\widetilde{E}
-module of rank one follows from the first isomorphism in (i), whereas the fact that
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}\widetilde{V},\alpha)
is a free
\widetilde{E}
-module of rank one from the second isomorphism in (i) combined with Lemma
4.12
.

The formula for
\vartheta
in (ii) follows directly from the definition of the Bockstein map and the proofs of the remaining claims in this portion are clear.
∎

Remark 5.16
.

In this remark, we return back to the setting of Chapter
4
and assume that
\widetilde{V}=V_{k}
is the infinitesimal thickening of the
p
-adic Galois representation attached to a critical modular form of weight
k
along the eigencurve.

The discussion in §
5.2
applies verbatim when the pair
({}^{c}\widetilde{V},{}^{c}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}})
is replaced with
(\widetilde{V}(\chi^{j}),\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}(\chi^{j}))
for integers
1\leq j\leq k-1
, without demanding that
k
is even. It is required that
k
is even only when one is interested (as we are in the remainder of this chapter) in the interpretation of the central critical values in terms of
p
-adic height pairings.

Proposition
5.15
(ii) tells us that the thick Selmer group
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})
behaves in a manner reminiscent of the behaviour of the extended Selmer groups in the presence of exceptional zeros. The exceptional zero that
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})
accounts for is that of thick
p
-adic
L
-function
\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(f,\xi)
, cf. (
5.1
).

The kernel of
\vartheta
contains
X{}^{c}D_{k}
. We infer from the commutative diagram in Proposition
5.15
(ii) that the complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}_{\mathrm{Iw}}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},\alpha)
is not semi-simple. It seems that the modification of the Bockstein map used in the earlier sections of Chapter
4
can not be extended to this case.

It is not clear whether or not the map
\vartheta
is nonzero. The requirement that
\vartheta\neq 0
appears to be a refinement of (
GP)
).

Based on the absence of a duality theory over
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
combined with the previous remarks, it is not clear to us whether or not the derivative of the secondary
p
-adic
L
-function
L^{[1],\pm}_{K,\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
at the central critical point can be computed in terms of
p
-adic heights. See, however, Theorem
5.29
(ii) below where we compute this quantity in terms of the second-order derivative of an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic regulator (at
X=0
). Nonetheless, the arithmetic meaning of
L^{[1],\pm}_{K,\alpha^{*}}(f^{*},\xi^{*})
at the central critical point, even granted the infinitesimal thickening

⁢

~
MC

(
f
)

of the
\theta
-critical Main Conjecture, remains mysterious.

5.3.
p
-adic regulators and leading term formulae

Our goal in this section is to compute the leading terms of the critical
p
-adic
L
-functions given as in §
2
, in terms of the regulators associated to the
p
-adic heights we have introduced in §
1.3
. As in the earlier sections in this chapter, we shall solely concentrate on the central critical values.

5.3.1.
The Selmer complex
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

Central critical twists

As before, we let
V_{\mathcal{X}}
and
V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}
denote the free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules of rank
2
as in §
2.4.2
, which are endowed with continuous actions of
G_{\mathbb{Q},S}
. Recall that the Galois representations
V_{\mathcal{X}}
and
V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}
interpolate Deligne’s representations over the affinoid neighborhood
\mathcal{X}
contained in the eigencurve, about the point
x_{0}
that corresponds to the
\theta
-critical eigenform
f_{\alpha}\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{1}(N)\cap\Gamma_{0}(p),\varepsilon_{f})
.

Definition 5.17
.

The universal
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{W}}
-valued cyclotomic character
\bbchi:\Gamma\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}^{*}
is given by

\bbchi(\gamma):=\chi(\gamma)^{k}\exp\left(\log_{p}(1+Y)\frac{\log_{p}(\left<\chi(\gamma)\right>)}{\log_{p}(1+p)}\right)\,,\qquad\gamma\in\Gamma\,.

We continue to assume that
k
is even and we consider the
central critical twist

{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}:=V_{\mathcal{X}}(\bbchi^{\frac{1}{2}})
of
V_{\mathcal{X}}
. Put
{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\bbchi^{\frac{1}{2}})
.
The pairing
(\,,\,)
given as in (
2.4.2
)
gives rise to an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-linear pairing

(5.16)

{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\otimes{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}(\chi).

For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\},

the
\varphi
-module
{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x}^{\prime})
decomposes into the direct sum

{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x}^{\prime})={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x}^{\prime})^{\varphi=\alpha^{\prime}(x)}\oplus{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x}^{\prime})^{\varphi=\beta^{\prime}(x)},\qquad\textrm{where
$\alpha^{\prime}(x)=\alpha(x)\varepsilon_{f}^{-1}(p),$
and
$\beta^{\prime}(x)=\beta(x)\varepsilon_{f}^{-1}(p),$
}

and we denote by
\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
the unique free
\mathscr{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
-submodule of rank one
of
\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},\mathcal{X}}(V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
such that
\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{x}={\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x}^{\prime})^{\varphi=\alpha^{\prime}(x)}

for all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}.
Set
{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}:={}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\bbchi^{\frac{1}{2}}).
The modules
{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}
and

{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
are orthogonal to each other under the canonical pairing induced from (
5.16
).

In this section, we study the Selmer complexes
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

and
\mathbf{R\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})
(see §
1.2.2
for general definitions).

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}
be a prime. Then the following statements hold true:

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{x})=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{x})=\{0\}
at any classical point
x\in\mathcal{X}(E),

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})=\{0\}
whenever
\mathcal{X}
is sufficiently small.

For sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
, the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})
are free.

Proof.

It follows from the local-global compatibility of Langlands correspondence that

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{x})=\{0\}=H^{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{x})

for all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
and
\ell\in S\setminus\{p\}
. By the local Euler characteristic formula, we then conclude that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{x})=\{0\}
for all

x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
. We deduce from the self-duality of
{}^{\rm c}V_{x}
that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})=\{0\}
.

It follows from Part i) that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})/\mathfrak{m}_{x}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})=\{0\}
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
, thence also that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is a torsion
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module with no support at any classical point in
\mathcal{X}(E)
(including
x=x_{0}
). On shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary, we can ensure that it has no support anywhere on
\mathcal{X}
.

Since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a PID, it suffices to prove that both modules are torsion-free.

Since the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
is finitely generated, we can ensure (on shrinking
\mathcal{X}
) that the torsion submodule of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
is contained in

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X^{\infty}]:=\bigcup_{n}\ker\left(\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{[X^{n}]}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\right)\,.

It therefore suffices to check that

{\rm im}\left(\mathbf{R}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\right)=\ker\left(\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{[X]}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\right)\,.

This is clear, since
\mathbf{R}^{0}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})=\{0\}
.

The proof of the freeness of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})
is identical and is omitted here.
∎

Let us set
\,{}^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},\mathcal{X}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})/{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},
so that we have the following tautological exact sequence:

0\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},\mathcal{X}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow 0\,.

We also set
\,{}^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig},\mathcal{X}}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})/{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Lemma 5.19
.

The following assertions are valid for sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
:

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})=\{0\}.

Assume that the condition (
GP)
) holds. Then the support of the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})_{\rm tor}
is
\{x_{0}\}
, and

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})_{\rm tor}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/X\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

The assertions i-ii) hold if we replace
\,{}^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
with
\,{}^{c}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Proof.

Let us consider the short exact sequence

(5.17)

0\longrightarrow\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{X-X(x)}\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{sp}_{x}}\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{x}\longrightarrow 0\,.

The exactness of the sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{X-X(x)}H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{sp}_{x}}H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{x})

for each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
and the vanishing
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{x})=0
whenever

x\neq x_{0}
shows (on shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary) that
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
can only have support at
x_{0}
; in particular it is a torsion
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module.

On the other hand, the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{\mathcal{X}})

together with the fact that
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})=0
show that
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
injects into
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{\mathcal{X}})_{\rm tor}
. Since we have

0=H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{\mathcal{X}})[\mathfrak{m}_{x}]

for each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
(including
x=x_{0}
), we can ensure that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{\mathcal{X}})_{\rm tor}=0
(shrinking
\mathcal{X}
, if necessary). This concludes the proof that
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})=0
.

We note that the vanishing of
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
does not imply the vanishing of
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{x_{0}}).
More precisely, using the long exact sequence in
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-cohomology that the short exact sequence (
5.17
) gives rise to, we obtain an isomorphism

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{x})\simeq H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[\mathfrak{m}_{x}]\,

for any
x
. The
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})_{\rm tor}
is finitely generated. Since
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{x})=0
if
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
and
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{x_{0}})\simeq H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})
is of dimension one over
E
, we can assume, on shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary, that the support of
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})_{\rm tor}
is
\{x_{0}\}
.

Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/X\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}},

we only need to prove that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X^{2}]=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X]
.
By the exactness of the sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{k})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{[X^{2}]}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

we reduce to proving that
\dim_{E}H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{k})=1.

To that end, let us consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}\longrightarrow\mathbf{D}^{\dagger}_{\mathrm{rig}}({}^{\rm c}V_{k})\longrightarrow{\,{}^{c}\mathbb{D}}_{k}\longrightarrow 0\,,

which induces an exact sequence

H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{\,{}^{c}\mathbb{D}}_{k})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{k})\,.

Let
V_{k}^{(\alpha)}
be the maximal crystalline sub-representation of
V_{k}

defined in §
2.1.1
and denoted there by
\widetilde{V}^{(\alpha)}.
Then the quotient

{}^{\rm c}V_{k}\Big/{}^{c}V_{k}^{(\alpha)}\simeq{}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{(\beta)}

has Hodge-Tate weight
-\frac{k}{2}
, and therefore the map

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{c}V_{k}^{(\alpha)})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{k})

is injective. We have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
