\textstyle{\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{D}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{c}V_{k}^{(\alpha)})\big/\operatorname{Fil}^{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{c}V_{k}^{(\alpha)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{\,{}^{c}\mathbb{D}}_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{c}V_{k}^{(\alpha)}),}

where the first row is exact and the image of the right-most map on this row is nonzero thanks to the condition (
GP)
). This shows that
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{k})
is of dimension one over
E
, as required.
∎

To simplify the notation, we denote by
H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})
the Greenberg-style Selmer group attached to the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module

{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}
, namely

H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}):=\ker\left(H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{\bigoplus_{\ell\in S}{r_{\ell}}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\oplus\bigoplus_{p\neq\ell\in S}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}^{\textup{ur}},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})\right)\,.

(cf. §
1.2.3
). In view of Lemma
5.18
, we have

H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}):=\ker\left(H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{\,r_{p}\,}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\right)\,.

Proposition 5.20
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\simeq H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}).

Proof.

We have the following exact sequence thanks the definition of Selmer complexes:

(5.18)

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow 0.

The proof of the proposition follows since
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}})=0
by Lemma
5.19
.
∎

5.3.2.
The rank-one case

In this section, we maintain the previous notations and conventions and assume in addition that

(5.19)

r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2):=\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f,s)=1\,.

as well as the hypothesis (
PR)
) hold true for the eigenform
f
.

In what follows, thanks to our discussion in Remark
3.18
, one may drop the assumption (
PR)
) from the statements below whenever (
CM)
) holds true (and it does hold true when
k=2
).

We denote by

{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
the image of the big Beilinson–Kato element
\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in H^{1}_{S}(V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\Lambda^{\iota}(1))
under the obvious projection, where the latter element is given as in §
2.4.3
.
Note that (
5.19
) implies (thanks to Kato’s reciprocity laws) that

(5.20)

{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x_{0},\xi)\in H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime})\,.

Recall that
the condition (
PR)
) implies (thanks to the Euler system machinery) that

(5.21)

\dim H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime})=0\,,\qquad\dim H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime})=1\,

(cf. Proposition
3.19
).
This in turn shows that the parity conjecture asserting that

(5.22)

\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f,s)\equiv\dim H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime})\mod 2

holds true.

Proposition 5.21
.

Suppose that (
5.19
) and (
PR)
) hold. Then on sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
we have

\dim_{E}H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})=1

for all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
. Moreover,

{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x,\xi)\in H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})

and the truth of the parity conjecture is constant on
\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
.

Proof.

The condition (
PR)
) shows that
\textup{res}_{p}({}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi))\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is nonzero. Since
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module of rank
2
, it follows (on shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary) that

\textup{res}_{p}({}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x,\xi))=\textup{res}_{p}({}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi))(x)\neq 0

for all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
.

The constancy of the sign of the functional equation in families (cf.
[
PX15
]
, §3.6) together with (
5.19
) shows that
\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{w(x)}{2}}L(f_{x},s)
is odd for all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
. In particular,
L(f_{x},\frac{w(x)}{2})=0
. This together with Kato’s reciprocity laws (and the conclusion of the preceding paragraph) show that

0\neq{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x,\xi)\in H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime}).

for all
x
as above. Now a standard application of the Euler system machinery (with the Beilinson–Kato Euler system for the eigenform
f_{x}
) shows that
\dim H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})=1
, which has the same parity as
\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{w(x)}{2}}L(f_{x},s)
.
∎

Recall that we have an isomorphism

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\simeq H^{1}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}).

Theorem 5.22
.

Suppose that
r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2)=1
and assume that the conditions (
PR)
) and
(
GP)
) hold. Then for sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
, the following assertions are valid:

Both
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
are free of rank one as
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules.

The
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
, and therefore also the isomorphic module
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
,
is generated by the element
X\cdot{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi).

Proof.

Equation (
5.21
) together with (
5.7
) show that

\dim\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\prime})=1.

The control theorem for Selmer complexes yields an injection

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})/\mathfrak{m}_{x_{0}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\prime}),

which in turn shows that

{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\leqslant 1.

Proposition
5.21
tells us that, on shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary,
H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{x},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}^{\prime})
is 1-dimensional for every
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
.
Consider the exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})/\mathfrak{m}_{x}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{x},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})[\mathfrak{m}_{x}]\longrightarrow 0,

induced from the control theorem. Since
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is a finitely generated
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module,
on further shrinking
\mathcal{X}
, one can ensure that this exact sequence induces an
isomorphism

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})/\mathfrak{m}_{x}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\simeq H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime}),\qquad\forall x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E).

This shows that
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\neq\{0\}
. Recalling from Lemma
5.18
that
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is free, and combining with the conclusion of the previous paragraph, we deduce that

{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})=1\,,

as required.

The argument to prove that
{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})=1
proceeds in a similar way.

Let us put
P^{\prime}:=X\cdot{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi).
We shall first prove that
P^{\prime}\in H^{1}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}).
Recall that

H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})=\ker\left(H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\,r_{p}\,}H^{1}(\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\right).

We need to check that
P^{\prime}
verifies the relevant local condition under our running hypotheses. For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E),
we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})/\mathfrak{m}_{x}H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{p,x}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})/\mathfrak{m}_{x}H^{1}(\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\,^{c}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{x}),}

where the vertical maps are injective. If
x\neq x_{0},
then

\ker\left(H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\,^{c}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{x})\right))\simeq H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V^{\prime}_{x}),

and it follows from Proposition
5.21
that

r_{p,x}(P^{\prime})=0.
Moreover, we also have
r_{p,x_{0}}(P^{\prime})=0.
Since the support of

H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})_{\rm tor}=\{x_{0}\},
this implies that

P^{\prime}\in\ker(r_{p})=H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}).

Let
Q^{\prime}
be a generator of
H^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}).
Then
P^{\prime}=a(X)Q^{\prime}
for some
a(X)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}.
To prove that
P^{\prime}
generates
H^{1}_{\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
for sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
, we only need to check that
a(0)\neq 0.

Suppose on the contrary that
a
is divisible by
X
and put
a(X)=Xb(X)
.
Then by definitions, we have

{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)-bP^{\prime}\in{H}^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})[X]=\{0\}\,

where the vanishing is because the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})={H}^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is torsion-free (cf. Lemma
5.18
). This in turn shows that
{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in{H}^{1}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
and hence

\textup{res}_{p}({}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x_{0},\xi))\in\ker\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\,^{c}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{x_{0}})\right)={\rm im}\left(H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{x_{0}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm Lemma~\ref{lemma: coboundary map}}}{{=}}\{0\},

contradicting the hypothesis (
PR)
).

∎

5.3.3.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic height pairing

Let
Q\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
be any element. For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
(resp.
w\in\mathcal{W}^{\rm cl}(E)
), we let
Q_{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})
(resp.
Q_{w}\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{w},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{w})
) denote the image of
Q
under the appropriate morphism. We similarly define, for a given
Q^{\prime}\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
,
Q^{\prime}_{?}
for
?=y,w
.

Following our notation in Section
2.2.1
, let us set

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ}:=\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})^{\circ}:=\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}.

The
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-bilinear pairing
{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
induces the cyclotomic
p
-adic height pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}\,:\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\,\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}.

We extend this pairing by linearity to a
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-bilinear map

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathbf{D}}\,:\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})^{\circ}\,\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\circ}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\,,

\left\langle\sum_{i}a_{i}^{\prime}\otimes z_{i}^{\prime},\sum_{j}a_{j}\otimes z_{j}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{D}}:=\sum_{i,j}a_{i}^{\prime}a_{j}\cdot\langle z_{i}^{\prime},z_{j}\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}.

Definition 5.23
.

We define the pairing

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{\mathcal{X}}\,:\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\,\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}

on setting

\left\langle Q^{\prime},Q\right\rangle_{\mathcal{X}}:=\frac{1}{2}\langle Q^{\prime},X\otimes Q+1\otimes XQ\rangle_{\mathbf{D}}

and call it the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic height pairing.

We remark that

\left\langle Q^{\prime},Q\right\rangle_{\mathcal{X}}:=\frac{1}{2}\langle X\otimes Q^{\prime}+1\otimes XQ^{\prime},Q\rangle_{\mathbf{D}}.

thank to the property (
Adj)
).
Moreover, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}

we have

X\otimes Q^{\prime}+1\otimes XQ^{\prime}=1\otimes 2X(x)Q^{\prime}+(X-X(x))\otimes Q^{\prime}+1\otimes(X-X(x))Q^{\prime}.

Thence, recalling from (
2.18
) the specialization morphism
{\rm sp}_{x}
, we have

\mathrm{sp}_{x}(X\otimes Q^{\prime}+1\otimes XQ^{\prime})=2X(x)Q^{\prime}_{x}.

This formula shows that the specialization of our
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic height pairing to a classical point
x\neq x_{0}
is given by

(5.23)

\left\langle Q^{\prime},Q\right\rangle_{\mathcal{X}}(x)=X(x)\cdot\left\langle Q_{x}^{\prime},Q_{x}\right\rangle_{x},\qquad\forall x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\},

where
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{x}
denotes Nekovář’s
p
-adic height on
V_{x}.

Lemma 5.24
.

Suppose that
r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2)=1
and condition (
PR)
) holds true. Then the specialization of the pairing
\left\langle\,,\,\right\rangle_{\mathcal{X}}
at
x=x_{0}
vanishes.

Proof.

At
x=x_{0}
we have

\mathrm{sp}_{x_{0}}(X\otimes Q+1\otimes XQ)=XQ_{k}\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V[x_{0}],{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}[x_{0}])=\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})[X].

We can identify
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})[X]

with
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})
and

XQ_{k}
with
Q_{x_{0}}.
Then
\left\langle Q_{x_{0}}^{\prime},Q_{x_{0}}\right\rangle_{x_{0}}
can be computed as
\langle\,Q^{\prime}_{x_{0}},Q_{x_{0}}\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}},
where

\langle\,,\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}}\,:\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})\rightarrow E

is the
p
-adic height pairing given as in Section
5.2.2
. Since
H^{1}_{0}(V_{x_{0}})=0
in the particular scenario we have placed ourselves in, the asserted vanishing follows from Corollary
5.8
.
∎

Let
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E)
be any
E
-valued classical point. In §
2.4
, we have fixed a canonical element
\omega_{x}\in\operatorname{Fil}^{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x})
and defined a canonical basis
\{\eta_{x}^{\alpha},\eta_{x}^{\beta}\}
of eigenvectors
where
\eta_{x}^{\alpha}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x})^{\varphi=\alpha(x)}
and

\eta_{x}^{\beta}\in{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(V_{x})^{\varphi=\beta(x)}.
Recall that for

x=x_{0}
, we have
\eta_{x_{0}}^{\alpha}:=\eta_{f}^{\alpha}=\omega_{f}.

Let us choose
\eta\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
such that
\eta_{x_{0}}=\eta_{x_{0}}^{\alpha}.

We note that, in general,
\eta_{x}\neq\eta_{x}^{\alpha}
if
x\neq x_{0}
. We recall from §
2.2.3
that we have put

\bbeta:=1\otimes X\eta+X\otimes\eta\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\,,

where
\eta\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a generator that we fix throughout. We also define
{}^{c}\eta_{?}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{?})
(where
?=y,w(y)
) as
\eta_{?}\otimes d_{\frac{w(x)}{2}}
where
\eta_{?}
is the image of
\eta
under the appropriate morphism, and
d_{j}
is the canonical generator of

{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}(j))
for any integer
j
(cf. §
1.1.5
). We remark that

{}^{c}{\rm sp}_{x}(\bbeta)=(X-X(x))\,{}^{c}\eta_{w(x)}+{2}X(x)\,{}^{c}\eta_{w(x)}\,,

so that the image of
{}^{c}{\rm sp}_{x}(\bbeta)
under the morphism

\pi_{x}:{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{w(x)})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})

equals
{2}X(x)\,{}^{c}\eta_{x}
.

5.3.4.
Cyclotomic twist of the large exponential map

Our main objective in this section is to study the central twist of the large exponential map. Recall that
\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})=\mathcal{O}_{E}[[\pi]]^{\psi=0}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}).
The map
d\mapsto d\otimes(1+\pi)
identifies
{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
with a
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-submodule of rank one of
\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
(note, however, that this map is not
\Gamma
-equivariant). The Iwasawa cohomology module
H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
is canonically isomorphic to
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)^{\iota}),
and we have
the specialization map

H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\simeq H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{X}}(\Gamma)^{\iota})\xrightarrow{\gamma\mapsto\bbchi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma)}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}).

Consider the map

\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}\,:\,{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

given as the composition

(5.24)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Exp}_{\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}},0}}H^{1}_{\mathrm{Iw}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{\gamma\mapsto\bbchi^{\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma)}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\,.\end{aligned}

Lemma 5.25
.

For sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
, the map
\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Note that the source of
\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module of rank one.
The exact sequence

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{X-X(x)}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

together with the vanishing of
H^{0}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})
in a sufficiently small neighbourhood
of
x_{0}
show that
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
is free of rank one over

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}.

By the definition of
\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}
in terms of the large exponential map, for each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{cl}}(E),
the specialization

(5.25)

\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D},x}\,:\,{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{x})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})

coincides with the map

(5.26)

(-1)^{w(x)/2}\Gamma\left(\frac{w(x)}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}-1}}{a_{p}(f_{x})}\right)\left(1-\frac{a_{p}(f_{x})}{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}}}\right)^{-1}\,\exp_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}}\,.

In particular, it is an isomorphism at
x=x_{0}.
This shows that
\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}
is necessarily bijective on a sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}.

∎

Each element of
z
of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{?},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{?})
(where ?=
\mathcal{X},x
) can be written uniquely as a pair
z=(a,b)
, where
a\in H^{1}({}^{\rm c}V_{?})
and
b\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{?})
. We define the map

\textup{res}_{p}:\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{?},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{?})\longrightarrow H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{?})\,,\qquad z=(a,b)\mapsto b\,.

When
?=x\neq x_{0}
, we shall denote the image of
\textup{res}_{p}(z)
(where
z\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})
) under the canonical isomorphism
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}_{x})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{x})
also by
\textup{res}_{p}(z)
.

Definition 5.26
.

Recall the generator
\eta\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
. For each
z\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
, we let
{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(z)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
denote the unique element that validates the identity

(5.27)

\textup{res}_{p}(z)={\rm LOG}_{\eta}(z)\cdot\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}(\eta)

inside the free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}})
of rank one.

We note that the map
{\rm LOG}_{\eta}:\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is well-defined thank to Lemma
5.25
.

For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}V_{x})/{\rm Fil}^{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}V_{x})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exp}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{x})\,.}

Recall that we have put

{}^{c}\eta_{x}:=\eta_{x}[{w(x)}/{2}]\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{x}(\chi^{\frac{w(x)}{2}})).

For each
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
,
we define
{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Fil}^{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})
as the unique vector such that

(5.28)

[{}^{c}\eta_{x},{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}]=1\,.

If
x=x_{0},
we set

{}^{c}\omega_{x_{0}}^{\prime}:={}^{c}\eta_{f^{*}}^{\alpha},

where
\eta_{f^{*}}^{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Fil}^{0}{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}({}{}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{\prime})
is the vector defined in Section
3.1.1
.

For all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
, we put

\displaystyle\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}\,:\,H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{x})\longrightarrow E,

\displaystyle\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}(z):=(\log_{{}^{\rm c}V_{x}}(z),{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}).

Proposition 5.27
.

For sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
the following holds true:

For any
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
we have

(-1)^{w(x)/2}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{w(x)}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}-1}}{a_{p}(f_{x})}\right)\left(1-\frac{a_{p}(f_{x})}{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}}}\right)^{-1}{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)(x)=\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}(\textup{res}_{p}(P_{x})).

Assume that
r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2)=1
and the conditions (
PR)
) and (
GP)
) hold. Then

{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\times}
for any generator
P

of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}).

Moreover,

(5.29)

(-1)^{k/2}\,C_{\mathrm{K}}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{a_{p}(f_{x_{0}})}\right)\left(1-\frac{a_{p}(f_{x_{0}})}{p^{\frac{k}{2}}}\right)^{-1}{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)(x_{0})=\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}(\textup{res}_{p}(\Delta(P_{x_{0}}))),

where
C_{\mathrm{K}}
is the constant defined in (
2.44
)
and
\Delta\,:\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})\rightarrow H^{1}_{\mathrm{f}}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}})
is the morphism from Corollary
5.14
.

Proof.

On specializing (
5.27
) to
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
and using (
5.26
), we have

\textup{res}_{p}(P_{x})=(-1)^{w(x)/2}{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)(x)\,\Gamma\left(\frac{w(x)}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}-1}}{a_{p}(f_{x})}\right)\left(1-\frac{a_{p}(f_{x})}{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}}}\right)^{-1}\exp_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}}({}^{c}\eta_{x}).

The asserted equality in Part (i) follows from the definition of
\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}
.

We recall from Theorem
5.22
and
(
5.7
) that, under our assumptions, the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-module
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
is of rank one and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})
is a one-dimensional vector space over
E
. Therefore, the natural morphism

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})/\mathfrak{m}_{x_{0}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\hookrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})

induced from the base change property of Selmer complexes is an isomorphism.
The image
P_{x_{0}}
of
P
is a generator of

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\eqref{eqn_prop_degenerate_local_conditions}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}).

This implies that
\textup{res}_{p}(P)_{x_{0}}=\textup{res}_{p}(P_{x_{0}})\neq 0
, which concludes the proof that
{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)(x_{0})\neq 0
(hence
{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\times}
on shrinking
\mathcal{X}
as necessary).

Reducing (
5.27
) modulo
X^{2}
and using Corollary
2.8
, we infer that

(5.30)

X\textup{res}_{p}(\Delta(P_{x_{0}}))\\
\equiv X(-1)^{k/2}{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)(x_{0})\,\Gamma\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{a_{p}(f_{x_{0}})}\right)\left(1-\frac{a_{p}(f_{x_{0}})}{p^{\frac{k}{2}}}\right)^{-1}\exp_{{}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}}^{(\beta)}}({}^{c}\kappa_{0}(\eta_{x_{0}}))\pmod{X^{2}}.

Recalling that

\left[{}^{c}\kappa_{0}(\eta_{x_{0}}),{}^{c}\omega_{x_{0}}^{\prime}\right]=C_{\mathrm{K}},

we conclude with the proof of (
5.29
) and our proposition.

∎

5.3.5.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic leading term formula

In this subsection, we work in the setting of §
5.3.2
. In particular, the conditions

r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2)=1
, (
PR)
)
and (
GP)
) are enforced. In this case, the
E
-vector space
H^{1}_{\rm f}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}})
is one-dimensional, whereas
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})
is two-dimensional. We recall from Remark
3.18
that one may drop the assumption (
PR)
) whenever (
CM)
) holds true (in particular, when
k=2
).

Let
P^{\prime}=X\cdot{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
be the generator of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
constructed in Theorem
5.22
. We also fix a generator

P
of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
and set

(5.31)

R_{\mathcal{X}}:=\langle\,P^{\prime},P\,\rangle_{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\,.

Recall the two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi)

constructed in Section
2.4.4
.

We have the following leading term formula for our
p
-adic
L
-function at classical points
x\neq x_{0}
:

Proposition 5.28
.

Assume that
r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2)=1
, and that the conditions (
PR)
) and (
GP)
) hold.
Suppose that
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
is not in the support of
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})_{\rm tor}
.
Then,

(5.32)

X(x)\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}(P_{x})\,\frac{d}{ds}L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi;x,\chi^{s})\big|_{s=\frac{w(x)}{2}}\\
={(-1)^{\frac{w(x)}{2}-1}\cdot 2}\cdot\Gamma\left(\frac{w(x)}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}-1}}{a_{p}(f_{x})}\right)\left(1-a_{p}(f_{x})p^{-\frac{w(x)}{2}}\right)^{-1}R_{\mathcal{X}}(y)\,,

where
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi;x):=L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)(x)
is the specialization of the two-variable
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}
to
x
.

Proof.

It follows from the definitions and the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-linearity of
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}
that

R_{\mathcal{X}}(x)=\langle P^{\prime}_{x},X(x)P_{x}\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}^{\prime}}\,.

Plugging in
X(x){}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x,\xi)
in place of
P^{\prime}_{x}
and using the Rubin-style formula (Theorem
1.15
), we have

(5.33)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} R_{\mathcal{X}}(x)=X(x)\left\langle\mathbf{z}_{0}(x,\xi),X(x)P_{x}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}^{\prime}}&=-X(x)\,\left(\mathfrak{d}_{\textup{cyc}}{}^{c}\mathbf{z}(y,\xi),X(y)\textup{res}_{p}(P_{x})\right)\\
&=-X(x)\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}(P_{x})\,\left(\mathfrak{d}_{\textup{cyc}}{}^{c}\mathbf{z}(x,\xi),X(x)\exp({}^{c}\eta_{x})\right)\,.\end{aligned}

Here, the cyclotomic derivative
\mathfrak{d}_{\textup{cyc}}
of a class is given in the statement of Theorem
1.15
and in the second equality, we have used the definition of
\log_{{}^{c}\omega_{x}^{\prime}}(P_{x})
.

The definition of the
p
-adic
L
-function
L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
in terms of the large exponential map (cf. §
2.2.3
) alongside the interpolation properties of the large exponential map (cf. §
1.1.5
), and the fact that
{\rm sp}_{x}(\bbeta)\otimes d_{\frac{w(x)}{2}}
maps to
{2}X(x){}^{c}\eta_{x}
under the natural isomorphism
{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{w(x)}[x]\xrightarrow[\pi_{x}]{\sim}{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}
combined together yields

(5.34)

\frac{d}{ds}L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}(\mathcal{X},\xi;x,\chi^{s})\big|_{s=\frac{w(x)}{2}}\\
={(-1)^{\frac{w(x)}{2}}\cdot 2}\cdot\Gamma\left(\frac{w(x)}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{w(x)}{2}-1}}{a_{p}(f_{x})}\right)\left(1-a_{p}(f_{x})p^{-\frac{w(x)}{2}}\right)^{-1}\left(\mathfrak{d}_{\textup{cyc}}{}^{c}\mathbf{z}(x,\xi),X(x)\exp({}^{c}\eta_{x})\right)\,.

This identity combined with (
5.33
) concludes the proof.
∎

Theorem 5.29
.

Assume that
r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2)=1
and the conditions (
PR)
) and (
GP)
) hold.

(
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic leading term formula) For sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
, we have the following identity in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
:

X\cdot{\rm LOG}_{\eta}(P)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial s}L_{\mathrm{K},\eta}^{\pm}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\ \Big|_{{s=\frac{w(x)}{2}}}=-R_{\mathcal{X}}\,.

The identity

(5.35)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \log_{\omega_{{x_{0}}}^{\prime}}&\,(P_{x_{0}})\,\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\widetilde{L}_{\mathrm{K},\alpha}^{\pm}(f,\xi)\Big|_{s=\frac{k}{2}}\\
&={(-1)^{\frac{k}{2}-1}\,C_{\mathrm{K}}}\,{\Gamma\left(\frac{k}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{p^{\frac{k}{2}-1}}{\alpha}\right)\left(1-\alpha p^{-\frac{k}{2}}\right)^{-1}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}+\frac{X}{2}\frac{d^{2}R_{\mathcal{X}}}{dX^{2}}\big|_{X=0}\right)\end{aligned}

holds in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/X^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
, where

\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime})\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})\xrightarrow{\langle\,,\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}}E

is the specialization of the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}
-valued height pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}
to weight
k
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Proposition
5.28
and Proposition
5.27
(i), which together tell us that the difference of the two sides agree at every
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)\setminus\{x_{0}\}
.

The asserted identity follows from Part (i)
once we carry out the following tasks:

a)

Prove that the expression
\dfrac{X}{2}\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}
is the linearization of the regulator
R_{\mathcal{X}}
about
X=0
:

R_{\mathcal{X}}(k)=\frac{X}{2}\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}\mod X^{2}\,.

b)

Compare the coefficient of
X^{2}
in the Taylor expansions of both sides in (
5.35
) (which manifestly agree in view of Theorem
5.29
).

Unravelling the definitions, we see that

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{X}}(k)

\displaystyle=\frac{X}{2}\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle[X]P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{X}{2}\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{X}P_{x_{0}}^{\prime},P_{x_{0}}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}}=\frac{X}{2}\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}

where the second identity follows from the discussion in the paragraph following Theorem
5.29
(where we explain that
[X]P_{k}^{\prime}=XP_{x_{0}}^{\prime}
) and the property (
Adj)
), whereas the final equality (i.e. the vanishing of the pairing
\left\langle{X}P_{x_{0}}^{\prime},P_{x_{0}}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}}
) has been observed as part of the proof of Proposition
5.24
. This concludes our proof.
∎

The naïve regulator

Let
P,P^{\prime}
be as in §
5.3.5
and recall that
P_{k}\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})
and
P_{k}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime})
denote their respective images. Recall that
P_{k}
(resp.
P_{k}^{\prime}
) generates the Selmer group
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})
(resp.
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime})
) as
\widetilde{E}
-modules. Therefore,
\{P_{k},[X]P_{k}\}
and
\{P_{k}^{\prime},[X]P_{k}^{\prime}\}
are
E
-bases of the Selmer groups
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime})
, respectively.

Attached to this data, we may define the
naïve
regulator in terms of the
p
-adic height pairing
\left\langle\,,\,\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}
evaluated on these
E
-bases:

R_{k}^{\hbox{\tiny na{\"{i}}ve}}:=\det\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle[X]P_{k}^{\prime},[X]P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}&\left\langle[X]P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}\\
\\
\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},[X]P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}&\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}\end{pmatrix}\,.

One might expect this regulator to show up in our leading term formulae. We will explain below why this cannot be the case, by showing that
R_{k}^{\hbox{\tiny na{\"{i}}ve}}
vanishes.

Note that we have,

\left\langle[X]P_{k}^{\prime},[X]P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}=\left\langle[X^{2}]P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}=0

thanks to (
Adj)
). Moreover, as we have explained in the proof of Theorem
5.29
(ii), we have

\left\langle P_{k}^{\prime},[X]P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}=\left\langle P_{x_{0}}^{\prime},XP_{x_{0}}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}}}\,\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Cor.}\ref{cor_prop_factor_degenerate_height_pairing}}}{{=}}\,\,0

and similarly,
\left\langle[X]P_{k}^{\prime},P_{k}\right\rangle_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k}}=0
as well. This shows that the naïve regulator
R_{k}^{\hbox{\tiny na{\"{i}}ve}}
on the thick Selmer groups vanishes.

We will conclude §
5.3.5
with an observation concerning the torsion submodule of
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
.

The base change properties of Selmer complexes give rise to the exact sequences

0\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})/X\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X]\longrightarrow 0

0\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})/X^{2}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X^{2}]\longrightarrow 0\,.

This in turn yields

(5.36)

\displaystyle\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X]=\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x_{0}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x_{0}})-{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})

\displaystyle\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X^{2}]=\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{k},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{k})-2\cdot{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\,.

Our considerations above leads to the following curious disparity concerning the behaviour of the torsion submodule of
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
:

Proposition 5.30
.

Suppose that the conditions (
PR)
) and (
GP)
) hold. Then for sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
we have

(5.37)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X]\,&=\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})[X^{2}]\\
\,&=\begin{cases}1&\hbox{ if }\quad L(f,\frac{k}{2})\neq 0,\\
0&\hbox{ if }\quad\textup{res}_{p}\left({}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x_{0},\xi)\right)\in H^{1}_{\rm f}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}V_{f}^{\prime}).\\
\end{cases}\end{aligned}

Proof.

The theory of Euler systems shows that

(5.38)

\eqref{item_PR1}\,\implies\,H^{1}_{0}({}^{\rm c}V_{f})=0\,,

cf. Proposition
3.19
. The proof of our proposition follows on combining Proposition
5.15
(ii), Theorem
5.22
(i), (
5.36
), and (
5.38
).
∎

Based on these observations, we propose the following:

Conjecture 5.31
.

For sufficiently small
\mathcal{X}
,

(5.39)

\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})_{\rm tor}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}/X\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\simeq E&\qquad\hbox{ if }\quad L(f,\frac{k}{2})\neq 0,\\
\{0\}&\qquad\hbox{ if }\quad\mathrm{ord}_{s=\frac{k}{2}}L(f,s)=1\,.\\
\end{cases}

We remark that Conjecture
5.31
indeed holds whenever the implication (extension of Perrin-Riou’s conjecture to higher weight forms)

r_{\rm an}(k/2)=1\quad{}\implies{}\quad\,\eqref{item_PR1}

holds true.

5.3.6.
Central critical twists and a conjecture of Greenberg

In the previous section, we have defined the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic regulator
R_{\mathcal{X}}
under the assumption that
r_{\mathrm{an}}(k/2)=1
, as well as the hypotheses (
PR)
) and (
GP)
), which were used (cf. Theorem
5.22
) to ensure that both
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-modules
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
are free of rank one. An extension of a conjecture of Greenberg (cf. Conjecture
5.33
) below predicts that this is still the case under the weaker assumption that the nebentype of the family over
\mathcal{X}
is trivial and the global root number of some (every) classical member of the family equals
-1
.

Our goal in §
5.3.6
and §
5.3.7
is to establish criteria for the validity of Conjecture
5.33
, so that we can define the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-adic regulator
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{X}}
under hypotheses that are less stringent then those in the previous section.

Let
W(f)
denote the global root number of
f
at the central critical point. A conjecture of Greenberg (cf.
[
Gre94
]
, see also
[
Arn10
]
, Conjectures 1.3 and 1.4) extended to positive-slope families
1
1

1

Greenberg’s original conjecture concerns slope-zero (Hida) families.
predicts that

(5.40)

\hbox{for all but finitely many $x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)$, we have }\,\,{\rm ord}_{s=\frac{w(x)}{2}}L(f_{x},s)=\begin{cases}1&\hbox{ if }W(f)=-1\\
0&\hbox{ otherwise\,. }\end{cases}

Remark 5.32
.

A prototypical result towards (
5.40
) is due to Rohrlich
[
Roh84
]
. To be more precise, let us consider the unique CM family
\bf{f}
that admits the slope-zero
p
-stabilization of the newform
f_{E}
associated with a CM elliptic curve
E
as a weight-
2
specialization. Rohrlich’s generic non-vanishing results in op. cit. (both when
W(f_{E})=1
and
W(f_{E})=-1
) imply a variant of (
5.40
) for the CM family
f
.

In view of the control theorems for the Selmer groups
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
(cf. the exact sequence (
5.41
) below) together with Bloch–Kato conjectures concerning the
L
-values in (
5.40
), we propose the following algebraic variant of this conjecture:

Conjecture 5.33
.

Assuming that
\mathcal{X}
is sufficiently small, we have

{\rm rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})={\rm rank}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})=\begin{cases}1&\hbox{ if }W(f)=-1\\
0&\hbox{ otherwise\,. }\end{cases}

In the next lemma, we give a sufficient condition for the validity of Conjecture
5.33
in the scenario when
W(f)=-1
. See also Remark
5.35
and §
5.3.7
below for further criteria.

Lemma 5.34
.

Let us denote by
{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
the image of the big Beilinson–Kato element

\mathbf{z}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in H^{1}(V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\Lambda^{\iota}(1))

over
\mathcal{X}
(cf. §
2.4.3
) under the map induced from the natural projection
V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\Lambda^{\iota}(1)\to{}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
. Suppose that the global root number of some (every) classical member of the family
\mathcal{X}
equals to
-1
and that
{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\neq 0
. Then Conjecture
5.33
is true.

Proof.

This is very similar to the proof of Theorem
5.22
(i). By the assumptions of this lemma, we can find infinitely many
x_{0}\neq x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
such that the specialization
{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(x,\xi)\in H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime})
of the element
{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)
is nonzero. This, and the fact that
L(f_{x},\frac{w(x)}{2})=0
together with the standard Euler system argument (along with the fact that
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}_{x})
coincides with the Bloch–Kato Selmer group for every classical point
x\neq x_{0}
) show that

\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}^{\prime})=1\,.

Since
{}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime}\simeq{}^{\rm c}V_{x}
as
G_{\mathbb{Q}}
-representations and
{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}^{\prime}\simeq{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}
, this also shows that
\dim_{E}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x})=1
.

The control theorems for Selmer complexes tell us that the sequence

(5.41)

0\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})/\mathfrak{m}_{x}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{x}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{x}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})[\mathfrak{m}_{x}]\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Since
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is a finitely generated
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module,
\mathbf{R}^{2}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})[\mathfrak{m}_{x}]=0
for all but finitely many
x
. This, together with the short exact sequence above, shows that
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})/\mathfrak{m}_{x}\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is a one-dimensional
E
-vector space for infinitely many
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
, which in turn implies (recalling that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a PID and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is a torsion-free
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module by Lemma
5.18
) that the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
-module
\mathbf{R}^{1}\boldsymbol{\Gamma}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})
is free of rank one.
∎

5.3.7.

We conclude our paper with further criteria for the validity of Conjecture
5.33
. We will only study the portion of the conjecture that concerns the Galois representation
V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
, as the complementary statements that concern
V_{\mathcal{X}}

Henceforth, we assume that
\mathcal{X}
is small enough to ensure that the conclusion of Lemma

the specialization of

to
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\rm cl}(E)
. The following conditions will be relevant to our study. One expects them to hold true whenever
W(f)=-1
.
We consider the following variant of the condition (
S1

)

).

Lemma 5.37
.

Suppose that (
GP)
) holds. Then the condition (
S2.1)
) is equivalent to the requirement that

(5.43)

X\cdot{}^{c}\mathbf{z}_{0}(\mathcal{X},\xi)\in\ker\left(H^{1}_{S}({}^{\rm c}V_{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r_{p}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{c}\mathbb{D}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{X}})\right).

Proof.

It follows from the definition of

and its interpolation properties (cf. Eqn. (
2.46
)) that the condition (

) is equivalent to the requirement that

where
r_{p}
is the obvious map (cf. the proof of Theorem
5.22
(ii)), and

•

\mathrm{EXP}_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{{\rm id}\,\otimes\,\mathrm{EXP}_{{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{W}}}H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},{}^{\rm c}\mathbf{D}_{\mathcal{X}})
with

as in
5.24
,

•

the pairing

) is in turn equivalent to the condition that

\textstyle{r_{\rm an}(k/2)=1\quad+\quad\eqref{item_PR1}\quad+\quad\eqref{item_C4}\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

), it therefore remains to explain that the property (
